## QUEUED

### B-002 — Resolve ADR-019 vs. ADR-010 conflict: episode content in the SaaS API — **RESOLVED, fully closed 2026-07-22**
**Objective:** `eami-api`'s memory endpoints stop violating (or are explicitly granted an exception to) the data-sovereignty rule in ADR-010.
**Resolution (2026-07-21):** full episode content stays on-prem; `eami-api` never serves it directly. Implementation split into 3 briefs — all done, all merged.
- [x] **Brief 1 — DONE, merged to master** (`b-002-gateway-episode-endpoint`, merge commit `3eab113`): `eami-gateway` gets a new dual-auth read endpoint (`GET /v1/gateway/episodes`, `/search`, `/{id}`) serving full episode content from its own on-prem Postgres. Dedicated secret (`GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY`), full unit test coverage including the security-critical forged-org_id and cross-org-404 cases. Reviewer + security subagent passes: clean. **Verified 2026-07-22 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...` and `go test ./... -v` both clean, 0 failures (18/18 new tests).**
- [x] **Brief 2 — DONE, merged to master** (`b-002-eami-api-proxy-layer`, merge commit `adcd3e9`): `eami-api` proxy layer (`internal/api/gateway_episodes.go`) forwarding UI requests to Brief 1's endpoint. The hard requirement is satisfied: `org_id` sent to the gateway is always the authenticated caller's own session org (`claimsFromContext(r).OrgID`), never client input — an optional `org_id` query param is accepted only as a tamper-check that 403s on mismatch before the gateway is ever called (structurally impossible for a forged org to reach the gateway, not just checked-and-rejected). Purely additive: `memory.go` had zero lines changed at this point. 11 tests including the centerpiece `TestGatewayEpisodes_List_MismatchedOrgIDSupplied_Returns403_GatewayNeverCalled`. **Verified 2026-07-22: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures.** Reviewer + security subagent passes: clean.
- [x] **Brief 3 — DONE, merged to master** (`b-002-memory-cutover`, merge commit `292d6a4`): `eami-api/internal/api/memory.go` **deleted entirely**, along with `eami-api/internal/store/episodes.go` (the direct, unprotected `episodes` table query — confirmed zero other callers before removal). `/v1/memory/episodes` and `/v1/memory/episodes/search` (the actual frontend-facing, `api/openapi.yaml`-documented routes `MemoryPage.tsx` calls) now point at Brief 2's already-secure handlers directly — same functions, new mount, zero duplicated logic. Added `GET /v1/memory/episodes/{episodeId}`, documented in `openapi.yaml` but never implemented before now. `MemoryPage.tsx` needed **zero changes** — response shapes verified byte-identical to the old ones. Security review: **leak confirmed fully closed**, not just a safer alternative added alongside — re-verified the org-isolation chain from `jwtMiddleware` through `checkOrgID` at the new mount points specifically, not assumed to carry over from Brief 2's review. 8 new tests in `memory_test.go`, reusing Brief 2's test fixtures with zero duplication; `gateway_episodes_test.go` itself has zero diff. **Verified 2026-07-22: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures.** Frontend build/lint/typecheck **not run** — Node/npm confirmed genuinely absent from this machine (not just off-PATH like Go was), so `MemoryPage.tsx`'s correctness rests on manual shape-verification, not a compiler/test run. AC #2's "manually verified via docker compose up" **not performed** — no Docker in this environment; flagged before building, not discovered after.
- [x] `DECISIONS.md` ADR-019 updated to Accepted with the resolution (2026-07-22, formalized as a full entry replacing its own Pending row — same number, not renumbered).
**Dependencies:** none — all three briefs done and merged to `master` (`3eab113`, `adcd3e9`, `292d6a4`).
**Severity:** was High (shipped code contradicting an accepted ADR); now resolved — **there is exactly one path to full episode content, and it enforces org isolation.**
**⚠️ B-015 stays open** — Brief 1's gateway endpoint itself still enforces nothing on its own if reached directly, bypassing eami-api. Not related to this closure; separate item.

### B-017 — `EpisodeStep` schema in `api/openapi.yaml` doesn't match real step JSON
**Objective:** Either fix the documented schema or the runtime shape so they agree — currently neither has ever matched the other.
**Context:** Discovered while verifying B-002 Brief 3 (2026-07-22). `openapi.yaml`'s `EpisodeStep` schema documents `step_number`, `tool`, `action`, `reasoning`, `decision`, `token_in`, `token_out`. The real runtime shape (`eami-gateway/internal/episode/recorder.go`'s `Step` struct, what actually gets written to and read from the `episodes.steps` JSONB column) uses `tool_name`, `action`, `params`, `result`, `decision`, `timestamp`. Only `action`/`decision` line up. Predates this branch entirely — same raw-JSONB passthrough existed in the now-deleted `memory.go`/`store/episodes.go` too — not introduced by any B-002 brief, just newly visible now that `GET /v1/memory/episodes/{episodeId}` is the first real, working route where this exact documented schema is checkable against actual output.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Decide which is authoritative (real shape vs. documented shape) — likely the real shape, since it's what's actually recorded and consumed
- [ ] Update `openapi.yaml`'s `EpisodeStep` schema to match (Architect-EAMI's file, not Code's to silently change)
**Dependencies:** none. Owner: Architect-EAMI (per `BOUNDARIES.md`, `openapi.yaml` changes are theirs).

### B-015 — Restrict direct network reachability of eami-gateway's episode endpoint
**Objective:** Ensure `eami-api`'s proxy is the *only* actual caller of `eami-gateway`'s `GET /v1/gateway/episodes*` — not just that it's the only one the app code assumes.
**Context:** Brief 1's security review (2026-07-21) confirmed: `eami-gateway`'s `GET /v1/gateway/episodes*` service-key auth path trusts a client-supplied `org_id` with no independent authorization check — by design, since that check is Brief 2's job. **Brief 2 (merge `adcd3e9`) and Brief 3 (merge `292d6a4`) are both now merged to master** — B-002 itself is closed, and `eami-api`'s proxy correctly enforces org isolation for all traffic that goes through it. What's left, and the reason this item stays open: Brief 1's gateway endpoint itself is unchanged — anyone who holds `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` and can reach `eami-gateway` directly (bypassing `eami-api` entirely, e.g. if both are on the same network segment with no firewalling between them) still gets zero enforcement from the gateway side. This is a network/deployment-topology question, not something either brief's application code can fix.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Merge `b-002-eami-api-proxy-layer` to master (done, `adcd3e9`)
- [ ] Confirm whether any environment reachable from outside the gateway's own trust boundary can hit `eami-gateway`'s episode route directly (not via `eami-api`'s proxy) — e.g. is `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` provisioned anywhere a non-`eami-api` caller could use it?
- [ ] If yes: restrict network reachability so only `eami-api` can reach `eami-gateway`'s episode endpoint (the proxy is the intended sole caller)
- [ ] Close this item once that network assumption is confirmed
**Dependencies:** none remaining from B-002 (fully closed); this is now a standalone network-hardening item.
**Severity:** Medium — `eami-api`'s org-isolation logic is built, merged, and verified; residual risk is purely about network reachability of `eami-gateway`'s endpoint outside the intended `eami-api`-only caller, same class of trust-boundary assumption as the gateway's existing unauthenticated `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` and `GET /healthz` routes.

### Thread B — admin-defined multi-hop AI workflows — **investigated 2026-08-12; Brief 1 (B-058, schema+CRUD foundation) DONE 2026-08-12; Brief 2 (B-059, execution engine + per-hop TOCTOU pinning) DONE 2026-08-12; Brief 3 (B-063, output→input mapping) DONE 2026-08-13; Brief 4 (B-064, extraction expression UI) DONE 2026-08-13; Brief 5 (B-065, Freshservice-styled visual redesign) DONE 2026-08-13 — see the DONE section below for all five; Briefs 6-8+ (durable escalation pause/resume, chain-aware approval UI, audit-log linkage, a real live-sample/variable-picker upgrade to B-064's extraction UI, and any others) remain NOT YET SCOPED, no B-ID assigned for any of them. This is Thread B's own (backend + UI) epic — see the separate "Workflow Canvas" epic immediately below for the drag-and-drop canvas redesign, a distinct epic that presents the SAME Thread B data, not part of Thread B itself.**

### Workflow Canvas — **DISCONTINUED 2026-08-13 (B-069), code removed — kept here as history, not deleted.** Real drag-and-drop canvas UI for the Workflows editor (`@xyflow/react`), investigated 2026-08-13, built across three briefs (B-066 read-only rendering, B-067 draw-time validation, B-068 save-time validation + real persistence — all DONE, entries preserved below). **Why discontinued:** real hands-on browser testing (the first time any human actually clicked through the canvas, rather than relying on this environment's code-inspection/structural-proof verification substitute) found multiple genuinely broken interactions — edge/node keyboard-deletion (root cause found: `onDelete`/`onNodesDelete`/`onEdgesDelete` were never wired to `<ReactFlow>` at all, plus `deleteKeyCode` defaulted to `'Backspace'`-only, missing Windows' separate `Delete` key; fixed but never re-confirmed live before the decision to remove) and step-configuration dropdowns not responding inside the canvas context (root cause never found despite exhaustive tracing against the actual installed `@xyflow/react`/`@xyflow/system` source). Every one of these had been "verified" only via code-inspection/zero-backend-call structural proof in B-066/067/068 — a disclosed gap (no browser automation in this environment) that, on first real contact with an actual browser, turned out to have let real bugs through. Rather than keep debugging an interaction layer with an unknown remaining bug count, the whole epic was removed; B-065's card editor (never broken, never depended on the canvas) is the sole editor again. The "Workflow Canvas ease-of-use principle" in CONTEXT.md is left in place for any future decision to revisit a visual canvas.
**Investigation findings (full writeup in this session's transcript):** `@xyflow/react` recommended directly over the `@workflowbuilder/sdk` wrapper (smaller, more mature, no unwanted Temporal-based reference-backend opinions to work around, since EAMI already has its own real execution engine). **The critical finding:** React Flow does not structurally prevent branching/cycles/disconnected nodes by default — it provides the primitives (`isValidConnection`, documented connection-limit and cycle-prevention patterns) but a real implementation needs BOTH draw-time gating (immediate UX feedback) AND a save-time full-graph validation pass (the actual correctness backstop — draw-time gating alone doesn't catch an edge or node *deletion* leaving a broken graph) before any future editing brief in this epic ships. No "Agent handoff" node type (escalation is a policy outcome today, invisible to workflow definition — representing it risks showing inaccurate information). Variable references (B-063/064) stay inside a node's config panel, not drawn as a second edge type. Zero schema changes needed anywhere in this epic; visual layout is recomputed from `step_order` client-side, never persisted, in the first brief at least.
**Objective (if built):** let an admin visually chain gateway_tools connectors (rest_api and/or ai_provider) so a call's response can route to another hop instead of always returning straight to the calling agent.
**Investigation findings (full writeup in this session's transcript / CONTEXT.md):**
- v1 should be a **linear ordered list of steps only** — branching is unbuildable until connector output is normalized (it isn't: `aiprovider.Response.Body`/`toolrouter` responses are opaque provider-native JSON, explicitly out of scope per `aiprovider/types.go`'s own doc comment), and the policy engine this would route through (`eami-policy/evaluator.go`) is itself a flat first-match list, not a graph.
- New tables needed: `workflows`/`workflow_steps` (steps reference `gateway_tools.id` by UUID FK, matching the `tool_server_ids` precedent from B-044/migration 009) + `workflow_runs`/`workflow_run_steps` for execution state. Neither `episodes` (write-once INSERT per call, no session_id column, confirmed by reading `episode/recorder.go`) nor `approval_requests` (built for exactly one pinned connector per row) provide a usable starting shape for multi-hop state — genuinely new component.
- B-057's TOCTOU connector-pinning pattern (`resolved_tool_id`/`resolved_config_hash`, migration `000005`) is the right pattern to reuse per-hop but needs array cardinality, not the current singular-FK design — a chain has one hold window per escalating hop, each independently vulnerable to the same admin-edits-mid-hold gap B-057 just closed for single calls.
- Escalation mid-chain has no home: `approval.Router.Hold()` blocks the one goroutine holding the original HTTP request (`approval/router.go`) — a real durable pause/resume mechanism for a multi-hop workflow is genuinely novel, no existing async-resume pattern to extend.
- **The one piece with zero precedent anywhere in this codebase:** the output→input mapping between heterogeneous connector responses (hop N's opaque JSON → hop N+1's `Parameters map[string]any`). This is the actual hard problem and the main reason this can't be tightly scoped yet.
- UI: a linear step-list form (mirrors `ToolsPage.tsx`'s existing slide-out drawer + `ActionPathsEditor`'s reorderable-rows pattern) is realistic for v1; a visual node-graph editor would be new frontend infrastructure (no graph-rendering dependency exists in `eami-ui` today) and isn't justified while v1 stays linear.
**Sizing:** honest estimate is 6-8 briefs minimum (schema+CRUD, step-list UI, output-mapping mechanism, execution/state machine, per-hop TOCTOU pinning, durable escalation pause/resume, audit `workflow_run_id`/chain-aware approval UI) — larger than the 5-brief paste-detection epic, since paste-detection had a fully-specified wire format from day one and this doesn't.
**Dependencies:** none blocking further investigation; needs founder scoping decision (which briefs, what order) before any further B-ID beyond B-058 is minted, per this repo's existing B-ID assignment convention. B-058 (below) was explicitly pasted as a task brief by the founder, not self-assigned from this QUEUED entry.

- **B-019** (2026-07-22) — standalone infra fix, not tied to any brief: `docker-compose.yml`'s `eami-ui` service had the wrong build context (`./eami-ui`) for a Dockerfile that copies repo-root `api/openapi.yaml`, breaking `docker compose up --build`. Fixed to `context: .` / `dockerfile: eami-ui/Dockerfile`. Verified with `docker compose build eami-ui`. Incidentally confirms Docker is available on this machine — see `BUILT.md` cross-cutting note (relevant to B-004, still QUEUED, not re-attempted here).
- **B-020** (2026-07-22) — standalone infra fix: `eami-collector` was crash-looping (`exec /app/docker-entrypoint.sh: no such file or directory`) because `docker-entrypoint.sh` had CRLF line endings, breaking shebang resolution. Stripped to LF. Verified: `docker compose build eami-collector` clean, container starts and stays running (not just builds). No Dockerfile change needed.
- **B-022** (2026-07-23) — `POST /v1/gateway/tools` now actually encrypts and persists the `credentials` object it always documented but silently discarded (decoded, never read, never written to `gateway_tools.credentials_encrypted`, 201 returned anyway — a gap found during a full-application audit, not previously tracked under its own B-ID). New `eami-api/internal/toolcreds` package (AES-256-GCM, key from `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`); `CreateTool` fails closed (500, no store call) if credentials are submitted but no key is configured. A security review pass caught a real bypass in an early version of the fix (deciding "were credentials submitted?" via the typed `ToolCredentials` struct meant an unrecognized field name decoded to an all-empty struct and reproduced the original silent-discard bug) — fixed by deciding presence structurally from the raw JSON and encrypting the raw bytes, not a re-marshaled struct; re-verified clean by the same reviewer. A general code-review pass caught `tools.go`'s other three handlers calling `s.queries` with no nil guard (unlike every other handler file in this package) and a non-standard `"config_error"` code; both fixed. 19 new tests (`toolcreds_test.go` + `tools_test.go`, `tools.go` had zero coverage before this). **Verified 2026-07-23 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures.** `TestTool`'s synthetic-connectivity stub is unchanged, explicitly out of scope — see B-023.
- **B-023** (2026-07-24) — `POST /v1/gateway/tools/{toolId}/test` now runs a real connectivity check instead of unconditionally returning a synthetic "connected" result. New `eami-api/internal/api/tool_connectivity.go`: real HTTP GET for `rest_api` (Bearer `api_key`, or Basic using oauth client creds as a best-effort fallback), real `pgx.ConnectConfig` handshake for `database` (proves reachability + auth in one step via SQLSTATE class `28xxx`), and an honest `misconfigured` for `mcp` (a local-subprocess tool type that can't be tested from eami-api's cloud process without a command-injection/RCE surface — a deliberate non-goal, not a gap). Response shape now matches `openapi.yaml`'s long-undocumented `{success, latency_ms, error}` (the old stub returned a different, never-matching shape). **Security-critical addition caught by review, not in the original plan:** since `eami-api` is EAMI's own cloud SaaS process (unlike `eami-gateway`, which is on-prem), an unguarded version would let an org admin/operator use this endpoint as a reachability oracle against EAMI's own cloud network (e.g. `169.254.169.254` metadata endpoints, internal services) — added `safeDialContext` (rejects loopback/link-local/private/RFC1918/ULA targets via `net.IP`'s own classification, resolve-once-then-dial-the-validated-IP to close a DNS-rebinding gap), wired into both the REST and database dial paths; re-verified clean by the security reviewer against the real 169.254.169.254/RFC1918 cases end-to-end. General code review separately caught and fixed: single-address-only dialing (now falls back through all resolved addresses), a per-call `http.Transport` leaking an idle connection + goroutines on every test (fixed with `DisableKeepAlives: true`), and an unbounded `pgx` connection close (fixed with a dedicated 2s close-only context). Credentials are read back only via a new `GetToolForTest` (raw SQL through `s.queries.DB()`, same escape hatch `finops.go` uses — `store/tools.sql.go` untouched) and never reach a log line or the HTTP response; `toolcreds.Decrypt`'s first production caller handles a wrong/rotated key as a clean `misconfigured` result, never a panic. 30 new tests, including direct coverage of the SSRF guard (loopback, AWS/GCP metadata address, RFC1918 x3, IPv6 ULA, and confirming real public IPs are *not* blocked). One deliberate test-only seam, `Server.toolDialOverride`, unexported, assigned only in `_test.go` files (confirmed via grep by the security reviewer) — production always uses the real `safeDialContext`. **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures** (149 total in the module, up from 143 pre-B-023). Frontend follow-up (B-024) done same day.
- **B-024** (2026-07-24) — `ToolsPage.tsx`'s "Test connection" button now reads the real result B-023 returns, instead of only checking whether the HTTP call threw (which B-023 made meaningless — `TestTool` always resolves 200, real result is in the body). `handleTest` reads `result.success`; on failure, `classifyTestError` parses B-023's `"<reason>: <detail>"` string into `auth-failed`/`unreachable`/`misconfigured`/a generic `failed` fallback. New `TEST_STATE_CONFIG` gives each state a distinct badge (amber/red/gray/red) reusing the page's existing `StatusBadge` color language; the full message is surfaced via the button's tooltip. **Verified with a real `tsc && vite build`** via `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .` (Node/npm still absent locally, but this Docker path gives a genuine compiler check — a reusable verification path noted in `BUILT.md` for future eami-ui work) — clean, zero type errors. Live `docker compose up`/browser click-through not performed (disproportionate for a narrow, already-type-checked change); manual verification steps documented instead. Scope confirmed strictly `ToolsPage.tsx` (`git diff --stat`: one file).
- **B-025** (2026-07-24) — closed a real, live authentication bypass: `eami-api/internal/config/config.go` had **no `validate()` at all**, and its `defaults()` hardcoded `ServiceKey: "changeme"` plus a `changeme`-password DSN, so an unset `API_SERVICE_KEY` env var meant `requireServiceKey` accepted a literal `X-Service-Key: changeme` header from any caller. `eami-gateway/internal/config/config.go` had a `validate()` (from B-002 Brief 1) but never checked `API.ServiceKey` (`GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`) — same gap. Both now reject `API_SERVICE_KEY`/`GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD` if empty or a known placeholder (`"changeme"`/`"devpassword"`), via new shared-shape (duplicated across the two modules) helpers `isPlaceholderSecret`/`dsnHasPlaceholderPassword`/`dsnPassword`. `docker-compose.yml`'s `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-devpassword}` (3 places) and `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s bare `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD}` (3 places) both now use compose's `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?...}` required-var syntax. `.env.example`'s `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`/`DATABASE_URL` literal `changeme` values replaced with blank + instructions. A security-review pass caught a real gap in the first version of `dsnHasPlaceholderPassword` (raw substring match, no trimming — a CRLF-corrupted `.env` value or leading whitespace on the DB password would bypass detection); fixed by extracting the DSN's password segment and validating it through the same trimmed/lowercased path as every other secret. A general code-review pass caught that the original tests only exercised `validate()` directly, not `Load()`'s actual env-var wiring (the real attack surface) — added `Load()`-level integration tests to both packages. 18 new tests total (8 `eami-api`, 10 `eami-gateway`). **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures**, plus live-verified against the running local `docker compose` stack: real secrets still start clean, and `API_SERVICE_KEY=changeme`/unset, `GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY=changeme`, and `API_DB_PASSWORD=devpassword` were each individually confirmed to produce the exact clean startup-refusal error via real containers. Out-of-scope findings from review logged as B-028/B-030 below, not fixed here (outside this task's `MAY MODIFY` list).
- **B-027** (2026-07-25) — added panic recovery to eami-api's alerting `Engine.Run` and eami-gateway's approval router, token-usage writer, and episode recorder — the 4 background/ticker goroutines in the stack with no panic protection (unlike HTTP request paths, already covered by chi's Recoverer / stdlib per-connection recovery). New `eami-gateway/internal/safego` package (`Guard`/`GuardErr`, used by 3 of the 4 targets — shared since they're all in the same module) plus a small local `guard` helper in `eami-api/internal/alerting` (the only target in that module). Recovery is per-iteration, not just per-goroutine-start: `alerting.Engine.evaluateRules` recovers each rule's evaluation individually (a panic in one rule still lets the rest of that tick's rules evaluate), `approval.Router.handleNotification` recovers each LISTEN/NOTIFY event individually (a panic resolving one approval doesn't tear down the LISTEN connection), and the token-usage writer / episode recorder — both fire-and-forget goroutines spawned fresh per event rather than persistent loops — are wrapped at their single call sites (`main.go`'s `safeWriteTokenUsage`, `episode.Recorder.Record` itself, covering all 4 of its call sites in `main.go` from one place). Added outer defense-in-depth too: `Engine.safeTick` wraps the whole tick (catches a panic in `ListAllActiveAlertRules`, outside the per-rule loop) and `Router.Run` wraps `listenLoop` via `GuardErr` (converts a panic anywhere in the LISTEN plumbing into an error, reusing the existing reconnect-with-backoff path). Existing non-panic error-handling (every `log.Printf`/`slog.Warn` on an expected failure) is untouched — confirmed via diff (`git diff --stat`: 4 files, 98 insertions/20 deletions, all wrapper-only). Two minimal, precedented test seams added (matching this codebase's existing `toolDialOverride`/`toolStoreOverride` convention): `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `tokenUsageWriteFunc` var and `episode.Recorder`'s new `episodeWriteDB` interface (mirrors `store.go`'s existing `episodeStore`/`pgxEpisodeStore` split) — both let tests inject a deterministic panic without a live Postgres/eami-api. 21 new tests across `safego`, `alerting`, `approval`, `cmd/gateway`, and `episode`, each proving the real production code path survives an injected panic and its next iteration/call still runs normally (not just testing the generic helper in isolation). **Verified 2026-07-25 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean, 0 failures in both modules** (full test suite, not just new packages). General code-review pass: clean — one trivial redundant `rule := rule` loop-variable capture (harmless under Go 1.22+'s per-iteration loop vars) flagged and removed before commit; no goroutine leaks/races/deadlocks, no sensitive data newly reachable via panic-value/stack logging, shutdown/`ctx.Done()` paths in both `Run()` loops unaffected, all test seams confirmed confined to `_test.go` files.
- **B-026** (2026-07-24) — eami-api's RS256 user-session JWT signing key regenerated in memory on every container restart, force-logging-out every user: `internal/config/config.go` had no env-var override for `cfg.Auth.RSAPrivateKeyPath` at all, so the docker-deployed system was always in ephemeral dev-mode regardless of `eami-api.yaml`. Mirrored `eami-gateway/internal/identity`'s `loadOrGenerateKey` pattern: new `auth.loadOrGenerateKey` generates+persists a 2048-bit key on first boot if missing, loads the same key on every subsequent restart, and — deliberately — propagates a real error rather than silently regenerating over a corrupt/unparseable existing file. New `API_JWT_KEY_PATH` env var (mirrors `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH`), new named Docker volume `api_certs` (mirrors `gateway_certs`) in both compose files, new `scripts/setup.sh` `generate_api_keypair()` mirroring the existing gateway equivalent. 5 new tests in `eami-api/internal/auth/auth_test.go` (package had zero before), including a restart-simulation test proving a token issued by one `Service` instance verifies against a second instance built from the same key path. **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build/vet/test ./...` clean, 0 failures** (both `eami-api` and `eami-gateway` modules). Reviewer + security subagent passes both clean (key-material handling: confirmed no key bytes ever reach a log/error line, confirmed atomic `0600`/`0700` permissions, confirmed corrupt-file/permission-denied errors aren't misclassified as "missing"). **Live-verified beyond the brief's minimum:** logged in against the real local stack, confirmed a token survives not just `docker compose restart eami-api` but a full `down`(no `-v`)/`up` cycle too. Discovered but not fixed (dead code, out of scope): `API_JWT_SECRET` is generated by `setup.sh` and documented in `.env.example` but never read by any Go code — the service is 100% RS256, no HS256 fallback exists.
- **B-028 / B-030 / B-031** (2026-07-25) — config-hygiene cleanup, three small doc/script fixes discovered during the B-025/026/027 security work, none of them live vulnerabilities. **B-028:** `eami-api.yaml`/`eami-gateway.yaml`'s 4 literal `"changeme"` values (service_key/dsn in each) replaced with `""` plus a comment naming the real env-var override and noting `validate()` (B-025) rejects empty/placeholder — confirmed via `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` that neither service's default invocation actually depends on the YAML's literal values (env vars always override). **B-030:** `scripts/seed-db.sh`/`scripts/create-audit-partition.sh` no longer silently fall back to a `devpassword`-embedded connection string when `DATABASE_URL` is unset — both now `exit 1` with an actionable error first, matching B-025's fail-closed contract; `bash -n` syntax-checked clean. **B-031:** `API_JWT_SECRET` (generated by `setup.sh`, documented as an "HS256 fallback" that was confirmed via grep to not exist in any Go code — service is 100% RS256) removed entirely: `setup.sh`'s `gen_jwt_secret()` function, its generation step, the `write_env` parameter/heredoc line, and the call-site argument (remaining positional params shifted, syntax-checked clean); `.env.example`'s block; one line each from `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml`. Re-grepped the repo after removal — zero remaining references outside historical doc/log prose. **Verified:** no Go files touched (build/test not applicable — confirmed via `git diff --stat`); `docker compose config` run against both compose files with required env vars set — both resolve cleanly (exit 0), and the resolved `eami-api` service block in each confirms `API_JWT_SECRET` is gone with no other environment values disturbed. Docker daemon itself wasn't running in this session, so a live `docker compose up` re-verification wasn't performed — `config`'s clean resolution is the evidence for AC #4, not a substitute for it if a live check is wanted later.
- **B-029** (2026-07-25) — first real Postgres backup/disaster-recovery mechanism: new `eami-backup` sidecar service (`postgres:16-alpine`, real cron via Alpine's busybox `crond` — `pg_cron` investigated first but rejected since it can't shell out to run `pg_dump`) runs scheduled `pg_dump -Fc` backups to a dedicated `postgres_backups` volume (separate from `postgres_data` so a lost data volume doesn't also lose the backups), with retention pruning and non-silent failure logging. New `RECOVERY.md` documents a step-by-step restore procedure. **Real end-to-end test performed** (isolated `docker compose -p` project, no impact to the real dev stack): confirmed cron fires on a real schedule with output reaching `docker logs`, confirmed retention pruning and failure logging both work, then did a full disaster-recovery drill — real test data inserted, backed up, `postgres_data` volume destroyed entirely, fresh `postgres` brought up, and restored, with the exact data verified present afterward. **A real bug was caught by this test, not assumed away:** the naive `pg_restore --clean` fails partway against TimescaleDB hypertables; the documented (and tested-clean) procedure instead does `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE` before a plain `pg_restore`. A reviewer pass caught 4 more real issues (dead `pg_dump` flags, unescaped quote in the cron env file, no `BACKUP_SCHEDULE` validation, orphaned `.tmp` files on a killed run) — all fixed and re-verified live. `scripts/reseed.sql`'s misleading "recovery" framing corrected to point at `RECOVERY.md`. Offsite/cloud storage explicitly flagged as the most important follow-up, not built this round (out of v1 scope per the brief). Full writeup in `BACKLOG.md`'s own B-029 entry above and `BUILT.md`.
- **B-032** (2026-07-25) — paste-detection groundwork: new `paste_events` TimescaleDB hypertable (append-only, real FKs to `orgs`/`endpoints`, no reuse of `discovered_endpoints` — investigated first and confirmed the wrong shape, dedup-by-tuple with an overwritten `last_seen`, can't hold per-event history) plus new `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` batch ingestion endpoint (service-key auth, single multi-row `INSERT ... unnest()` write regardless of batch size — not `reports.go`'s N-round-trips-per-batch pattern, flagged as a gap by a prior investigation this session and deliberately not repeated here). Content-scrub guarantee is structural (fixed request field set, no field can carry raw pasted text) and verified live against a real DB with a smuggled-field injection attempt, not just asserted. New `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` deliberately does NOT reuse the existing `UpsertAgentEndpoint` — that function unconditionally overwrites `hostname`/`agent_version`/`os_info` on every call, which would let a paste event's thin payload silently blank out richer metadata the full `eami-agent` scanner already collected for the same machine; now has direct regression test coverage (added after code review flagged it as the riskiest untested logic). Server-side domain allowlist (new `paste_domains.go`, mirrors `network_activity.KnownAIHosts`'s shape, not importable directly — different Go module). Reporting query performance proven, not assumed: 200,000 synthetic rows seeded, real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` confirms `idx_paste_events_org_domain` index usage with zero sequential scans, 5.3ms execution. Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran (mandatory, new privacy-sensitive ingestion surface); security review clean (no new vulnerabilities); code review caught and this session fixed a missing migration idempotency guard (now matches `005_token_usage.sql`'s `IF NOT EXISTS` convention), the missing clobber-regression test above, and a trivial `gofmt` issue. **Verified 2026-07-25 with a real toolchain, including a real Postgres+TimescaleDB (Docker Desktop was paused at session start, unpaused with explicit user confirmation first):** `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean across all 5 workspace modules, `go test ./...` clean for `eami-api` (9 new tests, all passing) and `eami-gateway` (sanity check, unmodified). `discovered_endpoints`/`reports.go` confirmed completely unaffected, both by inspection (files untouched) and by a live regression test proving the two ingestion paths coexist in the same process/DB with no behavior change. No browser extension exists yet (next brief) — this endpoint currently has no live caller; `api/openapi.yaml` not updated (out of scope, owned by Architect-EAMI — logged as B-033). Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api` section.
- **B-034** (2026-07-26) — native-messaging host for real-time paste-event relay (B0): new `eami-agent --native-messaging-host` mode so a future browser extension (B1, not built yet) can hand off paste events to `eami-agent` in real time via stdin/stdout (browser's standard native messaging protocol, not a network port), forwarded immediately via the existing `collector.Sender.Send()` rather than waiting for the 5-minute poll cycle. Investigation before building found `eami-agent` has **no `org_id` anywhere** (only `agent_id`, defaulting to hostname) — confirming a stated assumption in the brief was wrong, exactly as the brief asked to verify. Combined with `eami-collector`/`eami-api`/B-032's endpoint all frozen for this task, the event lands in the existing agent-report pipeline's raw JSON blob today, not literally in B-032's `paste_events` table yet — completing that wiring is logged as B-035, not silently left undone. **Two real bugs caught by this session's own testing, before any real extension existed to catch them in practice:** (1) the native-messaging manifest originally pointed straight at the real binary, but Chrome/Edge's manifest schema has no way to pass `--native-messaging-host` as an argument, so a real browser would have launched it in ordinary poll-loop mode instead of host mode — fixed with a hard-linked `eami-agent-nmhost` launcher next to the real binary, which `main.go` detects by name; live-verified end-to-end after the fix (real registry+manifest+launcher, invoked with no explicit flag, real message parsed and forwarded to a mock collector). (2) logging in native-messaging-host mode wrote to `os.Stdout` — the same stream the protocol uses for length-prefixed framing — corrupting the session on any log line; fixed by routing all logging to `os.Stderr` in this mode specifically, with a new regression test locking it in. **Security review finding, fixed:** no caller authentication meant any local process could invoke this mode directly and forward fabricated data using the agent's own collector credentials (a confused-deputy problem) — fixed with `internal/nmlauncher`'s parent-process verification (fails closed if the parent isn't a recognized browser, fails open with a warning if it can't be determined at all, `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` operator override) — explicitly not bulletproof, but real defense-in-depth. Code review also caught a missing stack trace in the panic-recovery log line (B-027 convention) and a Linux/macOS asymmetry (manifest/launcher never cleaned up on uninstall, unlike Windows) — both fixed. **Verified 2026-07-26 with a real toolchain:** `go build`/`go vet` clean on native Windows and cross-compiled for `linux/amd64`/`darwin/arm64`; `go test ./...` clean, all packages (21 new tests). Windows registration/uninstall live-verified against this machine's real registry; Linux/macOS installer script changes and their `/proc`/`ps`-based parent-process lookups are implemented but not exercised on a live host this session. WiX `CustomAction` wiring written as correct source but not compiled/run via a live `msiexec` install (no .NET SDK here) — the underlying Go logic it invokes was verified directly. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-agent` section.
- **B-034 CI-failure correction** (2026-07-26) — the "MSI — eami-agent installer" CI job failed on B-034's own commit at the "Build MSI" step, exactly in the `Product.wxs` comment block this task added: `WIX0104: An XML comment cannot contain '--'` (the session's own "--" em-dash writing convention, used throughout that comment block, is literally invalid inside XML comments). Rather than guess-fixing and re-flagging "untested" again, **installed a real local .NET SDK + WiX 4.0.6 (matching CI's exact version) via `winget`/`dotnet tool install`** — this machine previously had only the .NET runtime, not the SDK. Reproduced the exact CI error locally byte-for-byte, fixed the `--` sequences, then hit a **second, different real compiler error** on the next attempt (`WIX0400: The Custom element contains illegal inner text`) — the `<InstallExecuteSequence>` block's condition was written as WiX v3-style inner text (`<Custom ...>NOT Installed</Custom>`), but WiX v4 requires it as a `Condition="..."` attribute instead; confirmed authoritatively by downloading and reading WiX's own compiler source (`ParseSequenceElement` in `wixtoolset/wix`'s `Compiler_Package.cs`, which explicitly calls `InnerTextDisallowed(node, "Condition")`), not guessed from memory or a summarized doc page. Fixed both `<Custom>` elements to use the attribute form. **Verified with the real toolchain end-to-end, matching CI exactly:** ran `wix build` directly (exit 0, valid MSI produced) and separately ran the project's actual `installer/build.ps1` script with a real cross-compiled Windows binary (same command CI's workflow invokes), producing a genuine 2.5MB installable MSI with WiX 4.0.6 — the same version pinned in CI. No Go code affected; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` re-confirmed clean regardless. This closes the "not compiled/run through a live wix build" gap the original B-034 entry explicitly flagged as unverified.
- **B-036** (2026-08-03) — B1: Manifest V3 browser extension (`eami-browser-extension/`) completing the paste-detection epic's browser side (B-032 backend, B-034/B0 native-messaging host, this task). Detects paste events on 6 AI-tool domains, computes length + SHA-256 hash client-side, relays to B0 via native messaging — never the raw pasted text, never a direct network call. Extension ID (`ngmdfnecljeoleiancdedbmhjdihaoaa`) is deterministic (RSA public key embedded in `manifest.json`'s `key` field, Chrome's own documented derivation algorithm), computed two independent ways that agreed exactly, and now replaces the placeholder in B0's `nmregister_windows.go`/Linux/macOS manifests. Bundled domain allowlist (not fetched — no backend endpoint exists, matches this codebase's established convention). **The native-messaging→backend leg is fully live-verified**: a real message was sent through the real registered B0 host (invoked with no `--config` flag, relying entirely on the registry-based config fallback per ADR-014, exactly as a real MSI install would populate it) into a real Docker Postgres/collector/API stack, confirmed landing in `endpoint_reports`. **The in-browser leg could not be automated** — no browser-automation tool was available, and a `chromedp`-based attempt was abandoned after this session's elevated shell caused Edge/Chrome to de-elevate-relaunch in a way that broke reliable automation; while investigating, a blanket process-kill very likely terminated the user's own real Edge session (disclosed directly, not glossed over). Pivoted to careful line-by-line code review against Chrome's documented MV3 contracts, real (pure-Go, zero-browser-risk) JS syntax validation, and a `MANUAL_TESTING.md` checklist for the parts only a real browser click-through can verify. **Security review:** no-raw-content guarantee holds in the shipped code (traced, not assumed); one LOW note (a compromised/XSS'd allowlisted page could forge synthetic paste telemetry — requires the attacker to already control JS execution on a trusted third-party domain, not fixed, judged disproportionate). **Code review caught and this session fixed a real bug:** `background.js`'s original storage read-modify-write had a genuine lost-update race under concurrent paste events, contradicting its own "never lose a buffered event" goal — fixed with a promise-chain serialization queue. Also confirmed (not assumed): JS `toISOString()`'s millisecond fraction is correctly accepted by Go's `time.Parse(time.RFC3339, ...)`, and the batch-ack FIFO-ordering assumption is justified by B0's actual single-goroutine `RunHost` implementation. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s new `eami-browser-extension` section. **DONE and fully manually verified (2026-08-03):** all 5 steps of `MANUAL_TESTING.md` passed live, run to completion by the user personally on a real Chrome/Edge install — extension ID, scoped permissions, paste capture with no raw content, non-allowlisted-domain silence, service-worker-restart durability, and `flush()` sending with the event confirmed landing in `endpoint_reports`. Two real bugs were found and fixed along the way, neither catchable without a genuine browser + genuine OS-level native-messaging registration: (1) `background.js`'s `sendBatch` silently discarded `chrome.runtime.lastError` on a failed native-messaging connection, making a failed flush indistinguishable from a clean no-op — fixed by logging it via `console.warn` (retry semantics unchanged, visibility only). (2) `eami-agent`'s `internal/nmlauncher` parent-process check (B-034) refused to start the host because its hardcoded Chrome/Edge allowlist didn't recognize the user's real browser invocation — fixed by changing that branch from fail-closed to fail-open-with-a-warning, tracked for a real fix under **B-037** before customer ship. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-browser-extension` and `eami-agent` sections. Neither fix was filed as its own B-ID — both were bugs found during B-036's own acceptance testing, not new scoped work.
- **B-038** (2026-08-03) — B2: read-only admin UI over B-032's `paste_events` (`eami-ui`'s new `PasteEventsPage.tsx`, `/paste-events`, under the `Operations` sidebar group), plus two new `eami-api` GET routes. **Placement decided against Discover**: paste events are an org-wide time-series event log (filter bar + paginated table + `time_bucket` aggregation), structurally identical to Audit/FinOps, not to Discover's per-endpoint asset-inventory shape — new sidebar item, not nested under Discover. **Data source decided as `paste_events` directly, not the interim `endpoint_reports` blob**: confirmed live via direct query that the real `Dev Org` has zero rows in `paste_events` today (the 800,000 rows there all belong to B-032's own disposable `paste-events-perf-*` test orgs) and that the only real captured events (from B1's manual test pass) sit only in `endpoint_reports`' JSON blob — building against `paste_events` is still the correct call (indexed, purpose-built, zero rework once B-035 lands) but means this page shows an honest empty state for real orgs until B-035 ships; flagged plainly rather than reading the blob instead. **Backend:** `store.ListPasteEvents`/`CountPasteEvents` (mirrors `ListAudit`'s optional-filter/pagination pattern exactly, reuses `idx_paste_events_org`/`idx_paste_events_org_domain`) and `Server.PasteEventsTimeSeries` (raw `time_bucket()` aggregation grouped by `(bucket, domain)` directly in the handler, mirroring `FinOpsTimeSeries`'s exact structure — but returning real per-domain-per-bucket counts, not FinOps's proportionally-distributed approximation). Both new routes mounted in the existing admin/operator/viewer read group next to `/v1/audit`. `api/openapi.yaml` deliberately not touched (owned by Architect-EAMI) — widened B-033 to cover the gap; frontend uses the `apiFetch` escape hatch (same as `MemoryPage.tsx` did pre-B-002-Brief-3) until it's documented. **Tests:** 6 fast integration tests (domain/time filtering, pagination, org isolation, response-shape AC4 check, timeseries grouping, timeseries validation) plus 2 real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` tests reusing B-032's own 100,000-row seeded-volume convention — both confirm the exact queries these handlers issue use `idx_paste_events_org_domain`/`idx_paste_events_org`, zero sequential scans. **Verified 2026-08-03 with a real toolchain:** `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api` (including the full existing suite, not just new tests); frontend verified via a real `tsc && vite build` (Docker builder-stage trick from B-024, Node/npm still absent locally) — clean, zero type errors — and the running dev container served the new page/route with no compile error logged. No live interactive browser click-through performed (same standing environment limitation as B1 — no safe browser automation available here); manual verification steps provided instead. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api`/`eami-ui` sections. **B-035 queued as the explicit next task** (see its entry above, priority raised to High) — it's now the only thing standing between this shipped, tested UI and showing real data for a real org.

- **B-047** (2026-08-06) — security dependency updates + container hardening: 5 confirmed-reachable Go CVEs fixed (pgx→v5.9.2, golang-jwt/jwt→v5.2.2, x/text→v0.39.0 in both `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`, chi→v5.3.0, x/sys→v0.44.0 in `eami-agent`), chi's deprecated `RealIP` replaced with `ClientIPFromRemoteAddr` (verified functionally inert today, closes a header-spoofing-class deprecation), `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` containers now run as non-root (UID 65532), all 4 service Dockerfiles pinned by digest, `eami-ui`'s false nginx-non-root comment corrected, `postcss` patched. A real HIGH-severity gap (`eami-gateway`'s `/var/lib/eami-gateway` JWT-key fallback path left root-owned, unreachable via either compose file today but a latent landmine for any other deployment method) was caught by the mandatory code-review pass and fixed before commit, live-verified against the exact failure scenario. `react-router-dom`/`vite` CVEs deliberately left unfixed (no non-major-version patch exists for either — logged as B-048/B-049); `x/crypto`'s 17 advisories logged as B-050 (confirmed unreachable via `govulncheck`). **CI status: not yet confirmed green** — pushed (`bb2663e`), `Docker — eami-gateway`/`Docker — eami-ui` both passed clean in real CI, but `Go — eami-agent (darwin/arm64)` hit a confirmed platform-wide GitHub Actions outage (started before this push, per githubstatus.com) at the action-resolution step, before any of this task's code ran — ruled out as a regression by comparing against the identical job succeeding on the immediately prior commit. Recheck once GitHub confirms the incident resolved. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `Cross-cutting / shared` section.

- **B-051** (2026-08-07) — real database migration runner, closing the appliance investigation's C7 finding that no mechanism existed to apply a schema change to an already-running, already-seeded database (`schema.sql` only ever ran once, on a genuinely fresh Postgres data directory). Chose golang-migrate via its official `migrate/migrate` Docker image (digest-pinned) over embedding it as a Go library — zero new dependency in any of the 5 existing service `go.mod` files, matches this repo's established pattern of using official upstream images directly. New `schema/migrations-v2/000001_baseline.up.sql` — the verified-accurate cumulative content of `schema.sql`, hand-rewritten with idempotency guards throughout (`IF NOT EXISTS`, `DROP TRIGGER/POLICY IF EXISTS` + recreate, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) — a code-review pass mechanically diffed it against `schema.sql` and confirmed zero content discrepancies. `000002_add_orgs_table_comment` — a deliberately trivial, harmless (`COMMENT ON TABLE`, pure metadata) migration proving the mechanism end-to-end without an actual schema change, per this brief's own scope boundary. The old `schema/migrations/001-010` marked historical/non-runnable via new `schema/migrations/README.md` (audited: only 3 of 10 were actually idempotency-guarded, contradicting the task brief's own assumption about which ones were safe). New `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` `migrate` service runs once before `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` are allowed to start (`depends_on: service_completed_successfully`); the old `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` schema.sql mount removed entirely — one mechanism for fresh installs and upgrades, not two. New `scripts/migrate.sh` wrapper for manual/update-time runs. **A real MEDIUM security finding, found and fixed before commit:** `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` interpolated unescaped into a `postgres://` URL can misparse or leak on certain golang-migrate versions if it contains base64-reserved characters (`+`/`/`/`=`) — confirmed live-reproducible, since this session's own dev password already contained a `+`. Fixed at the root (`.env.example` now generates hex-only passwords, matching `setup.sh`'s own already-safe convention) plus defense-in-depth (`scripts/migrate.sh` now URL-encodes); the automatic-at-boot compose-service path left as a documented residual (same risk class already pre-existing, unfixed, in `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`'s own DSN construction — out of this brief's scope to fix). New standalone Go module `schema/migrationtest/` (deliberately **not** added to `go.work` — doing so broke `eami-gateway`'s Docker build, since that Dockerfile copies each workspace member's `go.mod` individually and `go.work` requires every listed member to exist on disk; run via `GOWORK=off`) — 4 real-Postgres integration tests, each creating/dropping its own throwaway database (the `eami_app` role has `CREATEDB`, confirmed not assumed), proving fresh-install, already-seeded-data (no data loss), double-apply (no duplication), and fresh-vs-incremental-path schema equality. **Live-verified beyond the automated tests**: this session's real dev database (already-seeded, real `dev@example.com` user) was stamped at baseline version 1 and then had migration 2 applied for real — data survived, confirmed via direct SQL; a fully isolated fresh `docker compose` project confirmed via real container state transitions that `migrate` completes and exits 0 strictly before `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` begin starting. Reviewer + security passes both ran for real; code review's other 2 findings (stale `x/text` CVE in the new test module's `go.mod`, zero CI wiring for the new test module) both addressed — the CVE bumped immediately, the CI gap logged as B-052 rather than papered over (it's actually a repo-wide gap, not specific to this module — no Go module's tests run in CI at all today). Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `Cross-cutting / shared` section.

- **B-052 / B-016** (2026-08-08) — `go test` (+`build`/`vet`) now runs for real in CI, for the first time ever, across all 6 Go modules, gated on a real Postgres. New `.github/workflows/build.yml` `test` job (matrix over the 5 `go.work` modules, each with its own `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` service container — the stock `postgres` image lacks the pgvector/TimescaleDB extensions the schema requires — with `schema/migrations-v2` applied via the pinned `golang-migrate` CLI before tests run) and a separate `test-migrationtest` job (`GOWORK=off`, no pre-migration needed since it manages its own throwaway per-test databases). `build-images` gained `needs: [test, test-migrationtest]` — a deliberate, user-confirmed choice to gate image pushes on tests passing (not run in parallel), since B-051's update mechanism now assumes published images are trustworthy. **The gate caught a real, previously-undetected bug on its very first real run**, closing B-016 (queued since 2026-07-22): `FinOpsSummary`/`FinOpsTimeSeries` called `s.queries.DB()` with no nil-guard, unlike every other handler's `"if s.queries != nil"` convention — a request that passes validation against a nil-`s.queries` test server nil-pointer-panicked, silently recovered into an opaque 500 by chi's `Recoverer` and masked by weak `!= 400` test assertions. Fixed with a minimal guard clause (`if s.queries == nil { writeError(500, ...); return }`), deliberately not a mock-store fix (would risk masking other real gaps behind fabricated responses), plus 2 new tests asserting the exact clean 500. **A real process failure caught along the way, self-corrected before being trusted**: an initial "verified locally" pass had actually been a false negative — Go's test-result cache doesn't invalidate on env-var changes without `-count=1`, so a stale pre-fix cached result was silently reused; re-run with `-count=1` reproduced the real panic with a full stack trace, and CI's own `go test` steps were subsequently given `-count=1` too, closing the same risk inside the gate itself. **Mandatory code-review pass found the identical unguarded pattern's twin, not caught by the original brief**: `paste_events.go:314`'s `PasteEventsTimeSeries` had the same bug, never exercised because its tests always require a real Postgres connection or skip cleanly (unlike `finops_test.go`'s deliberately-nil test server) — fixed identically, with a new no-Postgres-required proving test. AC1 (the CI gate actually blocks on failure) proven twice: organically, when the real B-016 bug failed `Test — eami-api` and `Docker — ${{ matrix.service }}` was correctly skipped; and deliberately, via a one-line assertion flip in `eami-policy/policy_test.go`, pushed, confirmed red + build-images skipped, then reverted and confirmed green again. CI duration: ~1m40s baseline → ~2m54s–3m12s post-change (real increase from sequential gating, judged acceptable given pushes only happen on main/tags). Security review: no findings (ephemeral CI-only Postgres password, pinned `golang-migrate` version, purely defensive nil-guards). Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `Cross-cutting / shared` section.

- **B-053** (2026-08-09) — VM appliance base image + first-boot detection (Model A, ADR-020). New `appliance/` directory: Packer template (`qemu` builder, Debian 12 generic cloud qcow2, NoCloud cloud-init seed for build-time SSH only), `provision-base.sh` (installs Docker, wires in the two systemd units, locks every account's password, disables `ssh.service`/cloud-init before capture), `eami-data-disk.sh` (idempotently partitions/formats/mounts a second virtio disk as `/data`, points both Docker's `data-root` **and** containerd's separate `root` there), `eami-stack.sh` (generates `/data/eami/.env` once if missing, runs `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file ... up -d`, writes `/data/eami/state=unconfigured` once — deliberately never runs `setup.sh`'s interactive org/admin seeding, so AC3's empty-`orgs`-table state holds by construction). **Real bugs found and fixed via actual QEMU/KVM boot testing, not just code review:** a systemd unit-ordering race where `DefaultDependencies=no` made `eami-data-disk.service` start concurrently with `systemd-udevd` itself, missing `/dev/vdb` (fixed with proper `After=`/`Requires=` plus `ExecStartPre=udevadm settle`); `parted` not installed on the base image; containerd's own persistent root staying on the OS disk while Docker's was correctly redirected — an OS-image update reset containerd's state while Docker's persisted metadata still referenced old containerd snapshots, producing `"RWLayer of container ... is unexpectedly nil"` on already-created containers (fixed by redirecting containerd's `root` to `/data/containerd` too); a `mount -a`-vs-systemd's-own-generated-`data.mount`-unit race on any boot after the first. **Two mandatory review passes both ran and found real issues, applied before shipping:** a code-review pass found the build-time SSH backdoor wasn't actually closed (locked password ≠ disabled key auth) and, on a follow-up pass, that removing Packer's `shutdown_command` (done to work around `sudo` breaking once the `packer` build account was fully deleted) meant Packer's QEMU builder force-kills the VM immediately rather than waiting for a graceful shutdown — risking a corrupted/inconsistent captured disk on every single build. Resolved with a considered trade-off: keep `shutdown_command` (needs working `sudo -n`) and don't fully delete the `packer` account, but strip its `authorized_keys` and lock its password so it's structurally unreachable (no valid credential, `ssh.service` disabled) — a documented, accepted residual over a build-corruption risk. A security review (manual, run inline) found no other issues; ephemeral build-time-only credentials, no secrets logged, `openssl rand -hex 32` for all generated secrets, `.env` written with `umask 077` (no world-readable window). **Emergency access:** SSH fully disabled by default; documented recovery path is hypervisor/physical console access via GRUB (unprotected bootloader, standard break-glass model for hardened appliances) — a console-triggered temporary-SSH-enable script was considered and explicitly declined (founder decision, smallest attack surface). **Verified via real QEMU/KVM boot testing** (this machine has genuine KVM acceleration inside WSL2, confirmed via `kvm-ok` and a live SeaBIOS boot before relying on it): AC1 (stack comes up automatically, health endpoints responding), AC2 (indirect via `depends_on: migrate: service_completed_successfully` — `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` only start after migrate succeeds), AC3 (starts healthy with zero orgs, by construction), AC4 (`/data/eami/state` confirmed `unconfigured` via direct disk inspection), AC5 (SSH disabled confirmed via `/etc/shadow` showing locked passwords and absence from `multi-user.target.wants`) all directly confirmed, repeatedly, across multiple clean rebuild-and-reboot cycles. **AC6 (data survives an OS-image rebuild): the core requirement — rebuild the OS disk, reboot with the existing data disk, stack comes back up healthy — was verified successfully** (a `{"status":"ok"}`/`"ok"` health-check pass following a real OS rebuild+reboot, both before and after the shutdown_command fix). **One thing NOT claimed as verified, disclosed honestly rather than glossed over:** a stricter secondary check (byte-for-byte comparing `/data/eami/.env`'s generated secrets before vs. after an OS rebuild, via host-side `debugfs` disk inspection) could not be completed reliably — repeated attempts hit nested-virtualization instability in this environment (QEMU-inside-WSL2, itself running on a real but resource-constrained host), including several confirmed self-inflicted tooling bugs along the way (a `pgrep -f` self-matching its own command line causing a 20+-minute false-stuck wait; `pgrep -x` silently never matching a 19-character process name; raw disk-extraction races from impatient `kill -9`s corrupting host-side qcow2 copies). This specific check is not claimed as verified; the core AC6 requirement is. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s new `appliance` section and `appliance/README.md`.

## QUEUED (added this session)

### EPIC — Approval routing, auto-rules, and in-session confirmation — **identified, not investigated, no B-ID assigned**
**Objective:** three related but distinct capabilities around how escalations reach and get resolved by a human, surfaced during B-047's own manual Approvals-page testing (2026-08-06/07), not scoped or sized yet.
**Status:** identified only. Needs the same investigate-first treatment as every other epic before any brief gets written — do NOT jump straight to implementation. Before scoping size/priority, confirm: (1) whether anything resembling tool ownership already exists (repo-wide grep, not assumed); (2) what `policy_conditions`' current schema could realistically support for auto-resolve rules without a redesign; (3) whether any existing channel already runs from an AI session back to its originating human user, or whether #3 needs entirely new infrastructure (this one is architecturally novel — treat the answer as genuinely unknown, not "probably no").
**Three capabilities:**
1. **Approval routing by tool ownership** — tools/policies need an "owner" concept so escalations route to the right person/team's notification channel, not one flat global admin queue. Today, any `admin`/`operator` role sees and can act on every pending approval regardless of which tool/business app it relates to — not viable for a real multi-app enterprise deployment with multiple tool owners.
2. **Auto-approve/auto-deny rules** — conditions under which an escalation resolves automatically without a human click (e.g. low-risk actions, trusted agents, time-of-day, spend thresholds). A policy-engine extension, not just an Approvals-UI change.
3. **In-session user confirmation** — a mechanism to prompt the human actually driving the AI conversation directly (not an admin) for certain escalations, in the moment. Nothing in the current architecture does this today, as far as is known — needs real investigation, not assumption.
**Acceptance criteria (investigation phase only):**
- [ ] Confirm/refute whether any tool-ownership concept exists anywhere in the schema or code today
- [ ] Document what `policy_conditions`/the policy engine would need to change to support #2
- [ ] Document what would need to exist (new infra, not just UI) to support #3, and whether it's even in scope for an on-prem gateway's trust model
- [ ] Only after the above: size and split into individually scoped, numbered briefs
**Dependencies:** none blocking the investigation. Implementation (once scoped) will likely touch `eami-api` (policies/tools/approvals), `eami-gateway` (approval router, policy evaluation), `eami-policy` (rule schema), and `eami-ui` (Approvals/Policies/Tools pages) — cross-cutting, not a single-module task.

### B-021 — Every `.sh` file in the repo has CRLF line endings except the now-fixed collector entrypoint
**Objective:** Prevent the same shebang-resolution crash (B-020) from recurring in any other script, and stop silent `\r`-in-heredoc corruption in scripts that build SQL/config strings.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-22 while fixing B-020. `file` on every `.sh` in the repo (`scripts/setup.sh`, `scripts/seed-db.sh`, `scripts/create-audit-partition.sh`, `scripts/generate-api-client.sh`, `eami-collector/scripts/create_api_key.sh`, all of `eami-agent/installer/{linux,macos}/*.sh`) reports CRLF terminators. No `.gitattributes` exists to pin LF for shell scripts, so a Windows checkout (`core.autocrlf=true` or similar) rewrites them on clone. These haven't crash-looped yet only because they're invoked as `bash script.sh` rather than exec'd via shebang — same landmine as B-020 for anything that changes to direct exec, and CRLF inside heredocs (`setup.sh`'s inline `psql` blocks) is a latent correctness risk even without a crash.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Add `.gitattributes` pinning `*.sh text eol=lf` (and likely `Dockerfile`, `docker-entrypoint.sh` by name)
- [ ] Normalize existing scripts to LF
- [ ] Confirm `scripts/setup.sh`'s inline heredoc SQL still runs clean after normalization (it already worked with CRLF since bash tolerates `\r` mid-heredoc-line in most cases, but worth confirming, not assuming)
**Dependencies:** none. Not fixed as part of B-020 — that task was scoped to the collector only.

### B-028 — `eami-api.yaml`/`eami-gateway.yaml` still ship literal `"changeme"` default values — **DONE, 2026-07-25**
**Objective:** Stop shipping a stale, misleading example config now that B-025's `validate()` rejects these exact values.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-24 during B-025's security review. `eami-api/eami-api.yaml` (`service_key: "changeme"`, `dsn: "...changeme@..."`) and `eami-gateway/eami-gateway.yaml` (`postgres_dsn: "...changeme@..."`, `api.service_key: "changeme"`) are baked into their respective Docker images (`COPY`'d, and each service's default `--config`/config path points at them) and unchanged by B-025. Not a live security bug — B-025's `validate()` correctly rejects these values at startup if no env-var override is present — but the files themselves are now internally inconsistent with `.env.example`'s cleanup and will always fail validation as shipped, which is confusing for anyone using the YAML path directly instead of env vars.
**Resolution:** All 4 literal `"changeme"` values replaced with `""` plus a short comment naming the real env-var override and noting `validate()` rejects empty/placeholder (B-025) — mirrors `config.go`'s own `defaults()` comments ("no default ServiceKey/DSN shipped on purpose"), not a new convention. Confirmed via `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` that neither service's Dockerfile/default invocation depends on the YAML's literal values — `eami-api`'s real DSN/service-key always come from `API_DB_HOST/NAME/USER/PASSWORD`/`API_SERVICE_KEY` env vars (which override the YAML unconditionally in `config.go`'s `Load()`), same for `eami-gateway`'s `GATEWAY_DB_*`/`GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Replace the literal `"changeme"` values in both YAML files with either blank/omitted fields (relying on env-var overrides, consistent with `.env.example`) or a comment-only placeholder that can't be mistaken for a working value
- [x] Confirm neither service's Dockerfile/default invocation relies on the YAML's current literal values being valid
**Dependencies:** none. Not fixed as part of B-025 — those YAML files weren't in that task's `MAY MODIFY` list.

### B-030 — `scripts/seed-db.sh`/`scripts/create-audit-partition.sh` have the same silent-weak-default pattern B-025 closed elsewhere — **DONE, 2026-07-25**
**Objective:** Close the same class of bug B-025 fixed in the Go services and docker-compose, in the remaining shell scripts.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-24 during B-025's security review. Both scripts fall back to `${DATABASE_URL:-postgresql://eami_app:devpassword@localhost:5432/eami}` (or equivalent) if `DATABASE_URL` is unset — the same silently-guessable-default pattern as the now-fixed `POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-devpassword` in `docker-compose.yml`.
**Resolution:** Both scripts now hard-fail with an actionable error ("DATABASE_URL must be set — see .env.example") and `exit 1` if `DATABASE_URL` is unset, before ever building a connection string — same "fail closed, actionably" contract as B-025's Go-side `validate()`. Each script's header comment updated to match (was: "or defaults to local dev" / "can override the default connection string"). `bash -n` syntax-checked clean on both.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Replace the fallback with a hard failure (clear error message) if `DATABASE_URL` is unset, matching B-025's "fail closed, actionably" contract
**Dependencies:** none. Not fixed as part of B-025 — `scripts/setup.sh` was in that task's `MAY READ` list (to confirm it wasn't the source of the defaults, which it wasn't), but `seed-db.sh`/`create-audit-partition.sh` were not in scope at all.

### B-031 — `API_JWT_SECRET` is dead config that implies an HS256 fallback which doesn't exist — **DONE, 2026-07-25**
**Priority:** Low — cosmetic/documentation-accuracy issue, no behavior gap, no security exposure. No urgency.
**Objective:** Stop shipping a config value that silently confuses future readers into thinking there's an HS256 signing path, when the service is 100% RS256.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-24 during B-026 (persistent RSA key for eami-api's JWT signing). `API_JWT_SECRET` is generated by `scripts/setup.sh` (`gen_jwt_secret`), documented in `.env.example` (`"JWT signing secret for user session tokens (HS256 fallback; prefer RS256 in prod)"`), and wired through as an env var in both `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` — but no Go code reads it anywhere. `internal/auth/auth.go`'s `Service` signs and verifies exclusively via `jwt.SigningMethodRS256`/`RSAPrivateKey`; there is no HS256 code path in `config.go` or `auth.go` at all. The `.env.example` comment describes a fallback that was either never built or was removed without updating the docs/generator — either way, it's misleading as written.
**Resolution:** Removed entirely, per this task's stated preference (nothing in `setup.sh`'s flow depended on the variable's value beyond writing it to `.env` — confirmed by tracing `write_env`'s call sites before removing). Deleted: `scripts/setup.sh`'s `gen_jwt_secret()` function, its Step-5 generation call, the `write_env` parameter and the `API_JWT_SECRET=...` heredoc line, and the corresponding call-site argument (remaining positional params renumbered/shifted, `bash -n` syntax-checked clean); `.env.example`'s whole `API_JWT_SECRET` block; `docker-compose.yml` line and `docker-compose.prod.yml` line. Re-grepped the whole repo afterward — zero remaining references outside this session's own doc/log entries (`BUILT.md`/`BACKLOG.md`/`CONTEXT.md`, which are history, not live config).
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Either remove `API_JWT_SECRET` entirely from `scripts/setup.sh`, `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml`, and `docker-compose.prod.yml`, **or** keep it but rewrite the `.env.example` comment (and any inline compose/setup.sh comments) to say plainly that it's unused/reserved, not an active HS256 fallback
- [x] If removing: confirm nothing else references `API_JWT_SECRET` (e.g. no test, no doc outside this cleanup, no downstream tooling) before deleting it from the generator/compose/env files
**Dependencies:** none. Not fixed as part of B-026 — that task's scope was specifically the RSA key persistence path; this is a pre-existing, unrelated dead-config observation surfaced along the way.

### B-029 — No Postgres backup/disaster-recovery mechanism — **DONE, 2026-07-25**
**Priority:** High — for a product whose core value is an immutable audit trail, `docker compose down -v` or disk loss being unrecoverable was a real gap, not cosmetic.
**Objective:** Add a genuine, scheduled backup mechanism with a documented, *tested* restore procedure — not just a backup that's never been proven to restore correctly.
**Context:** No backup mechanism existed at all. `scripts/reseed.sql` (a demo-data convenience script) was headed "Re-seed org and admin user after `docker compose down -v`," which made it easy to mistake for a real recovery tool — it only recreates one org and one user, not any of the data that existed before loss.
**Resolution:** New `eami-backup` service (`postgres:16-alpine` — has both `pg_dump` for PG16 and Alpine's busybox `crond`) added to both `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.prod.yml`, running a real cron schedule (`BACKUP_SCHEDULE`, default daily 03:00 UTC) that invokes new `scripts/backup-db.sh` (POSIX `sh`, since the container has no bash): `pg_dump -Fc` to a temp file, atomic `mv` into place only on success, timestamped log line either way, non-zero exit + `ERROR` log line on failure (never silent), and retention pruning (`BACKUP_RETENTION_COUNT`, default 14 newest kept). New `scripts/backup-entrypoint.sh` sets up the crontab, handles two dockerized-cron gotchas that would otherwise make this silently non-functional (crond doesn't inherit the container's env — captured into a sourceable file instead; crond job output doesn't reach `docker logs` by default — redirected into the container's own PID 1 fds), validates `BACKUP_SCHEDULE` has 5 fields before installing it (falls back to the default + logs an error rather than silently never firing on a typo), and runs one backup immediately at container start so a fresh deployment has a baseline backup within seconds. Backups land on a new `postgres_backups` named volume, kept separate from `postgres_data` specifically so losing/recreating the Postgres data volume doesn't also destroy the backups — and mounted read-only into the `postgres` service itself so restore can reference `/backups/<file>` directly with no manual file-copying. New `RECOVERY.md` documents what's covered (full `pg_dump`, all extensions/data) vs. explicitly not (offsite/cloud storage — flagged as the most important follow-up; PITR/WAL archiving; automated restore — all correctly out of this task's v1 scope) and a step-by-step restore procedure. `scripts/reseed.sql`'s header rewritten to stop implying it's a recovery tool and point at `RECOVERY.md` instead. `.env.example`/`scripts/setup.sh` document the two optional env vars and mention backups in the setup summary — no new required secrets, `start_stack()`'s existing `docker compose up -d` brings the new service up automatically, confirmed forward-compatible with the eventual VM-appliance story with no appliance-specific first-boot logic needed.
**Why not `pg_cron`?** Investigated first, since `postgres` already runs it (for `audit_log` partitioning, migration 007) — rejected because `pg_cron` jobs execute as SQL inside the Postgres backend and can't shell out to run an external client program like `pg_dump` to write a file; that needs a real separate client process, hence the sidecar container with real cron instead. Documented in `RECOVERY.md` so the reasoning isn't lost.
**Real end-to-end test performed (not theoretical) — 2026-07-25, isolated `docker compose -p` project, no impact to the real local dev stack/volumes:** brought up a fresh isolated stack; confirmed the initial on-start backup ran and produced a real file; waited past a real cron tick (schedule temporarily set to `* * * * *`) and confirmed a second backup fired *on schedule* with its output actually reaching `docker compose logs` (proving the PID-1-fd-redirection fix works, not just "should work"); manually triggered extra backups and confirmed retention pruning deletes the correct (oldest) files once the count is exceeded; simulated a backup failure (bad credentials) and confirmed a clear `ERROR` log line, non-zero exit, and no partial file left behind. **Disaster-recovery test:** inserted a real, identifiable test org row, took a backup capturing it, destroyed the `postgres_data` volume entirely (simulating total disk loss — `postgres_backups` survived, since it's a separate volume), brought up a completely fresh `postgres` container, and restored. **First restore attempt used `pg_restore --clean` and failed partway** — TimescaleDB hypertables don't support the `ALTER TABLE ... ONLY DROP CONSTRAINT` statements `--clean` generates against a database where `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/schema.sql` already ran on the fresh container's boot — a real, non-theoretical finding this test caught, not assumed. Fixed by wiping via `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE; CREATE SCHEMA public; GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO eami_app;` before a plain `pg_restore` (no `--clean`) — re-tested clean, **zero errors**, full 73-table schema restored (including all 20 `audit_log` monthly partitions), all 6 extensions (`pgvector`, `TimescaleDB`, `timescaledb_toolkit`, `pgcrypto`, `uuid-ossp`, `plpgsql`), TimescaleDB hypertable metadata, and the exact test org row (same UUID, name, slug, `created_at`) verified present and correct. This tested procedure — not the naive one — is what `RECOVERY.md` documents. All test resources (containers, volumes, images left as reusable cache) torn down after; confirmed zero leftover state via `docker volume ls`/`docker ps -a`.
**Reviewer pass (general-purpose subagent) caught 4 real issues before commit, all fixed and re-verified live:** (1) `pg_dump --no-owner --no-privileges` are no-ops for custom-format (`-Fc`) archives per pg_dump's own docs — only meaningful at `pg_restore` time, which already had them; removed from the dump side as dead/misleading. (2) The generated cron env-sourcing file interpolated `DATABASE_URL` inside single quotes with no escaping — a literal `'` in a hand-typed `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (not reachable via `setup.sh`'s generated alphanumeric-only secrets, but reachable if someone edits `.env` by hand) would corrupt the sourced file; fixed with a proper POSIX single-quote-escape helper, re-tested live with a password containing `'` end-to-end successfully. (3) No validation of `BACKUP_SCHEDULE` before writing it to the crontab; added a 5-field check with a fallback + `ERROR` log line. (4) A `.tmp` file from a hard-killed (SIGKILL/OOM) run was never cleaned up and retention pruning only globbed `*.dump`; added a stale-`.tmp` sweep at the start of each run. A 5th flagged concern — a possible race between `postgres`'s `service_healthy` condition and `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/schema.sql` finishing, which could make the automatic first backup capture an empty/partial schema — was investigated empirically (not just argued about) by tracing the container's real startup log: `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` follows the standard docker-library/postgres entrypoint contract (an internal bootstrap server applies `schema.sql` and fully shuts down *before* the real TCP-listening server — the one the healthcheck targets — ever starts), so the race does not exist for this image; confirmed by checking `information_schema.tables` at the exact moment `pg_isready` first succeeded.
**Not fixed, correctly out of scope (documented in `RECOVERY.md`'s "Future work"):** offsite/cloud backup storage (flagged as the most important follow-up — a lost/compromised host currently loses backups too, not just live data), point-in-time recovery/WAL archiving, automated restore tooling, backup encryption at rest.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-033 — `api/openapi.yaml` has no entry for `POST /v1/reports/paste-events`
**Priority:** Low — the route works correctly without it; this is a contract-documentation gap, not a behavior bug.
**Objective:** Keep `api/openapi.yaml` the authoritative source of truth for every `eami-api` route, matching every other handler in `reports.go` (`POST /v1/reports`, `POST /v1/internal/token-usage`, etc.), which are all documented there.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-25 during B-032's code review. `api/openapi.yaml` is owned by Architect-EAMI per `BOUNDARIES.md` and was not in B-032's `MAY READ`/`MAY MODIFY` scope, so the new route was deliberately left undocumented rather than edited outside the task's file boundary.
