Precedent exists for Code-EAMI editing schema/contract-adjacent files directly in-session (this same session edited `schema.sql`/migrations directly), so this may be assignable back to Code-EAMI rather than strictly requiring Architect-EAMI — founder to confirm. **Widened 2026-08-03 (B-038):** the same gap now also covers two new read routes, `GET /v1/paste-events` and `GET /v1/paste-events/timeseries` — deliberately left undocumented for the same reason (not in B-038's `MAY MODIFY` scope either), and the frontend calls them via `apiFetch`'s documented escape hatch rather than the generated typed client, same workaround `MemoryPage.tsx` already used for `/v1/memory/episodes*` before B-002 Brief 3 documented those.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Add a `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` path entry to `api/openapi.yaml`, request/response schema matching `PasteEventReport`/the `{accepted, rejected}` response shape in `eami-api/internal/api/paste_events.go`
- [ ] Confirm the service-key security scheme reference matches the existing `POST /v1/reports` entry
- [ ] Add `GET /v1/paste-events` and `GET /v1/paste-events/timeseries` path entries too (B-038), matching `PasteEventListResponse`/`PasteEventTimeSeries` in `eami-api/internal/api/types.go`; once documented, switch `usePasteEvents.ts`'s two hooks from `apiFetch` to the generated typed `api.GET`
**Dependencies:** B-032 (done), B-038 (done). Blocked only on confirming ownership/who picks this up.

### B-035 — Wire received paste events through to the real `paste_events` table server-side — **DONE, 2026-08-04**
**Priority:** High — explicitly queued as the next task after B-038 (2026-08-03). B-038 just shipped a full read UI over `paste_events`, but for the real `Dev Org`, that table has **zero rows** right now (confirmed live via direct query during B-038): every real captured paste event from B1's manual testing landed only in `endpoint_reports`' raw JSON blob, the interim path this item exists to retire. Until this ships, B-038's UI is correctly built and fully tested but shows an empty state for any real org — this is the missing link, not a nice-to-have.
**Objective:** Complete the path B-034 deliberately left unfinished: a paste event received by `eami-agent --native-messaging-host` and forwarded via the existing `collector.Sender.Send()` currently lands in `eami-collector`'s buffered agent-report pipeline, ending up in `endpoint_reports`' raw JSON blob column (via `eami-api`'s `processIngestItem`) — not in B-032's dedicated `paste_events` table, which is what the paste-detection epic actually needs for reporting.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-07-26 while building B-034. Both `eami-collector` and `eami-api` (and B-032's `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` specifically) were explicitly frozen/out of scope for B-034's `MAY MODIFY` list, so this wiring was deliberately not attempted rather than done partially or incorrectly. `eami-agent` has no `org_id` (confirmed during B-034's investigation) and must never hold `eami-api`'s service-key, so the eventual fix likely means `eami-collector` extracting a `paste_events` field from the buffered report JSON and forwarding it (with server-side `org_id` resolution, same as the rest of the agent-report pipeline) toward B-032's endpoint or an equivalent internal path — not `eami-agent` talking to `eami-api` directly.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Design and implement the `eami-collector`/`eami-api` server-side path that extracts a paste event forwarded via the existing agent-report pipeline (`outboundReport.PasteEvents` in `eami-agent/internal/nativemsg/nativemsg.go`) and writes it into B-032's `paste_events` table
- [x] Confirm `org_id` resolution follows the same server-side convention already used for the rest of the agent-report pipeline (`GetDefaultOrgID` today, or whatever it becomes for real multi-tenancy)
- [x] No change to the `eami-agent` → `eami-collector` wire format required beyond what B-034 already sends (`agent_id`, `hostname`, `collected_at`, `paste_events`)
- [x] Confirm B-038's `PasteEventsPage.tsx`/`GET /v1/paste-events*` show real data end-to-end once this lands — no UI-side change should be needed, only new rows appearing
**Dependencies:** B-032 (done, provides the target table), B-034 (done, provides the source data shape), B-038 (done, provides the read UI this unblocks). No remaining blockers — B1 (browser extension) is done and producing real traffic, so this can be verified with genuine end-to-end data, not just synthetic.
**Resolution (2026-08-04):** the brief's own assumption ("`eami-collector` already resolves org context") was investigated and found **not accurate** — `eami-collector` resolves no `org_id` anywhere (confirmed by grep); only `eami-api`'s `IngestBatch` does, via the pre-existing `GetDefaultOrgID`. Since `eami-agent`'s native-messaging relay already sends paste events through the existing, unmodified `/v1/ingest`→buffer→forward→`/v1/ingest/batch` pipeline, **the entire fix landed in `eami-api` alone** — zero changes to `eami-agent` or `eami-collector`, a real simplification over the brief's original sketch. `processIngestItem` now detects a paste-event-only item (`rep.PasteEvents != nil`) and routes it to a new `processPasteEventRelayItem`, which resolves the endpoint via B-032's non-clobbering `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` (not `UpsertAgentEndpoint`, which would otherwise blank out a real endpoint's `agent_version`/`os_info` with this item's empty scan fields) and writes via `BatchInsertPasteEvents` — never touching `endpoint_reports` for that item. `org_id` is always the server-resolved value; `agentReport` has no field a client could use to influence it. Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran (mandatory, trust-boundary task): security review found no High/Medium issues, confirmed the design stays safe if `GetDefaultOrgID` is later replaced by real multi-tenant resolution as long as org derivation keeps coming from an authenticated channel; code review caught a real Medium-High bug before ship (the original `len(rep.PasteEvents) > 0` check couldn't distinguish an absent `paste_events` key from an empty-but-present `[]`, which would have fallen through to the clobbering path) — fixed and locked in with a new regression test, plus added length/array-size caps both reviews independently flagged as missing. 6 new integration tests in `internal/api/ingest_paste_relay_test.go`, all posting the exact JSON shape a real B0 relay produces, covering AC1/AC2/AC4/AC5 plus the clobber-prevention centerpiece and the empty-array edge case. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`** (full module, not just new tests) **and `eami-agent`/`eami-collector`/`eami-gateway` all still build clean**, confirming the "zero changes needed" claim rather than just asserting it. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api` section. This was the last open item in the paste-detection epic (B-032→B-034→B-036→B-038→B-035).

### B-037 — `nmlauncher`'s parent-process allowlist needs to be genuinely maintained, not permanently fail-open — **DONE, 2026-08-04**
**Priority:** High before any real customer install — the current fail-open behavior is an explicitly acknowledged, user-accepted dev-testing convenience, not a production security posture.
**Objective:** Make `internal/nmlauncher.VerifyLaunchedByBrowser()`'s "parent determined but not recognized" case reliably distinguish a real, legitimate browser (across actual Chrome/Edge/Chromium-family installation paths, OS versions, and update channels) from an arbitrary local process, so this check can go back to failing closed with confidence — instead of today's fail-open-with-a-warning, which accepts *any* unrecognized parent.
**Context:** B0's original design (B-034, 2026-07-26) shipped this check fail-closed on an unrecognized parent. Real manual testing during B1 (B-036, 2026-08-03) hit a live false positive — a real, legitimate browser on this machine wasn't matched by `knownBrowserExecutables`' short hardcoded list, breaking the primary paste-flush flow entirely. Fixed same-day by flipping that branch to fail-open-with-a-WARN (see `BUILT.md`'s `eami-agent` section, new paragraph under B-034, and `CONTEXT.md`'s 2026-08-03 entry). The user explicitly accepted this as correct **for tonight's testing** but was equally explicit that it is *not* the final security posture: today's fix means the confused-deputy protection this check exists for (any local process could otherwise invoke `--native-messaging-host` mode and forward fabricated data using the agent's own collector credentials) is currently **not enforced** for any parent process not on the hardcoded list — which, per this same investigation, may include real, common browser installs. This must be revisited and tightened before this code path is exposed to a real customer.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Determine why the user's real browser wasn't matched (exact process name/path, browser vendor/version, whether it's a genuine Chromium-family variant missing from `knownBrowserExecutables` or a structural gap in how the immediate-parent lookup itself works on this OS/version) — **not fully confirmed (the original failure's exact parent name was never captured and can't be recovered), but real research ruled out the two most obvious hypotheses** (Windows channel-name incompleteness; a `cmd.exe`-intermediary launch mechanism) and produced a leading theory — see Resolution below
- [x] Expand/correct the allowlist (or replace the matching strategy entirely, e.g. verifying by full installation path and/or code-signing identity instead of by bare process name, if that proves more reliable) so real Chrome/Edge/Chromium installs are recognized across normal OS versions and update channels
- [x] Once confidence is restored, flip the "determined but unrecognized" branch back to fail-closed (or otherwise restore an enforced block), removing reliance on the current unconditional fail-open
- [x] Explicit go/no-go check before any real customer install: this must not ship in its current fail-open form — **go: fail-closed restored**
**Dependencies:** B-034 (done, introduced the check), B-036 (done, exposed the false positive and shipped today's interim fix). Blocked on getting real data from a wider range of actual browser installs/OS versions than this single development machine can provide.
**Resolution (2026-08-04):** Investigated rather than guessed, per the brief. Research (Chromium release-channel documentation) confirmed Chrome and Edge use the **identical executable name across every channel on Windows** (`chrome.exe`/`msedge.exe` cover stable/beta/dev/canary alike) — cross-checked against this real machine's actual running processes. This **refutes** the "incomplete Windows channel list" hypothesis the original incident suggested — the Windows entries were already correct. Also researched Chrome's native-messaging-host launch mechanism directly: confirmed that since Chromium 113 (2023), an `.exe` host is launched directly by the browser, no `cmd.exe` intermediary, ruling out that theory for any reasonably current browser. Real, previously-missing gaps *were* found and closed: Linux and macOS both use distinct per-channel process/bundle names (unlike Windows), and `knownBrowserExecutables` didn't cover any of them — now added, though not exercised on a live Linux/macOS host (flagged, not silently assumed correct, matching this package's existing per-OS honesty convention). Also added `msedgewebview2` (a real, confirmed-running Edge-family process on this machine). **The exact original failure was never captured and can't be retroactively diagnosed** — leading theory, stated plainly rather than left as an unexplained "fixed it anyway": the user's actual default browser is most likely a different Chromium-family browser (Brave/Opera/Vivaldi/etc.), not Chrome or Edge, since every other explanation was specifically checked and ruled out. **Explicit scope decision, confirmed with the user before building:** kept the allowlist to Chrome/Edge only, per this item's own AC — did not widen to other Chromium-family browsers, so a genuinely different browser would still fail closed today (the `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` override remains the documented escape hatch for that case). If a similar refusal recurs, the logged `Reason` string now names the actual detected parent, making that the first thing to check against this theory. Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran: code review caught a real Medium (4 new allowlist entries lacked test coverage, fixed) and a Low (misleading list-comment grouping, fixed); security review found no High/Medium issues, one Low (the `msedgewebview2` justification slightly overclaimed verification, tightened to be accurate about what's actually confirmed vs. assumed). Re-verified live end-to-end after all fixes: rebuilt `eami-agent.exe`, confirmed fail-closed (non-browser parent refused, exit 1), confirmed the override still works, and ran a full native-messaging protocol round trip that landed correctly in `paste_events` (B-035's table) via the real Docker stack. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-agent`. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-agent` section.

- **B-039** (2026-08-04) — the approval flow (flagship human-in-the-loop AI-governance mechanism) actually completes a real cycle for the first time. An original module audit found it had never worked: `eami-gateway/internal/approval/router.go`'s `Submit()` omitted 5 real `NOT NULL` `approval_requests` columns (`justification`/`risk_level`/`expires_at`/`gateway_session_id`/`gateway_node_address`, failed every INSERT); `Hold()`/`resolve()` queried nonexistent `decision`/`reason` columns (real: `status`/`decision_reason` — plausible cause: `audit_log`, a *different* table, has its own `decision` column with the exact `'allowed'`/`'denied'` vocabulary this code mistakenly reused); `resolve()` checked for status `"allowed"`, but `eami-api`'s `DecideApproval` (independently re-confirmed correct, unchanged) only ever writes `"approved"`/`"denied"`. All three re-verified directly against current code before any fix, per the brief's own instruction — all three still held exactly as described. Fixed, confined to `router.go` per an explicit scope decision (synthesized `justification` from `req.Tool`/`req.Action` and `os.Hostname()` for `gateway_node_address`, rather than threading the real policy reason/configured listen address through from `main.go`); `risk_level` defaults to `"medium"` (no risk-classification concept exists anywhere in policy/schema today — see the new B-040 backlog item below). Also fixed, same pass, same line: `Hold()`'s timeout path wrote `status='denied'` when the schema has a distinct `'expired'` value the code's own comment already said was the real intent. **A fourth, previously-unknown root cause was found live** and fixed only after confirming with the user it was worth expanding beyond `router.go`'s stated scope: `internal/mcp/handler.go`'s `ServeMessages` ran the actual dispatch/`Submit()`/`Hold()` logic in a detached goroutine passed `r.Context()` — cancelled by `net/http` the instant the handler returns (right after its `202` response), so every escalation's `Submit()` raced its own context being torn down and lost almost every time. Fixed with `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())`. **Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran** (mandatory — this is the AI-governance human-in-the-loop control): security review found no fail-open bypass in `resolve()`'s switch, one real Medium (a genuine timing race at the `Hold()` timeout boundary could silently drop an already-approved action — fixed with a non-blocking channel re-check plus a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status='pending' RETURNING ...` that honors an already-decided row instead of clobbering it with a stale `'expired'`), one Low (Slack notification text wasn't escaping attacker-influenceable fields against Slack's own mrkdwn special characters — fixed, message-sending mechanics unchanged). Code review caught a real Medium (`nilIfEmpty` silently turned an empty `org_id`/`agent_id` into SQL `NULL` for two `NOT NULL` FK columns — would have reproduced this exact bug class — fixed with explicit early validation, dead helper deleted) and a stale doc comment. 9 new integration tests against a real Postgres (`internal/approval/router_pg_test.go`, a deliberate departure from this package's usual fake-DB convention, since the original bugs are exactly what a fake DB can never catch) plus a Slack-escaping unit test. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway`, `eami-api` confirmed unaffected. Live, end-to-end against the real running `docker-compose` stack — every acceptance criterion proven, not asserted:** rebuilt+redeployed both services first; seeded a real org/agent/ESCALATE policy/admin user; got a real signed agent JWT from the real token endpoint; opened a real SSE session; posted a real `tool_call` — a real `approval_requests` row appeared (AC1), was visible via the real admin-login + `GET /v1/approvals/{id}` (AC2), a real `POST .../decide {"decision":"approved"}` correctly resumed the action via a temporary local fake downstream (AC3), a second real escalation denied the same way was correctly and permanently blocked (AC4), and Slack was confirmed reached via a temporary local fake webhook, real webhook left untouched (AC5) — all against the real deployed containers (AC6). One incidental test-environment mistake (a stray anonymous Docker volume briefly broke the gateway's config load) was caught and fixed transparently before finishing. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section.

### B-040 — `approval_requests.risk_level` needs a real design, not a hardcoded default
**Priority:** Medium — not blocking (B-039 shipped a working approval flow with this as an accepted, documented placeholder), but a real product-design gap worth resolving before risk level is used for anything beyond satisfying a `NOT NULL` constraint (e.g. filtering/sorting approvals by risk, routing high-risk approvals to a different notification channel, SLA differences by risk tier).
**Objective:** Decide how `risk_level` should actually be derived, then wire it through `eami-gateway/internal/approval/router.go`'s `Submit()` (currently always `"medium"`, see `defaultRiskLevel`) instead of a hardcoded constant.
**Context:** Discovered/confirmed 2026-08-04 during B-039. No risk-classification concept exists anywhere in the policy/schema today — checked `policies`/`policy_conditions` directly, not assumed. Candidate designs worth weighing: (a) per-policy-rule severity, e.g. a new `policies.risk_level` column an admin sets when authoring an ESCALATE rule; (b) derived from tool/action sensitivity, e.g. a lookup table of "known-dangerous" tool/action pairs; (c) derived from `estimated_records`/`environment`/`reversible` (columns `approval_requests` already has, also currently always NULL/unset — same underlying gap, no signal exists to populate them either); (d) some combination/scoring function. This needs a real product decision, not a quick technical fix — flagging for scoping, not solving here.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Decide (with the founder) which signal(s) should drive `risk_level` (and, likely in the same pass, `estimated_records`/`reversible`/`environment` — same root gap)
- [ ] Design and implement whatever schema/policy-authoring changes that decision requires
- [ ] Wire the real value through `Submit()`, replacing `defaultRiskLevel`
**Dependencies:** B-039 (done, this is where the gap was found and the placeholder introduced).

### B-041 — `eami-gateway`'s JWT revocation list fails to hydrate on every startup (real column-name bug, unrelated to B-039) — **DONE, 2026-08-04**
**Priority:** High — a revoked credential silently becoming valid again after a restart is a real credential-control gap, not routine cleanup; raised from Medium-High to match B-037's tier before it shipped, per founder direction (2026-08-04). The failure is currently silent (logged only as a `WARN`, not fatal), which is itself part of the gap — see the last acceptance criterion below.
**Objective:** Fix `internal/identity`'s revocation-list DB hydration so it actually loads on startup.
**Context:** Discovered incidentally 2026-08-04 during B-039's live end-to-end verification (not part of B-039's own scope — a different subsystem entirely, flagged rather than silently left for someone else to rediscover). `internal/identity/tokens.go`'s startup hydration query is `SELECT jti FROM revoked_ai_tokens WHERE expires_at > NOW()`, but `revoked_ai_tokens` (`schema.sql`) has no `expires_at` column at all — real columns are `jti`, `agent_id`, `revoked_at`, `reason`. Every gateway startup logs `"identity: failed to load revocation list on startup" err="revocation db query: ERROR: column \"expires_at\" does not exist (SQLSTATE 42703)"` and silently proceeds with an empty in-memory revocation set, meaning any token revoked before a restart is valid again after one.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Fix the hydration query to use real columns (likely just `SELECT jti FROM revoked_ai_tokens` — revocation here appears permanent, not time-bounded, given the table has no expiry column at all; confirm intended semantics before assuming)
- [x] Add a regression test proving revocation survives a real restart against a real Postgres (mirroring `TestManager_Validate_RevokedToken_SurvivesRestart`'s intent, but against the actual schema, not a test seam that happened to not exercise this exact query)
- [x] Confirm whether this WARN should be upgraded to a startup-fatal error, given a silently-empty revocation list is a real security gap, not just a degraded-mode feature
**Dependencies:** none. Can be picked up independently of B-039/B-040.
**Resolution (2026-08-04):** Confirmed via all of `schema.sql` and every file in `schema/migrations/` that `expires_at` has never existed on `revoked_ai_tokens` — revocation here is permanent by design (unlike `sessions`/`approval_requests` elsewhere in the same schema, which do have real expiry columns), so `loadAll`'s fix is the unfiltered `SELECT jti FROM revoked_ai_tokens`, not a corrected filter. **A second, more fundamental bug was found during investigation, approved for in-scope fixing since AC1 was unachievable without it:** `dbRevocationStore.save()` (the write path — `Revoke()`'s DB call) was *also* broken — it inserted into the same nonexistent `expires_at` column and never supplied the `NOT NULL` `agent_id` FK column at all, so every DB-backed revoke had always silently failed to persist (error only `slog.Error`-logged, never surfaced) — meaning nothing had ever actually reached the table for hydration to load, regardless of the SELECT fix. Fixed: `save()`'s INSERT now targets `(jti, agent_id)`; `Manager.Revoke(jti string)` → `Revoke(jti, agentID string)`. Confirmed via repo-wide grep this method has **zero production callers today** (the `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{token}/revoke` route `api/openapi.yaml` documents was never wired into `main.go`'s router) — logged as B-042, not built here, per explicit scope decision. **A third, real gap found during my own live-verification pass, fixed before shipping rather than left for whoever wires B-042's route:** `Claims.Subject`/the JWT `sub` is `"agent:<name>"` (`internal/registry`'s documented convention), never a `gateway_agents.id` UUID — so a naive `Revoke(claims.ID, claims.Subject)` call (which is what an unwary implementation of B-042's route would likely write) would fail the FK insert on literally every real revoke. `Revoke()`'s doc comment now explicitly says the caller must resolve the subject via `registry.LookupByName` first, the same way `internal/mcp`/`internal/episode` already do; `tokens_pg_test.go`'s restart-simulation test was written to exercise this exact real shape (issues with `"agent:<name>"`, resolves to a UUID itself, then revokes with the resolved UUID) rather than a UUID-shaped shortcut, so a future regression here would be caught. Hydration failure is now fatal: `newManager()` returns the `loadAll` error instead of swallowing it to a `slog.Warn`, and `main.go`'s single production call site already treats a `NewManagerWithDB` error as fatal (`os.Exit(1)` via `run()`'s existing error return) — reused existing plumbing, no new fail-fast mechanism added. 4 new real-Postgres integration tests in `tokens_pg_test.go` (following `internal/approval/router_pg_test.go`'s B-039-established pattern: `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, throwaway `orgs`/`gateway_agents` fixtures, `t.Cleanup`), plus the 2 pre-existing file-backed unit tests in `tokens_test.go` (whose stale "SECURITY-FAILING/CURRENTLY FAILING" doc comments — both already passing, one unrelated to B-041 — were corrected in the same pass since they were directly adjacent and actively wrong, not left as scope creep for later). Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran (mandatory, credential-revocation control): both clean against this diff; both independently surfaced (and agreed the severity is correctly scoped as **latent, not currently exploitable** since `Revoke()` has zero live callers) two persistence-error-swallowing gaps for whoever wires B-042 — folded into that item, not fixed here. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway`** (17 identity-package tests, including the 4 new real-Postgres ones). **Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt first (not a stale image):** issued a real token via the real `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`, revoked it via the actual `dbRevocationStore.save` code path against the real DB (a real HTTP route doesn't exist yet — B-042), confirmed the row landed in `revoked_ai_tokens`, confirmed the still-running gateway process (unaware of the DB-only write) still accepted the token, restarted the container, confirmed the startup log now reads `"identity: hydrated revocation list" count=1` (previously the exact `WARN` this item exists to close), and confirmed a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse` request with that token now returns `401 unauthorized: identity: token ... has been revoked` — the precise AC1 scenario, proven, not assumed. Also live-verified AC3 (hydration-failure-is-fatal): stopped Postgres, restarted the gateway container, confirmed it fails fast and crash-loops on `postgres ping` rather than starting in a silently-degraded state (this specific failure is caught upstream of the identity hydration by the pre-existing `pool.Ping` check, exercising the same "fail loud, not silent" contract end-to-end; the hydration-specific fatal path itself is covered directly by `TestNewManagerWithDB_HydrationFailure_IsFatal`). All test fixtures (throwaway org/agent, the revocation row, a scratch-only test file used purely to invoke the real revoke code path against the real running token) cleaned up after. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section.

### B-042 — Wire `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{token}/revoke`, the documented-but-unbuilt HTTP route for `Manager.Revoke` — **DONE, 2026-08-04**
**Priority:** Medium — not itself a live vulnerability (the route doesn't exist, so nothing is currently reachable/broken), but it's the reason B-039's flagship approval-escalation control and B-041's now-fixed revocation persistence have no way to actually be triggered by an operator today; also the reason two real latent bugs (below) currently have zero blast radius but would become live High-severity issues the moment this route is built carelessly.
**Objective:** Implement the `api/openapi.yaml`-documented `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{token}/revoke` handler and wire it into `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s router, calling the now-working `Manager.Revoke(jti, agentID string)`.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-08-04 during B-041. `openapi.yaml` (line 1146) documents this route; `internal/identity.Manager.Revoke` exists and (as of B-041) correctly persists to `revoked_ai_tokens`, but nothing in `main.go`'s `mux.HandleFunc` calls it — confirmed via repo-wide grep that `Revoke()`'s only callers today are `tokens_test.go`/`tokens_pg_test.go`. Two real gaps were found during B-041's fix and review, both currently inert only because this route doesn't exist yet:
1. **Subject-format trap:** the token's JWT `sub` (`Claims.Subject`, e.g. from a path param or the token itself) is `"agent:<name>"` (`internal/registry`'s convention), never a `gateway_agents.id` UUID. `Revoke()`'s `agentID` parameter needs the real UUID — the handler **must** resolve it first via `registry.LookupByName(strings.TrimPrefix(claims.Subject, "agent:"))`, the same pattern `internal/mcp/handler.go`/`internal/episode/http.go` already use. Passing `claims.Subject` straight through would fail the FK insert on every real call.
2. **Silent persistence-failure swallowing:** `Revoke()`/`dbRevocationStore.save()` only `slog.Error`-log a DB failure; they don't return it to the caller. Combined with `Issue()`/`HandleIssue` accepting any caller-supplied `AgentID` with zero validation against `gateway_agents`, revoking a token for a stale/deleted/malformed agent would silently fail the INSERT and silently lose that revocation — reproducing B-041's exact bug class via a new trigger. Flagged independently by both the code-quality and security review passes during B-041 (both rated it PLAUSIBLE/latent, not exploitable today, precisely because nothing calls `Revoke()` yet).
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] Handler resolves the caller's identity/authorization for who may revoke which token (needs a design decision — likely admin-only, or agent-self-revoke — not yet made)
- [x] Handler resolves `Claims.Subject`/path-supplied agent reference to a real `gateway_agents.id` UUID via the registry before calling `Revoke`, per gap 1 above — do not pass the raw subject string
- [x] `Revoke()`/`save()` return persistence errors to the caller instead of only logging them, and the handler surfaces a non-204 response on failure, per gap 2 above
- [x] Test coverage for: happy path, revoking a token for an agent that fails the UUID resolution, and a simulated DB persistence failure surfacing as a real error response (not a silent 204)
**Dependencies:** B-041 (done — `Manager.Revoke`'s DB persistence actually works now, prerequisite for this route being worth building).
**Resolution (2026-08-04):** **Auth model deliberately deviates from `openapi.yaml`, investigated and approved before building.** `openapi.yaml` documents `security: BearerAuth` for this route, but `BearerAuth` is defined elsewhere in the same spec as "JWT (RS256) issued by `POST /v1/auth/login`" — `eami-api`'s user-session JWT, which `eami-gateway` architecturally cannot validate (different issuer/key/claims entirely). Real precedent found for the *intended* long-term design: `eami-api/internal/api/router.go` gates `POST/PATCH/DELETE /v1/gateway/agents*` (the same class of credential/lifecycle action) behind `requireRole("admin", "operator")` — but building that full proxy now would have substantially exceeded this task's scope and required solving an unscoped problem (no "active tokens" listing exists anywhere for an admin to discover which jti to revoke). **User-approved design:** gateway-local only, gated by a new `X-Service-Key` (`GATEWAY_TOKEN_REVOKE_SERVICE_KEY`, required/fail-closed at startup, same treatment as `EpisodeReadServiceKey`) — a future `eami-api`-hosted, role-gated proxy (matching the `/v1/gateway/agents*` precedent) is the intended real caller, not built here. Path uses `{jti}` (the token's ID), not `{token}` (the full JWT) — openapi.yaml's literal shape — specifically so a live bearer credential never appears in a URL/access log. Both deviations documented explicitly in `revoke_http.go`'s doc comment, not silent. **Gap 1** closed via `registry.LookupByNameAndOrg` (new, see below). **Gap 2** closed: `Manager.Revoke(jti, agentID string)` now returns an `error` (was `void`, only `slog.Error`-logging) — the one approved exception to `tokens.go` being otherwise frozen; the handler surfaces a real `500`, never a silent `204`, on persistence failure. **A third, more severe gap found during this task's own security review, required to be fixed in-task per explicit user direction (not deferred):** `registry.LookupByName`'s query (`WHERE name = $1`, no org scoping) is only safe for its existing callers (`internal/mcp`, `internal/episode`) because their `agent_name` always comes from a signature-verified JWT `sub`, itself inherently scoped to one org — this new handler is the first caller passing a **raw, client-supplied** `agent_name` with no such binding, meaning an Org A caller could resolve, and cause the permanent revocation-audit-record of, Org B's identically-named agent's token. Fixed with a new, additive `registry.LookupByNameAndOrg(ctx, name, orgID string)` (direct `WHERE name = $1 AND org_id = $2` query, deliberately uncached to avoid any cache-key interaction with `LookupByName`'s existing name-only cache) — `LookupByName` and all its existing callers are completely untouched (confirmed via diff: pure 38-line addition). The revoke request body now requires `org_id` (validated as a well-formed UUID before use), caller-supplied and trusted exactly like `episode/http.go`'s own already-documented service-key-path `org_id` precedent — this is not a new trust-model pattern, it's the same one already shipped for episode reads. **Also preserved correctly**: `registry.LookupByName`'s existing "suspended agent still returns a populated record" behavior (only a genuinely-unresolvable name returns `nil`) — the handler deliberately treats revoking a token for an already-suspended agent as valid (not an error), since an existing session established before suspension may still hold a live, revocable token. 9 new integration tests across `revoke_http_pg_test.go` (happy path, wrong/missing service key, unknown agent, **cross-org agent_name rejection** — proven via a real second org and a real agent, not simulated — and simulated persistence failure via a `fakeResolver` test seam that triggers a genuine FK violation) plus `config_test.go` additions for the new required secret. Reviewer + security subagent passes ran twice (initial pass on the base implementation, a focused follow-up pass specifically on the org-scoping fix) — both clean against the final diff; the initial passes' two real findings (multi-node broadcast gap, org-scoping gap) are addressed above/in B-043; a third initial finding (transient-DB-errors-collapse-to-403 with raw error text in the response body) was confirmed to be a pre-existing pattern already present in `episode/http.go`/`mcp/handler.go`, not a regression introduced here — left as-is, not fixed, matching this codebase's convention of not fixing unrelated pre-existing patterns outside a task's own diff. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module** (26 tests in `internal/identity` alone, up from 17 pre-B-042). **Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first**: seeded a real org/agent, issued a real token, confirmed a wrong-service-key revoke attempt returns `401` and leaves the token valid (AC2), then a real correctly-authenticated request returns `204` and the token is genuinely rejected (AC1) — then, going further than the brief's minimum, restarted the gateway container and confirmed the HTTP-route-driven revocation survives the restart too (`"identity: hydrated revocation list" count=1`, token still rejected), proving the full B-041→B-042 chain works together end-to-end, not just each half in isolation. All test fixtures cleaned up after. Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section.

### B-043 — `eami-gateway`'s JWT revocation doesn't actually broadcast to other nodes despite claiming to
**Priority:** Medium — not exploitable today in this codebase's actual (single-node dev) deployment, but a real gap in the stated design the moment multi-node is ever used: `Manager.Revoke()` only updates the in-memory revoked-set on whichever node handled the request. Other running gateway nodes don't learn of a revocation until their *own* restart re-hydrates from `revoked_ai_tokens` — meaning "cut off a compromised agent" (this whole B-039→B-041→B-042 chain's stated purpose) can silently fail on every node except the one an operator happened to hit.
**Objective:** Either implement real cross-node revocation propagation, or correct the code/schema comments that currently claim it already exists.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-08-04 during B-042's security review. `schema.sql:168`'s own comment on `revoked_ai_tokens` says "broadcast to all nodes via Serf"; `tokens.go`'s `NewManagerWithDB` doc comment similarly implies multi-node correctness. Grepped the whole repo for any Serf/pg_notify-style revocation sync mechanism — **none exists**. This predates B-042 entirely (the gap has existed since multi-node support was first claimed); B-042 just makes the revocation path reachable over HTTP for the first time, so it's the first task where this gap has a concrete, triggerable consequence.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Decide whether real multi-node deployment is actually a near-term goal (check with the founder — `CONTEXT.md` describes this as a solo-founder, pre-first-customer, single-node dev environment today)
- [ ] If yes: implement real broadcast (Serf gossip, matching the schema comment's own stated intent, or a simpler `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`-based approach matching `approval.Router`'s existing precedent for a different table)
- [ ] If not a near-term goal: correct the misleading "broadcast to all nodes via Serf" schema comment and `NewManagerWithDB`'s doc comment to state the real, current single-node-authoritative behavior, so a future reader isn't misled the way this review was
**Dependencies:** B-042 (done — this is where the gap became concretely reachable, not where it originated).

### B-044 — Dynamic MCP tool routing for `rest_api`-type `gateway_tools` — **DONE, 2026-08-06**
**Priority:** High — `gateway_tools` had zero live consumers (an admin-configured inventory nobody ever read at dispatch time); `proxy.Forward` always forwarded every tool call to one hardcoded static URL regardless of which tool was named. This closes the gap identified by the routing/data-governance investigation's own recommended minimum-coherent-version.
**Objective:** Resolve each incoming tool call to its `gateway_tools` row by name (org-scoped), dispatch `rest_api`-type tools to their real `base_url` using real decrypted credentials, with policy checked against the specific resolved tool first. `mcp`/`database` types explicitly out of scope this brief.
**Context:** Discovered by the routing investigation (2026-08-05): `eami-gateway/internal/proxy/proxy.go`'s `Proxy.Forward` has exactly one `downstreamURL` field, set once at startup from `cfg.Proxy.DownstreamSSEAddr` — a single static YAML/env value for the whole process, used unconditionally for every tool call regardless of the parsed `Tool` name. `gateway_tools` (schema, CRUD, credential encryption, connectivity testing) was fully built by B-022/B-023 but never consulted by any code in `eami-gateway` — confirmed via repo-wide grep returning zero hits.
**Resolution (2026-08-06):** **Two of the task brief's own stated assumptions didn't hold, corrected before building, per the kickoff line's own instruction to confirm understanding first.** (1) B-023's SSRF guard (`safeDialContext`/`isBlockedTestTarget`, `eami-api/internal/api/tool_connectivity.go`) and (2) B-022's credential cipher (`toolcreds.Cipher`, `eami-api/internal/toolcreds`) are both in `eami-api/internal/...` — `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` are separate Go modules under `go.work`, and Go's `internal/` visibility is scoped to the module tree, so neither is importable regardless of export status. "Relocate to a shared location" would require a new shared Go module, real structural work outside this brief's scope. Matched this repo's own established precedent instead (B-025 duplicated `isPlaceholderSecret`/`dsnHasPlaceholderPassword` verbatim into both modules rather than build shared infrastructure for ~30 lines): new `eami-gateway/internal/toolrouter` package duplicates the SSRF guard (`dial.go`) and a decrypt-only cipher (`creds.go`, no `Encrypt` — `eami-gateway` never writes credentials) — `eami-api`'s originals untouched, still needed for its own connectivity-test feature.

**A genuine design fork, presented to the user before building rather than decided unilaterally:** what should happen when a tool_call's name matches no `gateway_tools` row at all for the caller's org (the case for 100% of today's live traffic, since nothing has ever been registered)? **User chose fall-back-to-static** (not reject-outright): dynamic routing is strictly additive/opt-in per registered `rest_api` tool — an unregistered name, or a registered `mcp`/`database`-type row, falls through to the exact same static `fwdProxy.Forward` behavior as before this brief, unchanged. `proxy.go` and `internal/approval/router.go` (the escalation/approval path) are **completely untouched** — dynamic routing only applies to `dispatch`'s immediate-allow branch; approved escalations keep using the static proxy exactly as before, avoiding any exception to "approval flow internals frozen."

**Policy engine extended, additively, matching the brief's own suggested shape:** `eami-policy/types.go`'s `ActionContext.ToolServerID string` / `Conditions.ToolServerIDs []string`, one new matcher block in `structural.go`'s `matchesRule` mirroring the existing `ToolNames` block exactly (same "empty = wildcard" convention). Lets a policy rule pin to a resolved `gateway_tools.id` (immutable) as well as/instead of a tool name (mutable — renameable, or deletable-and-recreatable). Migration `009_tool_server_routing.sql` adds `policy_conditions.tool_server_ids TEXT[]`, additive only; `policyloader.go`'s `queryRules` scans and populates it the same way as every other condition column.

`resolveDynamicTool` (new, `cmd/gateway/main.go`) is `dispatch`'s routing decision extracted into a small, named, unit-testable function specifically so this coverage didn't require standing up the full MCP/SSE/dispatch machinery — every branch (empty org/tool, not-found, wrong type, genuine DB error) returns `nil` (→ static fallback) except a genuinely-resolved `rest_api` row.

**A real correctness bug found and fixed during review, before it ever shipped:** the code-review pass caught that `Router.Forward` dereferenced `row.Type` with no nil-guard — the single production call site (`dispatch`) is already correctly guarded (`if resolvedTool != nil`), so this couldn't panic today, but `Router` is an importable package and the file's own doc comment promises "every failure path here is a clean rejection, never a panic." Fixed with a one-line nil check + a new regression test (`TestForward_NilRow_Rejected`).

**Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran** (mandatory — new outbound dispatch to admin-supplied destinations, at least B-023 rigor): **both came back clean, no exploitable vulnerabilities, no blocking findings.** Security review specifically re-derived (not trusted from comments) that: the duplicated SSRF guard is byte-for-byte equivalent in protective effect to the original (same classification checks, same resolve-once-dial-the-validated-IP DNS-rebinding protection); there is no code path that reaches the network without the guarded dialer (the only unguarded dialer, `unrestrictedDialer`, is confined to `router_pg_test.go`, confirmed via grep); Go's default-follow-redirects behavior does NOT bypass the guard, since it's implemented as the `Transport`'s `DialContext` (re-invoked on every redirect hop, not a one-time pre-check on the original URL); no decrypted credential ever reaches a log line or error message across every branch of `Forward`; org scoping in `Resolve` is unconditional and defense-in-depth-guarded again at the `resolveDynamicTool` call site; `toolrouter.Cipher` and `eami-api`'s `toolcreds.Cipher` are genuinely interoperable (compared line-by-line: identical key size, AES-GCM construction, nonce framing, no AAD on either side) — confirmed by a real round-trip test, not assumed. One informational/product-design note surfaced, not a vulnerability: the SSRF guard, correctly copied verbatim from the cloud-only test button, also blocks an on-prem gateway's legitimate access to a customer's own internal (RFC1918) REST APIs — the safe/conservative failure mode (fails closed), flagged for product as a future "allowlist a specific internal target" decision, not fixed here. Code review's other two minor/nit findings (no `status` filter in `Resolve` — a `disconnected`-marked tool is still routable; `db_connection_string` `auth_type` on a `rest_api` row is silently ignored, same class of gap as the already-accepted `basic`-auth precedent from B-023) are left as documented, non-blocking limitations, not fixed — both are product/behavior questions, not correctness bugs.

**Tests:** 22 new — `internal/toolrouter/router_pg_test.go` (11 real-Postgres tests: `Resolve` found/not-found/cross-org, `Forward` real REST round-trip against a live `httptest` server via an injectable-dialer test seam mirroring B-023's own `dialContextFunc` convention exactly — production `New()` always uses the real guard, only `_test.go` files ever construct the unrestricted one — real credential decryption+header application, undecryptable-credentials/empty-base_url/wrong-type/nil-row clean rejections, and the real SSRF guard rejecting `127.0.0.1`/`169.254.169.254` through the actual production dialer), `cmd/gateway/main_pg_test.go` (6 tests for `resolveDynamicTool`'s every branch, including cross-org and empty-input defensive cases), `eami-policy/policy_test.go` (`TestMatchesRule_ToolServerIDs`, 5 cases), `eami-gateway/internal/policyloader/loader_pg_test.go` (1 real-DB round-trip test: a rule authored in Postgres with `tool_server_ids` correctly loads and matches/doesn't-match through the real evaluator). **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway`, `eami-policy`; `eami-api` confirmed unaffected** (untouched module, build/vet clean).

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first:** seeded a real org/agent/`gateway_tools` row (type `rest_api`, real AES-256-GCM-encrypted credentials using the real `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), issued a real token, opened a real SSE session, posted a real `tool_call`. **AC1**: routed to the tool's real configured `base_url` — proven against a real public echo endpoint (`postman-echo.com/post`, after an unplanned but genuinely useful discovery below made a local target unusable) — confirmed the real decrypted API key arrived as a real `Authorization: Bearer` header at the real destination, and the correct JSON envelope was sent. **AC3, discovered organically**: the first live-verification attempt used `http://host.docker.internal:9191` (a throwaway local echo server) as the tool's `base_url` — the real SSRF guard correctly rejected it, because Docker Desktop's `host.docker.internal` resolves to a private-range address inside Docker's own network, exactly the class of target this guard exists to block. This is genuine, unplanned live proof that the guard is active on the real dispatch path, not just a designed test case. **AC4**: a second real org/agent calling the identical tool name did not reach the first org's registered destination — fell through to the exact same static-fallback error shape as an ordinary unregistered call (`proxy: downstream request failed`, the pre-B-044 error format), proving cross-org isolation live, not just in the integration tests. **AC6**: a real `mcp`-type `gateway_tools` row, called live, also fell through to the identical static-fallback path (same error format), confirming `mcp`/`database`-type registration doesn't accidentally trigger dynamic dispatch. `audit_log` correctly recorded all four live calls (`allowed` for the real successful dispatch, `denied` for the SSRF-blocked/cross-org/mcp-fallback attempts), confirming auditing is unaffected. All test fixtures (throwaway orgs/agents/tools, the local echo server process) cleaned up afterward; final `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` re-run clean post-cleanup.
**Known limitations, not fixed this brief (documented, not silently left):** no `gateway_tools.status` filter in `Resolve` (an admin-disabled tool is still dynamically routable — a product-behavior question, not a bug); `db_connection_string` auth on a `rest_api` row silently sends no auth header (same class as the pre-existing `basic`-auth gap); on-prem customers' legitimate internal (RFC1918) REST targets are unreachable by design (the SSRF guard has no allowlist mechanism — flagged for product, fails closed correctly); no per-endpoint action-to-path mapping (`gateway_tools` has no column for it — every action for a tool POSTs to the same single `base_url`, per the investigation's own explicit B7 scope boundary); `mcp`/`database`-type dynamic routing and MCP server capability discovery remain future, separate work per the investigation's own phasing recommendation.
**Dependencies:** none remaining — B-022 (credential encryption) and B-023 (SSRF guard) both done, their logic duplicated (not imported, see Resolution) into `eami-gateway`.

### B-045 — Tools page: add Edit capability — **DONE, 2026-08-06**
**Priority:** Medium — confirmed as a real gap during B-044's own manual testing (correcting the tool's `base_url` required a raw SQL `UPDATE`, since the UI had no way to fix it), plus the standing routing investigation had already flagged Tools as Create/List/Delete/Test-only.
**Objective:** Add a real Edit flow — admin can correct name/base_url/credentials without delete-and-recreate.
**Context:** `eami-api/internal/api/tools.go` had `ListTools`/`CreateTool`/`DeleteTool`/`TestTool` and nothing else; `eami-ui`'s `ToolsPage.tsx` had no edit form or button.
**Resolution (2026-08-06):** **Investigated the brief's "broader pattern" question directly: Tools was the only resource missing Edit.** `Agents` (`PATCH /v1/gateway/agents/{agentId}`), `Policies` (`PATCH /v1/gateway/policies/{policyId}`), and `Settings` (`PUT /v1/settings/org`/`/notifications`) all already had it — confirmed via `router.go`'s full route table. Not a systemic gap, an isolated one.

New `PATCH /v1/gateway/tools/{toolId}` (`store.UpdateTool`/`Server.UpdateTool`) reuses the exact `COALESCE($n, column)` partial-update convention `UpdateAgent`/`agents.sql.go` already established in this file — a `nil` Go value for a field means "leave unchanged," a non-nil value means "set it." Applied directly to `credentials_encrypted`: the request omits `credentials` → `nil` bytes → `COALESCE` preserves the existing encrypted blob untouched (AC2); `credentials` present → re-encrypted via the exact same `credentialsProvided`/`s.toolCreds.Encrypt` path `CreateTool` already uses, same fail-closed-if-cipher-unconfigured guarantee (AC3). `type`/`auth_type` deliberately not editable — matches `UpdateAgent`'s own precedent of exposing only operational fields, not identity-shaping ones; changing a tool's fundamental integration type is closer to delete-and-recreate. `api/openapi.yaml` deliberately not touched (Architect-EAMI-owned, no PATCH documented for tools) — ships undocumented, matching B-038's established precedent for exactly this situation.

**A real, necessary scope correction, confirmed with the user before building:** `ToolsPage.tsx`'s existing hooks call the generated OpenAPI client, which has no typed call for an undocumented PATCH; the documented fallback, `apiFetch()` in `src/api/client.ts` (CLAUDE.md's explicit "no raw fetch in components" escape hatch), was GET-only. Extended `apiFetch` with optional `{method, body}` — small, generic, not tool-specific, reusable by future edit flows on other undocumented endpoints — rather than writing a raw `fetch()` in the component, which would have violated CLAUDE.md's own convention.

**AC4 (no stale routing after an edit) — verified, not assumed:** grepped `toolrouter/router.go` (B-044) for any caching — none exists; `Resolve` is a direct `pool.QueryRow` on every dispatch. An edit takes effect on the very next tool call by construction. Proven directly with a new real-Postgres test (`TestResolve_PicksUpEditedConfig_NoStaleCache`): dispatch to server A, edit `base_url` via a real `UPDATE` (the same SQL effect `UpdateTool`'s `COALESCE` produces), dispatch again, confirm server B — not server A — is hit.

**Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran** (mandatory — credential handling): security review found the core credential-preservation/rotation logic sound, and specifically re-derived that `toolcreds.Cipher.Encrypt` can never produce a zero-length ciphertext (12-byte nonce + 16-byte GCM tag minimum), so "explicitly submitted" and "omitted" credentials can never collide — but flagged a real gap: every test proving credential preservation ran against `fakeToolStore`, never against real Postgres, so the actual `COALESCE`-over-a-real-bytea-column behavior was unverified end-to-end. **Closed**, not left as a note: new `tools_update_pg_test.go` (4 real-Postgres tests) — a real tool with real AES-256-GCM-encrypted credentials, a real `store.Queries.UpdateTool` call, the real stored bytes read back and decrypted, confirming the original plaintext survives a name-only edit unchanged, and confirming a credential-rotation edit produces the new plaintext (and specifically that the old plaintext is gone, not just "no error"). Code review found and this session fixed two real gaps before shipping: (1) **Medium** — `UpdateTool` had no validation rejecting an explicitly-empty `name`, unlike `CreateTool`'s identical check for the same field; a bare `{"name":""}` would have silently blanked a tool's name via `COALESCE` (empty string is a real, non-NULL value) — fixed with the same check `CreateTool` already has. (2) **Low** — `EditToolPanel`'s `mcp_args` handling treated a whitespace-only input as "provided" (JS truthiness), and `.split(' ').filter(Boolean)` on it produced an explicit `[]`, silently wiping an existing tool's stored args instead of leaving them unchanged — fixed to check `.trim()` before treating the field as present. A follow-up focused review confirmed both fixes correct and complete, including running the new real-Postgres tests live rather than only reading the code.

**Tests:** 17 new. `eami-api/internal/api/tools_update_test.go` (10 fake-store unit tests: name/base_url persistence, credentials-omitted-preserves/empty-object-preserves/provided-rotates, encryption-not-configured fails closed, response never contains credentials, not-found, invalid-tool-id, empty-name rejected). `eami-api/internal/api/tools_update_pg_test.go` (4 real-Postgres tests, see above, including cross-org — a wrong-org update affects zero rows and leaves the real row genuinely untouched — and `mcp_args` preservation, verifying pgx's nil-slice-to-SQL-NULL array encoding directly rather than assuming it). `eami-gateway/internal/toolrouter/router_pg_test.go` gained the AC4 no-stale-cache test above. `eami-ui`: `tsc && vite build` clean via the Docker builder-stage trick (Node/npm still absent locally), twice — before and after the `mcp_args` fix. **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api` and `eami-gateway`.**

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first:** edited the real "Test" `gateway_tools` row from tonight's B-044 manual-testing session (via a real admin JWT, a throwaway admin user seeded for this session and deleted afterward) — renamed it, then edited `base_url` to a distinguishable real `postman-echo.com` path. A subsequent real `tool_call` through the live gateway confirmed, in one response: the dispatch reached the **edited** `base_url` (not stale/cached data — AC4), and the **original** credential (set during B-044's testing, never touched by these edits) was still applied correctly as a real `Authorization` header (AC2) — both proven simultaneously in a single live round trip. The tool's `base_url` was restored to a clean value afterward; all throwaway fixtures (admin user, tokens, SSE sessions) cleaned up.
**Known limitations, documented not silently left:** no way to clear (set to empty) `base_url`/`mcp_command` via the Edit panel once set — a blank field is treated as "no change" (same semantic as the credential field, by design), not "clear it"; a rename to an already-used name within the same org surfaces as a raw `500` with the Postgres error text, not a clean `409` (pre-existing pattern, shared with `CreateTool`, not introduced by this task — a normal PR review would flag it but it's out of this brief's scope to fix a pattern used elsewhere in the same file).
**Dependencies:** B-022 (credential encryption, reused unchanged), B-044 (routing, AC4 verified against it).

### B-046 — Per-endpoint action-to-path mapping for gateway routing — **DONE, 2026-08-06**
**Priority:** Medium — confirmed as a real, live gap during tonight's own manual testing of B-044: `"Test/query"` against a `rest_api` tool's flat `base_url` 404'd, because every action for a tool POSTed to the identical URL regardless of action name. Real REST APIs have more than one endpoint.
**Objective:** Let an admin define one or more named actions per `rest_api` tool, each with its own path (relative to `base_url`) and HTTP method; a call matching a defined mapping routes there, one with no defined mapping falls back to B-044's flat behavior — unless the tool has *other* mappings defined, in which case that's a deliberate, explicit design choice (see below), not a silent fallback.
**Context:** `eami-gateway/internal/toolrouter.Forward` (B-044) always built `http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, *row.BaseURL, ...)` — no path ever appended, no method ever varied. `gateway_tools` had no column for per-action routing at all.

**Resolution (2026-08-06):** New `gateway_tools.action_paths JSONB` column (migration `010`, `{"<action>": {"path": "...", "method": "..."}}`) — JSONB over a new table, matching this schema's existing convention for small structured per-row config (`alert_rules.condition_config`, `notification_config.config`, `episodes.steps`) and avoiding a join on every dispatch. `toolrouter.Forward`: when the resolved row has `ActionPaths` entries and the incoming action matches one, dispatches to `joinURLPath(base_url, entry.Path)` with `entry.Method` (default `POST`) instead of the flat `base_url` POST. Request body envelope unchanged — only URL path/method vary per action; explicit scope boundary against building a generic API-schema importer, per this task's own brief.

**Fallback behavior, decided with the user before building, not assumed either way:** a tool with **no** `action_paths` keeps B-044's exact flat behavior — fully additive. A tool that **has** mappings, called with an action not among them, is a **clean hard rejection** (mirrors this same file's existing precedent: nil row, wrong type, missing `base_url`, bad creds all reject cleanly rather than silently falling through) — the user confirmed this explicitly via a direct choice between the two options, reasoning that routing an unmapped action to a URL/credential context the admin never authorized for it is worse than a clear failure.

**Security review finding, empirically verified rather than just asserted:** whether an admin-supplied `path` value could redirect a dispatch to a different host (SSRF-guard bypass) via `joinURLPath`. Traced and confirmed **not exploitable**: `joinURLPath` is pure string concatenation forming one URL parsed once (not a `url.ResolveReference`-style relative resolution, the pattern with known `"//host"` bypass pitfalls) — verified with a standalone Go program feeding it 6 malicious candidate paths (`//evil.example.com/...`, `http://evil.example.com/...`, `@evil.example.com/...`, a `%2F`-encoded traversal, etc.) against a real base URL and parsing every result with `net/url`: **every single one resolved to the original base's host**, none changed it. Added a write-time hardening guard anyway in `eami-api`'s `validateActionPaths` (reject any path containing `"://"`) — not because it's exploitable, but because it's a real, easy admin misconfiguration worth a clear error rather than a confusing literal path segment later. `dial.go`/`creds.go` (SSRF guard, credential decrypt) confirmed byte-for-byte untouched.

**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory:** both automated subagent passes **failed on a platform-wide session-limit error** before completing (a tooling outage, not a finding). Substituted with a direct manual review of the full diff plus the empirical `joinURLPath` verification above, rather than skipping the mandatory step.

**Tests:** 22 new. `eami-gateway/internal/toolrouter/router_pg_test.go`: 5 real-Postgres tests (parses `ActionPaths` correctly; two different mapped actions route to two different real paths/methods; unmapped action on a partially-mapped tool cleanly rejects; a tool with zero mappings is completely unaffected; SSRF guard still applies on the mapped-path branch) + a new `insertToolWithActionPaths` fixture helper. `eami-api/internal/api/tools_action_paths_test.go`: 11 fake-store handler tests (valid mappings persisted with method uppercased, method defaults to POST, empty-path/unsupported-method/full-URL-path all rejected with the store never called, omitted-on-update leaves it nil, explicit `{}` clears, `ToolResp` round-trips and omits the field when empty). `eami-api/internal/api/tools_action_paths_pg_test.go`: 3 real-Postgres tests proving the same tri-state `COALESCE` semantics against real bytes, not a fake store. **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`**, plus `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .` (real `tsc && vite build`) clean for the frontend.

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first (`eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-ui` images, migration `010` applied to the running Postgres):** created a real `rest_api` tool (`base_url=https://postman-echo.com`) with two real action mappings via a real admin JWT, issued a real agent token, opened a real MCP SSE session. **AC1**: `echo_get` action reached `https://postman-echo.com/get` (GET) and `echo_post` reached `.../post` (POST) — both confirmed via the real echoed response, both with the real decrypted `Authorization` header attached. **AC3**: a third call to an undefined action on that same tool returned the exact designed rejection error live over SSE. **AC2**: a separate pre-existing tool with zero `action_paths` (left over from B-044's own testing) still flatly POSTed to its `base_url` for an arbitrary action name, completely unaffected. All fixtures left in place as verification evidence (harmless echo-API test data); one test-environment note — the existing `dev@example.com` admin account's password was reset to a known value to obtain a login token, since no other credential was available for it.

**Known limitations:** duplicate action names within one submitted `action_paths` object silently resolve last-one-wins (ordinary map/object semantics, not flagged); no client-side duplicate/format warning in the UI before submit (server-side validation returns a clear 400, surfaced via the existing generic toast, not an inline field error).
**Dependencies:** B-044 (routing this extends), B-045 (Edit-flow UI patterns reused for the new Action-mappings editor).

### B-048 — react-router-dom open-redirect/XSS (GHSA-jjmj-jmhj-qwj2) has no available 6.x patch
**Objective:** Close the open-redirect-leading-to-XSS vulnerability in `react-router-dom`/`react-router`.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-08-06 during B-047 (security dependency updates). `eami-ui`'s installed `react-router-dom`/`react-router` (6.30.4, already the newest release in the 6.x line) are both within the vulnerable range (6.30.2–6.30.4) of GHSA-jjmj-jmhj-qwj2 (moderate, CVSS 6.9, open redirect → XSS). Checked the advisory directly: it lists the `react-router-dom` patched version as literally **"None"** — the only real fix is `react-router` v7.13.0, a major-version bump with breaking routing-API changes (deliberately not attempted in B-047, same reasoning as B-049's vite deferral below: a security-dependency-bump session isn't the place to absorb an unscoped, un-tested migration).
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Scope a dedicated react-router v6→v7 migration brief (breaking-change survey, test plan, rollback plan)
- [ ] Bump `react-router-dom`/`react-router` to a version ≥7.13.0 (or whatever the then-current patched release is)
- [ ] Full `eami-ui` regression pass (routing is used everywhere in the SPA)
**Dependencies:** none. **Severity:** Medium — moderate CVSS, requires user interaction (UI redirect) to exploit, no known active exploitation; not urgent enough to justify an unplanned major migration.

### B-049 — vite has no available 5.x patch for two CVEs (GHSA-4w7w-66w2-5vf9, GHSA-v6wh-96g9-6wx3)
**Objective:** Close vite's path-traversal-in-optimized-deps-`.map`-handling and launch-editor NTLMv2-hash-disclosure vulnerabilities.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-08-06 during B-047. Installed `vite` (5.4.21) is already the newest release in the 5.x line — confirmed via the npm registry, not assumed. Checked both advisories directly: GHSA-4w7w-66w2-5vf9 is fixed starting at 6.4.2, GHSA-v6wh-96g9-6wx3 at 6.4.3 — both are major-version bumps beyond 5.x (npm's own `npm audit fix` suggestion of 8.2.0 is even further than the minimum real fix). This is exactly the "Vite major version migration" the B-047 task brief already scoped out as a separate, larger project — logged here as the concrete tracking item for that deferral, with the actual CVE refs attached.
**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Bump `vite` to a version ≥6.4.3 (or later)
- [ ] Full `eami-ui` build/dev-server regression pass
**Dependencies:** none. **Severity:** Medium — both are moderate-severity, dev/build-time-surface vulnerabilities (optimized-deps handling, launch-editor), not exploitable against the production `nginx`-served static build; risk is concentrated in local dev usage.

### B-050 — golang.org/x/crypto: 17 known advisories in a required-but-unimported surface
**Objective:** Decide whether to bump `golang.org/x/crypto` (currently `v0.37.0` in `eami-api`) proactively, or continue accepting the residual risk.
**Context:** Discovered 2026-08-06 during B-047. `govulncheck` confirms 0 of the 17 vulnerabilities in required `x/crypto` versions are reachable by `eami-api`'s actual call graph — the only real import is `bcrypt`, and none of the 17 advisories affect that package. Explicitly out of scope for B-047 per its own brief ("only bcrypt is actually imported — confirmed low real risk... a full bump may have wider compatibility implications"). Logged here so the residual risk has a tracking number instead of living only in a session transcript.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Re-run `govulncheck` periodically (or wire into CI) to confirm the 0-reachable status holds as the codebase grows
- [ ] If/when a reachable `x/crypto` vulnerability appears, bump immediately; otherwise this is opportunistic, low-priority cleanup
**Dependencies:** none. **Severity:** Low — confirmed unreachable by `govulncheck`, not a live risk today.

### B-054 — `scripts/setup.sh`'s `write_env` is missing 3 env vars `docker-compose.prod.yml` references — **DONE, 2026-08-11**
**Objective:** `write_env()` in `scripts/setup.sh` now generates and writes `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY`, `GATEWAY_TOKEN_REVOKE_SERVICE_KEY`, and `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — all three referenced by both `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` (added by later work: B-002 Brief 1, B-042, B-022/B-044 respectively) but never written by `setup.sh`, which predates them. Re-confirmed the gap directly against `write_env()` before building, per the task brief's own instruction.
**Fix:** all 3 generated via the existing `generate_secret` (`openssl rand -hex 32`), identical to `SERVICE_KEY`/`COLLECTOR_API_KEY`; `write_env()` gained 3 new positional params, written into the `.env`'s `eami-gateway` section (one entry each — both compose files reference these exact names from both `eami-gateway`'s and `eami-api`'s env blocks, so no second differently-named variable is needed the way `SERVICE_KEY`/`GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY` required). Investigated sharing this logic with `appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh` (B-053's own independent duplicate secret generator) — decided against it: editing `eami-stack.sh` was outside this task's scope, and duplicating small security-relevant generation helpers matches this repo's existing B-025 precedent over building shared infra for it. `eami-stack.sh`, `docker-compose*.yml`, and app code untouched.
- [x] `write_env()` generates and writes all 3 missing vars (reused `generate_secret`/hex-32, matching `SERVICE_KEY`/`COLLECTOR_API_KEY`)
- [x] Verified against a real `docker compose up` (isolated throwaway `-p eami-b054-test` project, fresh Postgres, real rebuilt images) that all 3 features work with a freshly-generated `.env`, not just that the vars are non-empty: `GET /v1/gateway/episodes` returned `200` with the real generated episode-read key and `401` with a wrong one; `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{jti}/revoke` returned `400` (past auth) with the real revoke key and `401` with a wrong one; gateway startup log confirmed `"tool router ready","credentials_configured":true` for the encryption key. Throwaway stack/volumes fully torn down afterward.
**Dependencies:** none. **Severity:** was Medium (silent partial breakage for anyone running `setup.sh`, fails closed per each feature's own contract but with no clear signal why); now closed. Full writeup, including an incidental real-dev-stack recovery this session's own live verification surfaced (pre-existing stale Docker Desktop state, unrelated to this fix, restored before finishing), in `BUILT.md`'s `Cross-cutting / shared` section.

### B-055 — First-boot web setup wizard (console-token gated) — **DONE, 2026-08-10**
**Objective:** Close B-053's own flagged gap — the appliance boots with an empty `orgs` table and no mechanism existed for the first visitor to safely claim admin. Built the wizard, gated by a one-time token displayed only on the VM's hypervisor console (matching how Forcepoint/One Identity/Veeam/Cisco ASAv/IBM handle this exact moment), not open network reachability.
**Resolution:** New `eami-api/internal/api/bootstrap.go` (`GET /v1/setup/status`, `POST /v1/setup/token/validate`, `POST /v1/setup/bootstrap`), new `setup_tokens` table (`schema/migrations-v2/000003`), new `eami-ui` `/setup` wizard page, new console-token-generation step in `appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh`. Concurrency guard is two real Postgres locks — a row lock (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, serializes two requests sharing the same token) plus a global `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (serializes *every* concurrent bootstrap attempt regardless of which token it holds — added after code review found the row-lock-only version didn't cover two different valid tokens racing, see below). Abandoned-session recovery is the same transaction's rollback semantics — an interrupted attempt never leaves the token spent or the system half-configured. Full design/verification detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api`/`eami-ui`/`appliance` sections.
**A real, disclosed deviation from B-053's own stated assumption:** B-053 assumed the wizard would read `/data/eami/state` directly from the host filesystem — not possible, since `docker-compose.prod.yml` stays frozen and nothing bind-mounts that path into any container. The real gate is DB-based (`SELECT count(*) FROM orgs`); the file is now informational-only, documented in `appliance/README.md`.
**Test coverage:** 9 new real-Postgres integration tests (`bootstrap_test.go`, each against its own throwaway database), including two concurrent-race tests (one shared token, and — added after the code-review finding below — two different valid tokens, 5 workers each, exactly 1 winner) and a real interrupted-transaction recovery test. **Verified 2026-08-10 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` clean across `eami-api`; real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean (Node/npm newly installed this session, previously absent).**
**Reviewer + security subagent passes: code review found a real Medium-severity gap, fixed and empirically proven, not just reasoned about.** The original row-lock-only concurrency guard didn't serialize two requests holding two *different* valid, unconsumed tokens — both could observe zero orgs and both insert one, contradicting this brief's own "enforced at the database level" requirement (the "only one live token at a time" invariant was shell-script-enforced, not DB-enforced). Fixed with the advisory lock above. Verified the bug was real before trusting the fix: disabled the new lock, ran the new different-tokens regression test 5 times, it failed 3/5; restored the fix, it passed reliably. Three smaller findings also fixed: a UI bug setting an error message on a form about to be unmounted; a server email regex stricter than the frontend's (rejected client-accepted addresses like `admin@localhost`); the UI forcing token re-entry on *any* bootstrap failure instead of only a dead-token/already-configured one. One disclosed, not-fixed limitation: the rate limiter's map never prunes empty keys (low severity, real fix needs a background sweep goroutine). Security review: no exploitable findings.
**Live-verified end-to-end, twice** against a real, isolated, genuinely-empty stack (not the shared dev DB — see B-056 below for why that DB's own org count was misleading): real console-style token generated via `eami-stack.sh`'s exact commands → validated → bootstrap succeeded (201) → **real login with the created credentials succeeded** (200, real JWT) → status flipped to configured → token reuse cleanly rejected (401). Repeated after rebuilding with the code-review fixes, this time with a dot-less-domain email to prove that fix live too. **Not performed this session:** a from-scratch Packer/QEMU appliance rebuild (B-053 already proved that mechanism separately; this brief's only change to it — the token-generation step — was exercised for real via its exact commands) and interactive browser click-through (browser automation unavailable this session).
**Post-push CI failure, found real and fixed (not a platform incident) — two distinct bugs, not one:** the first push (`95d26cf`) broke CI in two separate jobs. (1) `schema/migrationtest`'s `migrate_test.go` hardcoded an expected schema version of 2 in four places; B-055's new migration (000003) makes the real version 3 — a stale test expectation, not a migration-mechanism defect. Fixed all four checks, gave the fresh-vs-incremental-path comparison test a third `Steps(1)` call (it was silently missing `setup_tokens`, causing a table-count mismatch), and added `setup_tokens` to the fresh-schema spot-check list. (2) A **separate, unrelated-looking bug** in the *new* `bootstrap_test.go` itself: its `bootstrapTestPgConn` copied `migrate_test.go`'s own narrower env-var pattern (only checks `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, reads the password from `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` separately) — but CI's matrix `test` job (which runs `eami-api`) only ever sets `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, never `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (that's only additionally provisioned for the standalone `test-migrationtest` job, and `.github/workflows/build.yml` already has a comment explaining exactly why, predating this session). Fixed by parsing `TEST_DATABASE_URL` as a full DSN when set, matching every other `eami-api` real-Postgres test file's existing convention. Confirmed both root causes independently by reading the actual CI job logs (not assumed) before fixing either. Both fixes verified locally reproducing CI's exact env-var setup (`TEST_DATABASE_URL` only, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` unset) before the second push (`39ee334`) — **confirmed genuinely green via the GitHub API, zero failed jobs, Docker image builds (gated on both test jobs) also succeeded.**
**Dependencies:** B-053 (done). **Severity:** was High (no race-safe first-admin claim mechanism existed at all); now closed.

### B-056 — perf-seed tests leak synthetic orgs into the shared dev database — **DONE, 2026-08-11**
**Objective:** the `paste-events-perf-a`/`paste-events-perf-b` orgs (and their 100,200 child rows each) should never land in the shared dev database at all, closing both the root-cause bug and the underlying architectural gap.
**Root cause, confirmed empirically, not the file originally named:** this entry's original filing blamed `seedPasteEventsPerfData` (`paste_events_read_test.go:362`) — that function was tested in isolation and does **not** leak (correctly uses `t.Cleanup(pool.Close)`). The actual, 100%-reproducible bug is in `paste_events_test.go`'s `TestPasteEvents_ReportingQuery_UsesIndex_AtRealisticVolume` (B-032, 2026-07-25 — matching both the leaked-org naming with no `-read-` infix and the leak's own dated history exactly): `defer pool.Close()` (a plain Go defer, scoped to the test function) closes the pool when the function returns; `t.Cleanup`-registered callbacks are a *separate, later* teardown phase that runs only after the function body (and its defers) has already fully unwound — so the registered `DELETE FROM orgs` ran against an already-closed pool, erroring immediately, with the error silently discarded (`_, _ = pool.Exec(...)`). Proven directly: ran the test in total isolation (no other tests, no timeout, no interruption) — clean `PASS`, exit 0 — and it still leaked both orgs every time; the identical manual `DELETE` via `psql` succeeded instantly with a clean cascade to 0 child rows, ruling out any FK/cascade problem. A repo-wide grep confirmed no other real-Postgres test file mixes `defer pool.Close()` with `t.Cleanup`-registered row deletes — this was an isolated, one-function bug, not a systemic pattern.
**Fix:** both `TestPasteEvents_ReportingQuery_UsesIndex_AtRealisticVolume` (`paste_events_test.go`) and `seedPasteEventsPerfData` (`paste_events_read_test.go`, converted too even though it wasn't buggy, since seeding 200,000 synthetic rows into the shared dev DB at all is the underlying problem B-056 actually named) now create their own throwaway database per run — reusing `bootstrap_test.go`'s already-established `bootstrapTestPgConn`/`newThrowawayDB`/`applyMigrations` helpers directly (same package, zero duplication) instead of connecting to the shared dev DB via `pasteEventsTestDSN`. The whole throwaway database (and everything seeded into it) is dropped by `newThrowawayDB`'s own `t.Cleanup` — the per-org `t.Cleanup` DELETE calls are gone entirely, removing the exact class of ordering bug, not just this one instance of it.
**Live-verified, including the two scenarios explicitly asked for:**
- Normal run: all 3 affected tests pass, shared dev DB unchanged (still only `Dev Org`), zero leftover throwaway databases.
- **Simulated failed run** (forced `t.Fatal` injected after seeding, then reverted): test fails as expected, but cleanup still ran — zero leak in the shared DB, zero leftover throwaway database.
