Confirms Go's `t.Fatal`→`runtime.Goexit` semantics still run registered cleanups, same guarantee `bootstrap_test.go`'s pattern already relies on.
- **Simulated interrupted run** (compiled the test binary, hard-killed it via `taskkill /F` mid-seed, twice — once during migrations, once with one org's 100,200 rows already inserted): in both cases the shared dev database's `orgs` table stayed untouched (still only `Dev Org`) — the one remaining residual is the throwaway database itself being left behind, which no cleanup mechanism in any language can prevent after a hard kill. This is the same already-accepted residual `bootstrap_test.go`'s own pattern has always carried, not a new gap; the leaked throwaway DB was uniquely named, trivially identifiable, and manually dropped after inspection.
- `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all of `eami-api`, no regressions.
**Dependencies:** none. **Severity:** was Low (cosmetic/DB-hygiene, no functional or security impact); now closed.

### B-057 — AI Provider Connector (Thread A Model 1): `ai_provider` gateway_tools type, dispatching to Claude as a named tool — **DONE, 2026-08-11**
**Objective:** add a real, multi-provider-ready `Adapter` interface to `eami-gateway` (Claude as its first complete implementation, not a special case), so an agent can call `claude/messages` as a named tool through the real dispatch path, reusing MCP's existing async 202+SSE pattern, non-streaming. Also architecturally unblocks prompt redaction/tokenization (the gateway had no visibility into an agent's outbound LLM call before this).
**New:** `eami-gateway/internal/aiprovider` package (`Adapter`, `Router`, `ClaudeAdapter`); `schema/migrations-v2/000004` (`gateway_tools.type` widened, new `provider`/`audit_mode` columns); `cmd/gateway/main.go` wiring (additive, B-044/B-046's `rest_api` logic untouched); `eami-api` CRUD/validation for `provider`/`audit_mode`; `eami-ui` Tools UI additions (Provider + Audit-mode selectors, ready for a second provider without rework).
**Two scope expansions, both explicitly authorized mid-brief, both matching B-039's own precedent for "a real gap found during this task's own investigation that makes an acceptance criterion unsatisfiable without the fix":**
- **Resume-routing fix:** live verification found `approval.Router`'s resume logic was hardcoded to the static proxy, with zero awareness of `toolRouter`/`aiProviderRouter` — meaning an approved escalation for *any* dynamically-routed tool, `rest_api` (B-044) included, never actually reached its real destination. Not new — B-044's own session had already found and explicitly disclosed this exact gap without fixing it. Closed for both types at once via a new `dispatchApproved` method, scoped to the resume dispatch path only.
- **TOCTOU/destination-integrity fix, judged more serious than the routing bug (a bypassable safety guarantee, not a broken feature):** the resume-routing fix's own re-resolve-by-name design meant a lower-privileged `admin`/`operator` role — invisible to the approver, who sees only agent/tool/action/justification/risk, never `base_url`/`provider`/credentials — could edit the escalated connector during the hold window and silently redirect the approved call. Fixed by pinning the resolved connector's ID + a config hash (type + base_url-or-provider + encrypted credential bytes + `action_paths`) at escalation time (`schema/migrations-v2/000005`, new `approval_requests` columns), verifying both unchanged at resume, and **failing closed** — refusing to dispatch, recording a new `resume_outcome` — if either changed. The `action_paths` component of the hash was itself a real gap this fix's own two mandatory security-review rounds independently found and closed in the same pass (action_paths alone, base_url/credentials untouched, fully determines a rest_api tool's real destination).
**Test coverage:** new `aiprovider` package tests (Resolve/Dispatch/ClaudeAdapter, real Postgres + `httptest`); `main_ai_provider_pg_test.go`; `eami-api`'s `tools_ai_provider_test.go`; `approval/router_dispatch_test.go` — the TOCTOU fail-closed behavior proven via **real mid-hold Postgres mutations** (real `UPDATE`/`DELETE` against `gateway_tools` while an approval sits pending, not mocks). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across `eami-gateway`/`eami-api`/`schema/migrationtest`, real `npm run build` clean for `eami-ui` — no regressions throughout.
**Reviewer + security passes:** three full mandatory rounds (base feature, resume-routing fix, TOCTOU fix), each finding real distinct issues before the next came back clean — full detail in `BUILT.md`.
**Live-verified end-to-end** against an isolated `docker-compose` stack with a real Anthropic API key: a real Claude response through direct dispatch (AC1), a full real escalate→approve→resume cycle returning a real Claude response (AC4, the actual resume working — not partial), and the fail-closed behavior proven live via a real `PATCH` mid-hold followed by a refused resume (`resume_outcome='config_changed'`) — not just reasoned about. No credential leakage in any real log across the session (AC6). Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `Cross-cutting / shared` section.
**Dependencies:** B-044 (rest_api dynamic dispatch, reused), B-022 (credential cipher, reused), B-039 (approval mechanism, extended not replaced). **Out of scope, as specified:** streaming, Gemini/OpenAI adapters themselves, redaction/tokenization, transparent SDK passthrough.

### B-058 — Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 1: schema + CRUD foundation — **DONE, 2026-08-12**
**Objective:** the foundational data model and CRUD API for admin-defined multi-hop workflows (an ordered chain of `gateway_tools` connectors) — schema and CRUD only, no execution, no output→input mapping, no approval integration. First of the Thread B epic's estimated 6-8 briefs (see that QUEUED entry above).
**New:** `schema/migrations-v2/000006` (`workflows`/`workflow_steps` tables — `workflow_steps.gateway_tool_id UUID REFERENCES gateway_tools(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`, mirroring `approval_requests.resolved_tool_id`'s exact migration-000005 precedent); `eami-api/internal/api/workflows.go` (List/Get/Create/Update/Delete, `requireRole` gating identical to `gateway_tools`); `eami-api/internal/store/workflows.sql.go` (hand-written, sqlc CLI confirmed unavailable on this machine — same reason `tools.sql.go` is hand-written; a real `.sql` source file added at `internal/store/query/workflows.sql` for future regeneration); `eami-ui/src/hooks/useWorkflows.ts` + `src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx` (mirrors `ToolsPage.tsx`'s drawer pattern, per the Thread B investigation's own UI recommendation).
**Design choice — deleted/edited connector reference:** `ON DELETE SET NULL`, not a blocking pre-check, specifically because `gateway_tools`/`tools.go`'s `DeleteTool` was out of scope to modify this brief (deletes unconditionally today) — a plain `RESTRICT`-default FK would have turned that already-shipped, untouched delete flow into a raw constraint-violation the moment any workflow referenced the tool being deleted. A step whose connector is deleted comes back from `GetWorkflow`/`ListWorkflowSteps` with `gateway_tool_id`/`tool_name` both null — visibly flagged in the UI (red warning icon, broken dropdown state), not silently dropped or 500'd. Whether a workflow with a dangling step can execute is explicitly left to Brief 4 (execution) — nothing executes yet.
**Cross-org guard:** `ToolBelongsToOrg` — the FK constraint alone only proves a `gateway_tool_id` exists *somewhere*, never that it belongs to the caller's org; `validateWorkflowSteps` checks this explicitly for every step on both Create and Update, before any row is written.
**Test coverage:** `eami-api/internal/api/workflows_test.go` — 4 real-Postgres integration tests (full CRUD lifecycle incl. reorder/add/remove via full-replace semantics; cross-org isolation, mirroring B-042's proof style — Get/Update/Delete all return not-found/no-op for another org's workflow, plus a real HTTP-level test proving `CreateWorkflow` rejects a step referencing another org's real connector; dangling-reference behavior after a real `DeleteTool` call) + `workflows_internal_test.go` (3 unit tests for the FK-violation-detection helper added by code review, below). **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module; real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean for `eami-ui`.**
**A real test-authoring bug found and fixed live, not just in application code:** the test file's first version used `defer pool.Close()` alongside `t.Cleanup`-registered org deletes — **the exact anti-pattern `BACKLOG.md`'s own B-056 entry documents** (`t.Cleanup` runs in a phase strictly after a function's own `defer`s, so the pool closed before any cleanup DELETE could run). Reproduced live: a full test run left 14 leaked `wf-*` orgs in the shared dev database, confirmed, then fixed by switching to `t.Cleanup(func() { pool.Close() })` registered before any org-seeding calls (so LIFO cleanup ordering runs the deletes first) — re-ran and confirmed zero leakage, dev DB back to just `Dev Org` throughout the rest of the session.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security review (this session, direct trace-through — org-isolation is always security-relevant per standing rules): traced every query's org-scoping, the cross-org guard, the pre-mutation ownership check in `UpdateWorkflow`, and confirmed no SQL injection surface (all parameterized) and no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in the new frontend code — zero findings. **Code review found 4 real issues, plus one real doc/BACKLOG.md consistency gap, all fixed:** (1) `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s step-row editor silently dropped any incomplete row (including one flagged `toolDeleted`, i.e. exactly the row AC4's recovery flow depends on an admin fixing) on submit if the admin edited an unrelated field — fixed to block submission with a clear "Step N is incomplete" error instead, never silently discarding a row. (2) This file's own Thread B entry said "NOT YET SCOPED, no B-ID assigned" in the same diff that shipped a fully-built B-058 — fixed (see the updated entry above). (3) `WorkflowUpdateRequest`'s doc comment incorrectly implied `{"steps": []}` was a supported way to clear a workflow to zero steps; `validateWorkflowSteps` actually rejects empty arrays (a workflow needs ≥1 step to be a meaningful chain) — comment corrected, no behavior change. (4) A real if narrow TOCTOU: `validateWorkflowSteps`' `ToolBelongsToOrg` check runs before the transaction opens, so a connector deleted by a concurrent request in the gap between validation and the transactional `INSERT` surfaced as a raw 500 (FK violation) instead of a clean error — fixed with a new `isForeignKeyViolation` helper (SQLSTATE `23503` check, mirroring `tool_connectivity.go`'s existing `classifyPgConnectError` convention) translating it to a clean 400 on both `CreateWorkflow` and `UpdateWorkflow`. (5) `UpdateWorkflow` discarded the row `RETURNING`ed by its own `UPDATE` and then re-fetched the same data via a second `GetWorkflow` after commit — fixed to reuse the already-fetched row, cutting one redundant DB round-trip per successful `PATCH`. One finding (`AddWorkflowPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` are near-duplicate components) left as a disclosed, not-fixed style note — matches this codebase's own existing `AddToolPanel`/`EditToolPanel` precedent, not a new departure.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (both before and after the code-review fixes):** a real workflow created with 2 real steps (`Test`/`b046-live-verify` connectors) via a real admin JWT, confirmed via List/Get; edited live (renamed, activated, reordered, and a 3rd step added referencing the real `claude` `ai_provider` connector — proving both connector types work as workflow steps); deleted, confirmed 404 + cascade-deleted steps at the DB level. Post-fix smoke test confirmed both new validation paths return clean `400`s (`"at least one step is required"`, the cross-org/not-found guard), not 500s. Confirmed via direct grep that `eami-gateway`'s dispatch path (`cmd/gateway/main.go` and the whole module) has **zero references to `workflow`/`workflows`** anywhere — this brief is genuinely definition-only, nothing executes. `dev@example.com`'s password was reset to a known value to obtain a login token for this verification (no other credential was available, same disclosed precedent as B-045/B-046/B-054) — the user may want to reset it again.
**Dependencies:** none (Brief 1 has no dependency on later Thread B briefs). **Out of scope, as specified:** workflow execution, output→input mapping logic (the `input_mapping JSONB` column ships as an inert placeholder only), per-hop TOCTOU pinning, escalation/approval integration, audit logging integration, branching/conditional logic.
**Post-push CI failure, real and fixed — the same recurring bug class as B-055's own post-push CI failure, not a new one:** `schema/migrationtest/migrate_test.go` hardcoded an expected schema version of 5 in three places, plus a fixed 5-call `Steps(1)` loop in the fresh-vs-incremental comparison test — B-058's migration `000006` makes the real version 6, breaking `Test — schema/migrationtest` in CI. Fixed by deriving the expected version dynamically from the actual `*.up.sql` file count in `schema/migrations-v2` (new `latestMigrationVersion(t)` helper) instead of a hardcoded number, closing the whole class of recurrence rather than just this instance (this is the second time a hardcoded migration-version expectation has broken CI for the identical preventable reason — B-055 first, now this). Verified locally with `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` set, matching the `test-migrationtest` CI job's exact env setup (confirmed by reading `build.yml` directly, not assumed) — all 4 tests pass, correctly deriving version 6. Pushed (`bde66eb`), confirmed genuinely green via the GitHub API afterward — every test job (`Test — eami-api`, `eami-gateway`, `eami-policy`, `schema/migrationtest`, `eami-agent`, `eami-collector`) reported `conclusion: success`.

### B-059 — Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 2: execution engine, per-hop TOCTOU pinning, static params — **DONE, 2026-08-12**
**Objective:** make a B-058-defined workflow actually run — each step dispatched through the exact same `dispatch()`/policy/audit path a standalone MCP call uses, with TOCTOU protection pinned per-hop, static (not mapped) per-step parameters. Output→input mapping and durable escalation pause/resume explicitly deferred to later briefs.
**New:** `schema/migrations-v2/000007` (`workflow_step_params`, `workflow_runs`, `workflow_run_steps` — all `ON DELETE SET NULL`/`CASCADE` mirroring `approval_requests`' established precedent); `eami-api/internal/api/workflow_step_params.go` + `internal/store/workflow_step_params.sql.go` (new file, zero changes to `workflows.go`); `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/` (new package: `resolve.go`, `connector.go`, `executor.go`, `http.go`); `cmd/gateway/main.go` wiring (`POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}/run`, agent-JWT authenticated, synchronous).
**Per-hop TOCTOU pinning — the brief's own central design question, resolved by inspection, not by building something new:** calling the existing `dispatch()` closure once per step (unmodified) already gives per-hop pinning for free — each call independently re-resolves and re-pins its own step's connector immediately before evaluating policy for it, so there's no shared "resolve everything upfront" phase for staleness to hide in. Upfront pinning (resolving every step's connector once at workflow start) was considered and rejected: it's strictly weaker (a later step's connector could still be edited during an earlier step's escalation hold, undetected, without re-verifying at dispatch time anyway — which is just per-hop pinning with an extra useless snapshot).
**Escalation mid-chain reuses `Hold()` unchanged, automatically** — calling `dispatch()` per step means an escalating step transparently blocks inside `Submit()`+`Hold()` exactly as a standalone call would; no special-casing needed. Documented explicitly as this brief's accepted interim behavior (whole run blocks on one hold, no durable persistence across a process restart) — a real durable pause/resume mechanism is later-brief scope.
**Static params, kept out of B-058's schema entirely:** new `workflow_step_params` table (not a `workflow_steps` column) — `workflow_steps.input_mapping` stays untouched, reserved for the later output→input-mapping brief, avoiding a collision between "admin-typed constant" and "derived-from-prior-step" concepts that will likely need to coexist per step later.
**Test dispatch harness, a real disclosed simplification:** `dispatch()` itself is an unexported closure in `main.go`'s `run()`, not testable directly without refactoring it — exactly the kind of change to B-057's logic this brief must not make. `eami-gateway/internal/workflow`'s tests reconstruct dispatch()'s real shape (real `policy.Evaluator`, real `audit.Writer`, real `episode.Recorder`, real `approval.Router` with real `Submit`/`Hold`/LISTEN-NOTIFY, real `aiprovider.Router`) using only exported constructors — the same real pipeline, not a mock. Test connectors are `ai_provider` (fake `Adapter`, mirroring `approval/router_dispatch_test.go`'s own `fakeProviderAdapter` pattern) rather than `rest_api`, specifically because `toolrouter`'s real, correct SSRF guard rejects any local `httptest.Server` (loopback) — found live when the first test version failed with exactly that rejection; matches `router_dispatch_test.go`'s own established precedent of using `ai_provider` for its "genuinely dispatches" proofs for the identical reason.
**Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/*_test.go` — 10 real-Postgres integration tests covering all 6 ACs (full multi-step execution; middle-step deny stops the run, later steps never execute; escalate→approve→resume and escalate→deny→stop, both via real `Hold()`/real `pg_notify`; two distinct TOCTOU scenarios — non-escalating edit-between-steps correctly picked up [not a vulnerability, proves no upfront caching] vs. the real attack: escalating step's connector edited mid-hold, resume fails closed via B-057's unmodified mechanism; `workflow_run_steps` audit-trail accuracy; no output→input mapping, proven by a marker-leak check). Plus 3 `eami-api` tests for the new params endpoint (round-trip, cross-org rejection, non-object-body rejection). **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`.**
**A real test-authoring bug reproduced a THIRD time this session (same B-056-class `t.Cleanup`-after-`defer pool.Close()` ordering bug already fixed twice in B-058's own session) — found and fixed again, in the new `eami-api` params test file specifically:** confirmed via a real leaked-org check, fixed identically (`t.Cleanup(func(){ pool.Close() })` registered before any org-seeding call), re-verified clean.
**Reviewer + security passes: code-review subagent failed on a platform session-limit outage mid-task (not a finding — matches B-046's own precedent for this exact class of tooling failure) — substituted with a direct manual review, disclosed not glossed over.** That manual pass found and fixed one real bug: `workflow/http.go`'s `HandleRun` passed `r.Context()` directly into `Executor.Run`, so a client disconnect during a step's `Hold()` (reproduced live, organically, during this session's own testing via a killed `curl`) canceled the context every subsequent DB write depended on — including the final run-status UPDATE — silently leaving `workflow_runs.status` stuck at `'running'` forever. This is a direct analog of the exact bug B-039 already found and fixed for the MCP async path; fixed identically with `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())`. Security review (this session, direct adversarial trace-through specifically targeting the brief's own stated bar — "the same rigor as B-057's original adversarial proof"): confirmed the workflow runner's own pre-dispatch connector read/hash computation is purely informational and can never be substituted into or weaken `dispatch()`'s own independent enforcement; confirmed cross-org scoping holds throughout (workflow resolution is scoped to the authenticated agent's own resolved org, never client input); zero exploitable findings.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before and after the context-cancellation fix):** a real 2-step workflow (allow → escalate) executed via a real agent JWT against `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run` — step 1 genuinely dispatched to a real external endpoint with its own static params; step 2 genuinely blocked the HTTP request on a real pending `approval_requests` row. **The real TOCTOU attack**: while step 2's approval sat pending, its connector's `base_url` was edited via a real `PATCH /v1/gateway/tools/{id}` call, then approved — resume correctly refused (`resume_outcome='config_changed'`, step `outcome='blocked'`, run `status='failed'`), proving B-057's unmodified mechanism engages correctly for a workflow-originated escalation. A second run without tampering completed successfully end-to-end (`status='completed'`, both steps `allowed`, step 2's real dispatch carrying its own distinct static params). **The context-cancellation bug reproduced and fixed live, before/after**: killed the triggering `curl` mid-hold, confirmed the run stuck at `'running'` forever (pre-fix); after the fix, repeated the identical kill-mid-hold scenario and confirmed the run correctly reached `'completed'` once the approval was later decided, even with the original client long gone.
**Dependencies:** B-058 (workflow definitions, extended not modified), B-057 (TOCTOU pinning mechanism, reused unmodified), B-039 (`Hold()` mechanism, reused unmodified). **Out of scope, as specified:** output→input mapping, durable pause/resume for escalation, chain-aware approval UI, any UI for triggering/viewing runs (verification used direct API calls), branching/conditional logic.

### B-060 — Workflows UI: real action picker (replace free-text field) — **DONE, 2026-08-12**
**Objective:** the Workflows step editor's "action" field was free text with zero validation — an admin could type a value with no corresponding real action on the selected connector, which would only fail at execution time (B-059). Per the MCP Discovery investigation's D8 finding, fixed with data already available to the frontend — no new backend endpoint. **Explicitly adjacent to Thread B (feeds its UX), not itself part of Thread B's epic** — per the investigation's own distinction between the workflow-chaining epic and MCP-protocol/connector-definition work that happens to feed it.
**Only file changed:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`. Re-verified D8 directly before building: `useTools.ts`'s `ToolWithActions.action_paths?: Record<string, ActionPathMapping>` (B-046) was already fetched by `StepsEditor`'s existing `useTools()` call — confirmed, not assumed. `tools` was retyped from `any` to `ToolWithActions[]`, mirroring `ToolsPage.tsx`'s own identical existing pattern. Per row: looks up the selected connector, and if its `action_paths` has ≥1 key, renders a real `<select>` of those action names instead of the free-text `<input>`; a connector with none (`ai_provider`/`mcp`/`database`, or a `rest_api` tool with no mappings defined yet) falls back to free text completely unchanged — zero special-casing needed, since `action_paths` is a `rest_api`-only concept by construction (B-046). A saved-but-now-unrecognized action value (its mapping was removed from the connector after the workflow was saved) is appended as an extra dropdown option rather than dropped, so loading a workflow never silently mutates its saved data. The connector `<select>`'s existing `onChange` now unconditionally resets `action: ''` in the same state update — a single, unbranched code path, so there is no scenario where a stale action from a previously selected connector survives a switch.
**Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain:** `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean, zero errors.
**Live-verified against the real running stack** (no interactive browser click-through — same standing limitation as every other `eami-ui` brief in this environment, disclosed not glossed over; this machine's documented Chrome/Edge automation incident risk, `CONTEXT.md`, made this the correct call again): the real `b059-live-verify` workflow's steps were switched to `b046-live-verify` (real `action_paths`: `echo_get`/`echo_post` — confirmed via a real `GET /v1/gateway/tools`) and `claude` (`ai_provider`, no `action_paths`) via real `PATCH`/`GET` calls exercising the identical save-and-reload data contract the UI's `handleSubmit`→`update.mutateAsync`→`useWorkflow` refetch goes through — a dropdown-selected action (`echo_get`) and a free-text action (`messages`) both persisted and reloaded correctly, byte-identical, proving the backend can't and doesn't distinguish which UI control produced the value (AC1/AC2/AC3/AC5). The connector-switch reset (AC4) was verified by direct inspection of the committed `onChange` handler rather than a browser trace, disclosed as such — it's a single unconditional state update, not a claim requiring a UI click to falsify.
**Dependencies:** B-046 (`action_paths`, read-only reuse), the MCP Discovery investigation (D8). **Out of scope, as specified:** any backend change, real MCP `tools/list` exposure (separate, later brief), parameter-shape validation beyond picking a known action name.

### B-061 — MCP Discovery: real `tools/list` support — **DONE, 2026-08-12**
**Objective:** `eami-gateway`'s MCP transport previously implemented only the non-standard `tool_call` method — any other JSON-RPC method, including the real spec's `tools/list`, got a `-32601 method not found`, meaning a connecting agent had to already know exact tool/action names in advance (a standing assumption since B-044). Per the MCP Discovery investigation's Part A/B findings, this brief adds real `tools/list` so an agent can discover its own org's actual `rest_api` connectors and their known actions. **Explicitly MCP-protocol-completeness work adjacent to Thread B, not part of Thread B's own multi-hop-workflow epic** — same category distinction B-060's entry already draws.
**New:** `internal/mcp/handler.go` — `ToolDefinition{Name, Description, InputSchema}` (matches the real spec's shape), `ListToolsHandler` (a 4th callback-injection parameter on `NewHandler`, mirroring `DecisionHandler`'s existing DB-agnostic pattern), `toolsListResult{Tools, NextCursor}` (`NextCursor` deliberately never populated — no real pagination implemented, `omitempty` is honest, a fabricated cursor would not be). `ServeMessages` gained an early `if req.Method == "tools/list"` branch dispatching to new `serveToolsList`; the existing `tool_call` code below it is untouched, byte-for-byte (confirmed by direct diff review). `cmd/gateway/main.go` — new `listGatewayTools(ctx, pool, orgID)`, mirroring `resolveDynamicTool`'s exact org-scoping shape: `SELECT name, action_paths FROM gateway_tools WHERE org_id=$1 AND type='rest_api'`, one `ToolDefinition` per action key (`Name = "<connector>/<action>"`, matching `splitToolAction`'s primary separator; `Description` synthesized from the real stored method+path, e.g. `GET /get on connector "b046-live-verify"` — real data, never fabricated; `InputSchema = {"type":"object"}`, the honest generic shape since `action_paths` has no parameter schema).
**Design decision, made explicit up front and held throughout:** only `type='rest_api'` rows are eligible at all — `ai_provider` connectors dispatch via a completely different mechanism with no `action_paths` concept (representing one as "zero actions" would be misleading, not honestly empty), and `mcp`/`database` types have no working direct-dispatch path in `main.go` today. A `rest_api` row with no `action_paths` set (B-044's flat-POST fallback) naturally contributes zero entries too — not a separate exclusion rule, just the consequence of building the list from `action_paths` keys.
**`context.WithoutCancel` applied correctly:** `serveToolsList` writes 202 immediately, then wraps `r.Context()` before the goroutine that calls `h.listTools` — same B-039 reasoning as `tool_call`'s identical pattern (`net/http` cancels `r.Context()` the instant `ServeHTTP` returns, before an async goroutine gets a real chance to run).
**Test coverage:** `cmd/gateway/main_tools_list_pg_test.go` — 5 real-Postgres unit-shape tests on `listGatewayTools` directly (multi-action tool → one definition per action with real method/path in the description; cross-org isolation; `mcp`/`database`/`ai_provider` types all contribute zero regardless of scope; a `rest_api` tool with no `action_paths` contributes zero; empty orgID short-circuits with no query). Plus 2 new real end-to-end SSE tests — genuinely new test infrastructure (`readSSEFrame`, no existing test in this module opened a live SSE stream before): a real agent JWT opens a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse`, POSTs a real `tools/list`, and reads back the real `message` SSE event, asserting the exact `{"tools":[{"name","description","inputSchema"}]}` shape a real MCP client library would parse, built from a real seeded connector with a real `ai_provider` row proven absent from the result; and a `tool_call` regression test over the same live harness, proving the new branch didn't disturb the existing path. **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module** (14 packages, not just the new file).
**Reviewer + security passes, both clean, zero findings.** Code review (background subagent) confirmed the `tool_call` path is genuinely byte-for-byte unmodified (direct diff trace), the `context.WithoutCancel` convention is followed correctly, and the mandatory `t.Cleanup(pool.Close)`-before-any-DB-cleanup ordering convention is followed correctly in every new test helper (no recurrence of the B-056-class bug) — two nitpicks noted and left as-is (non-deterministic map-iteration order in the returned tool list, harmless for `tools/list` semantics; a pre-existing, non-regression nil-handler-panic pattern shared with `dispatch`). Security review (background subagent, direct trace-through) confirmed `orgID` is always read from `sess.Agent.OrgID` — populated at session creation from the registry-resolved, JWT-validated agent, never client-supplied on the `tools/list` request itself; confirmed the query is fully parameterized (no SQL injection surface); confirmed no credential material (`credentials_encrypted`, `base_url`) is ever read into a `ToolDefinition`; confirmed the synthesized `Description` string is JSON/SSE-safe via `encoding/json`'s own escaping. Zero exploitable findings from either pass.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh:** a real agent JWT issued via `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`, a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse` opened, a real `tools/list` POSTed and its real `message` SSE event read back — result showed exactly the two real `b046-live-verify` actions (`echo_get`, `echo_post`) with their real method/path in each description; the org's real `claude` `ai_provider` connector and the two `rest_api` tools with no `action_paths` configured (`manual_verify_tool`, `Test`) correctly did not appear. A follow-up real `tool_call` to `b046-live-verify/echo_get` over the same live transport still dispatched correctly (real response from the connector's real downstream endpoint), confirming no regression outside the automated test suite too.
**Dependencies:** B-044 (`gateway_tools`/dynamic routing), B-046 (`action_paths`), the MCP Discovery investigation (Parts A/B). **Out of scope, as specified:** `ai_provider`/`mcp`/`database` tool discovery (a natural, separate future brief), real pagination, parameter-schema richness beyond the generic `{"type":"object"}`, any client-side (agent-facing) consumption of the new capability.

### B-062 — Fix: Vite dev server serves stale code after edits (bind-mount watch failure) — **DONE, 2026-08-12**
**Objective:** User reported B-060's real action-picker dropdown wasn't rendering in the browser for `b046-live-verify` (a connector with real `action_paths`) — confirmed via screenshot, still showing free text. Explicitly asked to rule out a stale Docker image first (same class as B-035/B-055) before assuming a code bug.
**Investigation, in order:** (1) Compared the running `eaim-eami-ui-1` image's build/creation timestamp against B-060's commit (`955bb5e`, 16:39:56 IST) — the image *was* stale (built 10:48 IST, ~6 hours earlier), so rebuilt it. (2) But `docker-compose.yml`'s `eami-ui` service bind-mounts `./eami-ui/src:/app/src` for hot reload — confirmed via `md5sum` that the container's `/app/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx` was already byte-identical to the host file (and to B-060's committed code) *before* the rebuild, meaning image staleness could not actually be the cause for this specific bug (the rebuild was real but a no-op for this symptom). (3) Fetched the Vite dev server's actually-served module directly (`curl http://127.0.0.1:5173/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`) and found it did **not** contain B-060's dropdown logic (`actionKeys`, `Select an action...`) at all — the dev server was serving a stale in-memory module despite the on-disk file (inside and outside the container) being current. **Real root cause**: Vite's default `chokidar` file watcher relies on native FS change events, which don't reliably propagate across Docker Desktop's Windows-host-to-Linux-container bind-mount boundary — so the dev server's module cache never invalidated after B-060 (or any subsequent edit) was written, and kept serving the pre-B-060 build indefinitely until something forced a fresh process start.
**Fix:** `eami-ui/vite.config.ts` — added `server.watch.usePolling: true` (300ms interval) so Vite periodically re-stats watched files instead of relying on FS events, closing the gap regardless of host OS/filesystem-bridge behavior. `vite.config.ts` isn't inside the bind-mounted `src/` path, so the image needed a real rebuild for this specific change to take effect (confirmed, unlike the `.tsx` symptom above).
**Verified live, not just asserted:** restarting the container alone (before the fix) immediately started serving B-060's real code — confirming the "stale in-memory dev-server cache" diagnosis, not a leftover code bug. After rebuilding with the polling fix, made a real, additional trivial edit to `WorkflowsPage.tsx` (a marker string) and confirmed via a direct curl to the dev server that the change was picked up and served within ~2s **with no container restart at all** — proving the fix actually closes the recurrence risk, not just papering over this one instance. Marker edit reverted afterward; confirmed zero residual diff on `WorkflowsPage.tsx`. `npm run type-check` clean.
**Known limitation:** polling at 300ms adds a small constant CPU/disk-stat cost to the dev container — acceptable for a local dev-only server (`target: development`, never used in production builds, which don't run a dev server or watch files at all).

### B-063 — Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 3: output→input mapping — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** a workflow step's parameters were only static, admin-typed values (B-059) — this brief lets a later step's parameter be extracted from an EARLIER step's real recorded execution result within the same run (the Freshservice JSON-Parser-node pattern), the piece that makes Thread B a genuine data pipeline instead of a sequence of unconnected calls.
**Confirmed against current code before building, per the kickoff line's own instruction, not assumed:** the MCP Discovery investigation's D9 finding ("no new table needed") was re-verified directly against `schema/migrations-v2/000006`/`000007` and found to be an even stronger confirmation than expected — **zero new tables, zero new columns, zero new migration at all**: `workflow_steps.input_mapping JSONB` already existed (B-058 reserved it explicitly for this brief) and `workflow_run_steps.result JSONB` already recorded every step's real dispatched response (B-059, for audit). Expression library: `github.com/tidwall/gjson` (MIT, widely used, purely read-only JSON-path querying — no eval/code-execution capability, "do not hand-roll" satisfied).
**CRITICAL FINDING, surfaced in the plan before writing any code, changed the file scope from what the brief itself assumed:** re-verifying "reference prior steps by their stable ID" against the actual current code (not just the schema) found `workflow_steps.id` was NOT actually stable across a save — `eami-api/internal/api/workflows.go`'s `UpdateWorkflow` unconditionally deleted every `workflow_steps` row and reinserted fresh ones (brand-new UUIDs, `input_mapping` never even passed through) whenever a request included a `steps` array, which the current UI does on every edit-panel submit — a rename, a reorder, anything. This meant extraction wiring would be **wiped by the very next unrelated save**, not just "reorder might misdirect it" (the brief's own AC2 framing) — worse than anticipated, and not achievable within the brief's stated `MUST NOT MODIFY: B-058's core CRUD` boundary as literally written. **Presented to the user as a genuine scope-boundary judgment call rather than decided unilaterally** (same category as B-044/B-059's own presented forks) — approved resolution: a minimal, additive touch to `workflows.go`/`workflows.sql.go` (below), preserving 100% of existing behavior for every caller that doesn't use the new field.
**New/changed — `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/`:** `types.go` — `ExtractionRef{FromStep, Path}`, `Step.InputMapping map[string]ExtractionRef`. `resolve.go` — `Resolve()` now also reads `workflow_steps.input_mapping`. New `params.go` — `resolveParams(step, priorResults map[uuid.UUID]StepResult)`: merges static params with extracted values (**extraction overwrites a static value for the same key — the documented, deliberate precedence rule**), erroring cleanly (no partial substitution) if a `from_step` isn't in `priorResults` (covers "future step," "different workflow," and "different run" identically — all three simply absent from the map) or if the gjson path doesn't resolve. `executor.go` — `Run` now accumulates a `priorResults map[uuid.UUID]StepResult` per-run, purely in-memory, passed into each `runStep` call; `resolveParams` runs right after connector resolution and before dispatch — a failure there records `outcome='blocked'` with a clear `error_detail` exactly like the existing dangling-connector path, and **dispatch is never called**.
**New/changed — `eami-api`:** `WorkflowStepDTO` gained `ID`/`InputMapping` (new `ExtractionRefDTO` type); `InsertWorkflowStep` takes a caller-assigned `id` (was DB `DEFAULT`) plus `inputMapping []byte`; new `resolveStepIDs` reuses a submitted step's real id only if it matches a currently-existing step of THAT workflow (`ListWorkflowSteps` scoped by `workflow_id`, itself already org-verified via `GetWorkflow` earlier in the same handler) — anything else (omitted, malformed, another workflow's id) mints fresh, identical to today's behavior for any caller that's never seen this field.
**A second real bug found live during this brief's own verification (not part of the originally approved plan, fixed the same session, disclosed not glossed over):** `workflow_step_params.workflow_step_id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE` (migration 000007) — so even with step-id reuse working correctly, `DeleteWorkflowSteps`' unconditional delete-then-reinsert still cascade-deleted a step's *static* params the instant its row was deleted, even though the same id got reinserted moments later. Reproduced live: an unrelated sibling-step edit silently zeroed a step's static params to `{}`. Fixed by capturing each reused step's current params (via the existing B-059 `GetWorkflowStepParams`) before the delete, and re-writing them (via the existing B-059 `UpsertWorkflowStepParams`) inside the same transaction right after the reinsert — no new SQL, reuses both functions as-is.
**Reviewer + security passes, both mandatory (a new expression-evaluation surface reading across step boundaries), both ran, both clean:** Code review found and both got fixed: (1) `go.mod` had `tidwall/gjson` incorrectly marked `// indirect` (`go mod tidy` not run) — cosmetic, fixed; (2) the SAME real step id echoed on two different submitted steps in one request would let `resolveStepIDs` assign it to both, and the second `INSERT` would hit `workflow_steps`' primary-key uniqueness (23505, a code `isForeignKeyViolation` doesn't recognize) — surfaced as a raw 500 with a leaked Postgres error string instead of a clean 400; fixed with an explicit duplicate-id check in `validateWorkflowSteps`, tested. Security review, matching B-042's own rigor for the cross-run/cross-workflow isolation claim (AC4): independently re-derived (not trusted from comments) that `resolveParams` has zero DB calls anywhere in its path, that `priorResults` is a fresh function-local map per `Run()` call (never shared/cached/global — no cross-run or cross-org aliasing possible), and that a step genuinely cannot reference itself or a not-yet-executed step (traced the exact ordering: `priorResults[step.WorkflowStepID] = sr` is written only *after* that step's own `resolveParams` call already ran). Independently re-traced `resolveStepIDs`' SQL scoping and confirmed airtight — zero exploitable findings from either pass.
**Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/params_test.go` (7 pure unit tests, no DB: static-only unaffected, nested-path extraction, unresolved reference — covering future-step/cross-workflow/cross-run identically since all three manifest as absence from the map, non-"allowed" prior step, path-not-found, precedence, no-aliasing-of-step.Params). `mapping_test.go` (3 real-Postgres end-to-end tests: a real 2-step run with a real extracted value genuinely received by a real fake-adapter downstream call (AC1); a real path-not-found failure with `workflow_run_steps.outcome='blocked'` confirmed at the DB level and the downstream adapter confirmed never called (AC3); a REAL second workflow's real step id substituted as `from_step` in a different workflow, confirmed to never resolve (AC4, real data not a synthetic UUID)). `eami-api/internal/api/workflows_test.go` gained 5 new tests: `id` round-trips across a pure reorder (AC2); `input_mapping` survives an unrelated field edit (proving the severity finding above is actually fixed); an echoed id from a *different* workflow is never reused/stolen (security-review-anticipated guard, real HTTP round trip); the duplicate-echoed-id regression guard; static params survive a reused-step-id sibling edit (the second bug's regression guard). **Verified 2026-08-13 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` modules.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (multiple times, as each review/verification finding was fixed):** a real admin session created a real 2-step workflow (`b046-live-verify`/`echo_post` both steps, real `postman-echo.com` dispatch) with step 1's real static params (`lookup_key`) and step 2's real `input_mapping` (`forwarded_key` ← step 1's `json.params.lookup_key`). A real agent JWT ran it via `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run`: step 1's real response genuinely echoed `lookup_key`; step 2's real downstream HTTP request genuinely carried `forwarded_key` with that exact extracted value — traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column directly via `psql`, not just the HTTP response. Separately confirmed the params-carry-forward fix live (step 1's params intact after an unrelated step-2 edit) and AC3 live (a broken path on the same live workflow failed the run cleanly, `outcome='blocked'`, a precise error naming the exact param/path/step, step 1 unaffected). Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Dependencies:** B-058 (`workflow_steps`/`input_mapping` column, extended not modified), B-059 (execution engine/`workflow_step_params`, both reused), the MCP Discovery investigation (D9). **Out of scope, as specified:** any UI for defining extraction expressions (a real variable-picker UX is a separate, later polish brief — this brief is the backend mechanism only, verified via direct API/SQL calls); branching/conditional logic; the visual canvas redesign.

### B-064 — Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 4: extraction expression UI — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** B-063 built the real backend mechanism for output→input mapping but shipped with zero UI — every extraction so far was configured via direct API/SQL calls. This brief adds a real, working (deliberately minimal) interface in the Workflows editor for an admin to configure it themselves.
**Two findings, re-verified directly against current code before building, that changed what "minimal but real" actually meant here (kickoff line's own instruction):** (1) **No static-value step UI existed at all** — `grep` confirmed zero references to "params" anywhere in `WorkflowsPage.tsx`; B-059's `PUT/GET .../workflow-steps/{stepId}/params` had only ever been driven by direct API calls. CONTRACTS' "static value (existing)" phrasing assumed a UI that was never built — so this brief's real scope also had to include the first-ever static-param row editor, a precondition for "static vs. extracted" to be a meaningful per-param choice, not a scope choice made unilaterally. (2) **No path exists today for fetching a prior step's last real run result** — `workflow_run_steps`/`workflow_runs` are gateway-owned tables with zero references anywhere in `eami-api`, and `eami-ui` has no network path to `eami-gateway` at all (`vite.config.ts`'s only proxy target is `VITE_API_URL`; `docker-compose.yml`'s `VITE_GATEWAY_URL` is set but never referenced in any frontend source file — dead config). Per the brief's own file scope, no backend file was authorized to fix this. **Decision, confirmed with the user before building:** the ASSUMPTIONS section's "real sample" half is not feasible within this brief's scope — always use the plain-text/dropdown fallback, never attempt a live preview. Doesn't cross the file-scope boundary (the safe choice, not an expansion); logged as a natural, separate future brief, likely worth pairing with whenever a runs-history view gets built for other reasons.
**Extraction-source eligibility, precisely:** a step is a valid `from_step` target only if (a) it is earlier in the *current, live* step order being edited, and (b) it already has a real backend `id` (returned by a prior `GetWorkflow`/`Create`/`UpdateWorkflow` response). A step just added in the current edit session has no id yet and is never selectable as a source — but it can immediately be configured to extract *from* an already-real-id'd earlier step. Real, disclosed constraint, not an oversight: AC1's actual flow (save with step 1 → reopen → add step 2 extracting from step 1) has zero friction from this.
**`eami-ui/src/hooks/useWorkflows.ts`:** `WorkflowStep` gained `id`/`input_mapping`; the Create/Update step-input shape gained optional `id`/`input_mapping` (purely additive, matches the backend's own additive contract exactly). New `useWorkflowStepParams`/`useSetWorkflowStepParams` wrapping B-059's endpoint directly (its first-ever UI consumer).
**`eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`:** `StepRow` gained `localId` (client-generated `crypto.randomUUID()`, or the step's own real id once loaded — a frontend-local analog of the exact "reference by stable id, not position" problem B-063 already solved server-side, needed because a reorder must not misdirect a reference mid-edit-session either), `id?`, and `params: ParamRow[]`. `ParamRow` unifies static and extracted params into one per-step list with a mode toggle — an extract row gets a real `<select>` of eligible sources (`rows.slice(0, i).filter(r => r.id)`, labeled `"Step N: <connector> / <action>"`, never a raw UUID) plus a plain-text path input. `moveRow`/`removeRow`/every param mutation route through a single `commit()` that always re-runs new `revalidateExtractionRefs` — any extract row whose source is no longer earlier-and-real-id'd gets flagged `invalid` (visible red border + a clear message), never silently cleared or silently left looking fine (AC3, mirrors the existing `toolDeleted` step-level pattern). `validateAndConvertRows` extended to block submission on any invalid/incomplete param, and to split each step's params into `input_mapping` (rides along in the main Create/Update body) vs. static params (saved separately, see below).
**Static params solved with zero "save, reopen" friction, via a design choice not in the original plan's first draft:** since static params live on a *separate* endpoint keyed by a real step id, and a brand-new step's id isn't known until the main save's response comes back, new `saveStaticParams` fires the B-059 PUT for every step with static params **using the ids the SAME save's response just returned** — so a step's own static params can be set in the exact submit that creates it. Only the extraction-*source* eligibility constraint above remains (a step can't extract from a sibling created in the identical submit, since `input_mapping` must be embedded in the request built *before* the response is known) — real, minor, doesn't block any stated AC.
**`EditWorkflowPanel`'s row-init:** extract rows seed synchronously from the loaded workflow's `input_mapping` (already embedded, no extra fetch); static rows seed via `useQueries` (one query per already-real-id'd step, TanStack Query's own multi-query primitive) so `rows` only initializes once the workflow *and* every step's current static params have loaded.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean, run locally (Node/npm confirmed installed on this machine, superseding `CLAUDE.md`'s prior "not installed" toolchain note). No dedicated reviewer/security subagent pass — this brief's own STANDING RULES didn't mandate one (frontend-only, zero backend/SQL/extraction-logic surface touched, matching B-060's identical no-review precedent for a comparably-scoped UI-only brief) — but the diff was self-reviewed directly before commit.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh, served-module content confirmed via direct `curl` before proceeding (per the B-062 lesson — never assume a rebuild landed):** browser automation remains unavailable in this environment (documented standing limitation, unchanged since B-058/B-059/B-060's own identical disclosures) — so "configured entirely through the UI" was verified by sending the exact request sequence and body shapes the UI's own `validateAndConvertRows`/`saveStaticParams`/hook code generates for a real simulated admin flow (add step 1 with no params → reopen → add a static param on step 1 + a new step 2 extracting from step 1 → submit → confirm persistence on reload), not literal clicks. **AC1**: full round trip confirmed — step 1's static `lookup_key` and step 2's `input_mapping` (referencing step 1's real, preserved id) both reloaded byte-identical after save. **AC4**: the resulting workflow, run for real via a real agent JWT, genuinely dispatched step 2 with the value extracted from step 1's real recorded response — traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column, the same evidence B-063 used. **AC2/AC3** verified by direct code trace, disclosed as such rather than a browser-observed claim (matches B-060's own identical precedent for its connector-switch-reset claim) — AC3 specifically is a pure client-side state transition with no network round-trip to observe live at all. Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Dependencies:** B-058/B-059 (`useWorkflows.ts`/`WorkflowsPage.tsx`, extended not modified in their core CRUD/action-picker logic), B-060 (the action-picker pattern this extends), B-063 (the backend mechanism and API contract this is a UI for). **Out of scope, as specified:** a polished, Freshservice-grade auto-suggested-fields/live-JSON-browsing experience (needs a new backend read endpoint, deliberately not built here — see finding 2 above); the visual card/canvas redesign; durable mid-chain escalation / chain-aware approval UI (separate briefs).

### B-065 — Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 5: Freshservice-styled visual redesign — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** restyle the Workflows editor's step list from a plain form-row list into the researched Freshservice pattern — steps as a clear visual sequence of cards, with detailed per-step configuration in its own slide-out panel. Presentation only, confirmed and held throughout: zero changes to `useWorkflows.ts`'s contracts, any backend code, or B-063/B-064's extraction logic/API shape.
**Confirmed before building, per the kickoff line's own instruction:** no graph-canvas library (React Flow or similar) is needed — a vertical stack of step cards with a small connecting chevron between them reads as a clear sequence without node/port machinery, matching the standing v1-linear-only scope (no branching modeled anywhere in this schema/UI). No blocking reason found to revisit this.
**The one real structural change:** previously, a step's connector/action pickers AND its full B-064 parameter editor were inline in one form row (B-064's own inline accordion). CONTRACTS asked for a step CARD (summary only) plus a "configure" affordance opening the detail editor in its own slide-out panel, mirroring `ToolsPage.tsx`'s drawer pattern literally — so the connector/action/parameter editing UI moved into a new, second, layered slide-out panel (`StepConfigPanel`, higher z-index + its own backdrop over the workflow panel itself), opened from a "Configure" button on each card. Every underlying handler — `moveRow`, `removeRow`, `validateAndConvertRows`, `revalidateExtractionRefs`, `updateParam`/`addParam`/`removeParam`, `saveStaticParams`, `commit` — is reused completely unchanged; `StepConfigPanel` holds zero state and zero validation logic of its own, purely a view over the same shared `rows` state.
**New `summarizeParams`** drives each card's one-line, always-accurate summary (AC2) computed from already-loaded local state, no new fetch: "No parameters configured", a specific "Extracting `x` from Step N" sentence for a single extraction, or a count ("2 static, 1 extracted") otherwise. Existing `toolDeleted`/invalid-extraction warnings (B-058/B-064) are surfaced directly on the card (red border + message), not hidden behind Configure.
**Status prominence + Activate action (AC4):** `EditWorkflowPanel` gained a prominent status badge next to a real, standalone "Activate" button when a workflow is `draft` — a genuine new small capability (not just a restyle), firing an immediate `PATCH {status: 'active'}` that touches nothing else, so flipping status doesn't require a full steps save. The existing status dropdown is untouched and still works (e.g. to set Disabled) — no capability lost.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. No dedicated reviewer/security pass, matching B-064's own precedent — this brief's standing rules didn't mandate one and the diff is confined to `WorkflowsPage.tsx` (`useWorkflows.ts` confirmed zero diff via `git diff --stat`), no backend/SQL/extraction-logic surface touched.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt fresh, served-module content confirmed via direct `curl` before proceeding (per B-062's lesson):** browser automation remains unavailable in this environment (same standing limitation every prior `eami-ui` brief has disclosed) — verification used the same request-sequence-equivalence method B-064 established: a real simulated admin flow (create with step 1, draft status → reopen → configure step 1's static param + add step 2 extracting from step 1 → save → click Activate → reload) sent as the exact request shapes the new UI code generates. **Confirmed live:** step 1's id/static param and step 2's `input_mapping` all persisted correctly on reload; the standalone Activate PATCH flipped `status` to `active` while leaving both steps byte-identical (proving AC4's design — status-only, never touching steps); the resulting workflow, run for real via a real agent JWT, genuinely dispatched step 2 carrying the value extracted from step 1's real recorded response, traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column, the same evidence B-063/B-064 used. **Disclosed limitation, explicitly, not overclaimed:** this proves the data/API side is unaffected by the redesign, but — with no browser session available — cannot itself observe the new card layout rendering; the dev-server-served-module `curl` check is the closest available proof the new `StepConfigPanel`/card component tree is real and wired, short of an actual rendered-pixel check. Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Dependencies:** B-058/B-060 (the step list/action-picker this restyles), B-064 (the parameter editor relocated into `StepConfigPanel`, logic unchanged). **Out of scope, as specified:** a graph-canvas/node-port library (confirmed unneeded); drag-to-reorder (up/down arrows preserved, restyled into the card); any new backend capability or API shape change; the sample-browsing extraction UX (still deferred, per B-064).

### B-066 — Workflow Canvas, Brief 1: `@xyflow/react` integration + read-only rendering — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** first brief of the new Workflow Canvas epic — prove `@xyflow/react` integrates cleanly in this stack and renders an existing workflow's real steps as ordered, read-only canvas nodes, before any editing/interactivity gets built. B-065's card UI remains the only editor; this is a read-only preview reached via its own route.
**A stated premise corrected before building on it:** the brief (and the preceding investigation) assumed Tailwind here has "no compiler, only pre-defined utility classes." Re-verified directly: `tailwind.config.ts`/`postcss.config.js` confirm a real JIT-compiled pipeline. Doesn't change the outcome (React Flow ships its own precompiled CSS, zero interaction with Tailwind either way) but worth correcting rather than silently building on a false premise.
**`eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`, additive only (19 lines changed, confirmed via `git diff --stat`, not assumed):** `ParamRow`, `StepRow`, `summarizeParams` gained `export` — zero logic touched, `StepsEditor`/`StepConfigPanel`/`AddWorkflowPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` are byte-for-byte untouched. One new `Waypoints` icon `<Link>` per table row to `/gateway/workflows/{id}/canvas`.
**New `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx`:** fetches the same data `EditWorkflowPanel` already fetches (`useWorkflow` + the same per-step static-params `useQueries` pattern), builds the same local row shape purely to call the *imported* `summarizeParams` (not reimplemented) per step, and renders a new `WorkflowStepNode` custom node (same Tailwind classes/red-border-warning treatment as B-065's cards, so the two views read as one product) in a simple deterministic vertical stack from `step_order` — no position persisted anywhere. Explicitly, structurally read-only: `nodesDraggable`/`nodesConnectable`/`elementsSelectable` all `false` on `<ReactFlow>`, every `<Handle>` `isConnectable={false}` — there is no code path that could mutate anything, not just an absent save button. One new route in `router.tsx`.
**Real, measured bundle-weight data (not estimated):** `npm run build` before/after — JS grew from 933.80 kB → 1,110.92 kB minified (256.72 kB → 313.67 kB gzipped, **+~57 kB gzipped**), CSS grew ~16 kB (React Flow's own precompiled stylesheet).
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean on the first attempt — re-confirms the investigation's library recommendation against actual integration, not just research (per this brief's own kickoff line instruction). No dedicated reviewer/security pass — presentation-only, no backend/SQL surface, matching B-064/065's precedent.
**Live-verified, honestly split between what's actually checkable and what isn't, disclosed plainly rather than overclaimed:** rebuilt/redeployed `eami-ui` (a real image rebuild was required here, unlike a pure `.tsx` edit — this brief added a `package.json` dependency, and `node_modules` isn't bind-mounted, only `src/` is); confirmed via direct `curl` to the dev server that the served modules contain the new `WorkflowCanvasPage`/`WorkflowStepNode` code and the updated `WorkflowsPage.tsx`/`router.tsx`, all `200`s, no import-resolution errors. Confirmed via real API calls the exact data the new page's hooks fetch is correct and complete for a real existing 2-step workflow (`b063-live-verify`, including its real `input_mapping`) and a temporary real 5-step workflow created specifically to exercise AC4's "many steps" case (both `GetWorkflow` and all 5 steps' `params` endpoints returned clean `200`s) — temp workflow deleted afterward. Confirmed zero regression to B-065 via a real spot-check `PATCH` through the unchanged edit flow. **AC4's "zero steps" case is defensively handled in code (an `EmptyState` render path) but could not be produced via the real API for a live check** — `validateWorkflowSteps` has required ≥1 step since B-058, so a truly empty workflow isn't currently reachable through normal use; the empty-state code path is real but unexercised against live data, disclosed rather than glossed over. **AC1's "renders correctly... in the right order" and AC2's "no visual/CSS conflict... verify by viewing both side by side" are genuinely visual claims this environment's standing no-browser-automation limitation cannot verify directly** — data correctness and code-is-served correctness are both confirmed live above, but the actual rendered pixels were not seen by this session. Disclosed plainly, not claimed as verified; the user is invited to look at `/gateway/workflows/<id>/canvas` themselves.
**Dependencies:** B-058/060/064/065 (the data and summary logic this reads, unmodified). **Out of scope, as specified:** any interactivity beyond viewing; the two-layer connection-validation design from the investigation's A.2 finding (nothing editable yet, so nothing to validate); position persistence; cutting over from cards to canvas as the primary editor.

### B-067 — Workflow Canvas, Brief 2: interactivity + draw-time connection validation — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** make B-066's read-only canvas genuinely interactive — click a node to configure it (`StepConfigPanel`, B-065, reused verbatim), add/remove nodes and draw connections locally, and real draw-time validation (one-in/one-out + cycle rejection) per the investigation's A.2 finding. Explicitly NOT the save-time full-graph validation layer (Brief 3) — draw-time alone is real and useful as local UX and as Brief 3's foundation, but the investigation was explicit this is not sufficient on its own, and this brief must never let a structurally-broken graph reach the backend.
**A critical finding, re-verified against the actual current code before building, not taken on the brief's own faith:** the brief's ASSUMPTIONS claimed clicking a node "already has real, working persistence (B-064's param mutators)... unchanged." Re-checked directly: `StepConfigPanel`'s param-mutator props only ever updated **local `rows` state** in every existing caller — the actual network write only ever fired from the *enclosing panel's* main "Save changes" submit, which B-066's new canvas page doesn't have. There was no "already persists" behavior to inherit.
**Consequently, a real design fork, resolved and disclosed rather than silently decided:** static params have their own independent endpoint (`PUT /workflow-steps/{id}/params`, B-059) — safe to save immediately from the canvas, touches nothing structural. `input_mapping` (extraction) has **no independent endpoint at all** — B-063/064's backend only ever writes it as part of a full workflow PATCH that also carries `steps` (structure), which this brief is explicitly forbidden from calling. Resolution: static param edits save for real, immediately, when a step's config panel closes (AC1); extraction edits made via the canvas stay local-only this brief, surfaced through the same unsaved-changes banner as structural edits — a real, disclosed consequence of the existing contract, not a bug.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`, two more additive `export` keywords only (2 lines changed total, confirmed via `git diff --stat`):** `StepConfigPanel` and `revalidateExtractionRefs` join B-066's existing exports (`summarizeParams`/`ParamRow`/`StepRow`) — zero logic touched anywhere in this file.
**`WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` becomes stateful:** local `rows`/`edges`/`hasUnsavedChanges` state, seeded once from the fetched workflow (same guarded-init pattern `EditWorkflowPanel` already established). `closeConfigPanel` is where the persistence split lives: compares a step's static-params snapshot before/after the panel was open, fires a real PUT only if it actually changed (never a redundant write), and separately flags `hasUnsavedChanges` if any extraction-mode param was touched. `addNode`/`removeNode` mutate only local state (grep-confirmed to contain zero `apiFetch`/`mutateAsync` calls — AC4). `isValidConnection` implements React Flow's own documented reference pattern exactly: self-loop rejection, one-outgoing-edge-max, one-incoming-edge-max (via existing-edge counts), and cycle rejection via `getOutgoers` (AC2/AC3) — each rejection surfaces a specific toast naming the reason, not a silent no-op. `WorkflowStepNode` gained `onConfigure`/`onRemove` callbacks (via `data`, standard React Flow custom-node pattern) and the same `Settings2`/`Trash2` icons B-065's cards already use.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. No dedicated reviewer/security pass — presentation-only, no backend/SQL surface, matching B-064/065/066's precedent.
**Live-verified, per this brief's own stated verification method:** rebuilt/redeployed; served-module `curl` confirmed the real `getOutgoers` cycle-check, `isValidConnection`, `closeConfigPanel`, and all three new `WorkflowsPage.tsx` exports are the code actually deployed. **AC1, real persistence**: a real static-param PUT (matching exactly what `closeConfigPanel` generates) against the real `b063-live-verify` workflow's step 1 confirmed to persist via a follow-up `GET`, and confirmed the workflow's own `updated_at` timestamp was completely unaffected (proving the params write genuinely never touches `workflows`/`workflow_steps` at all) — reverted afterward, zero residual test data. **AC2/AC3, draw-time rejection**: verified by direct code inspection of `isValidConnection`'s four checks (self-loop, one-out, one-in, `getOutgoers`-based cycle detection) plus confirming this is the exact code served — no live test suite exists in this repo to run automatically, and a literal draw-and-reject browser interaction remains unavailable in this environment (same standing limitation). **AC4, "no backend call fires"**: verified by direct code inspection — `addNode`/`removeNode`/`onConnect` grep-confirmed to contain zero `apiFetch`/`mutateAsync`/`mutate(` call sites, a precise structural proof, not a substitute for literally watching network traffic (which remains unavailable). **AC5**: `git diff --stat` confirms `WorkflowsPage.tsx` changed by exactly 2 lines.
**Dependencies:** B-066 (the canvas this extends), B-065 (`StepConfigPanel`, reused unmodified), B-059/063 (the static-params and extraction endpoints this brief's persistence split is built around). **Out of scope, as specified:** persisting structural changes to the backend (Brief 3); save-time full-graph validation (Brief 3 — this brief's draw-time layer is explicitly not a substitute); cutover from cards to canvas as the primary editor.

### B-068 — Workflow Canvas, Brief 3: save-time validation, structural persistence — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** close the investigation's own A.2 two-layer requirement — draw-time validation (B-067) alone can't catch a deletion leaving a broken graph, so this brief adds the mandatory save-time full-graph validation backstop, then real structural persistence through the existing `UpdateWorkflow` PATCH (the same endpoint/contract `EditWorkflowPanel`'s cards already use, unmodified). First brief in this epic where the canvas actually writes to the backend.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`, two more additive `export` keywords only (confirmed via `git diff --stat`):** `validateAndConvertRows` and `saveStaticParams` join the existing exports — zero logic touched anywhere in this file, same established pattern as every prior canvas brief.
**New `validateGraph(rows, edges)` in `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx`:** the actual save-time backstop, re-deriving graph validity fresh from current state rather than trusting anything from draw time. Requires exactly one node with zero incoming edges (start) and one with zero outgoing edges (end), no node with more than one edge in or out, and a walk from the start that visits every node exactly once — the only shape the backend's flat, ordered `steps` array can represent. Every rejection names the specific offending step(s) by number, per CONTEXT.md's Workflow Canvas ease-of-use principle (e.g. "Step 2, Step 4 have no connection leading into them..."), never a generic "invalid graph."
**`handleSave`:** `validateGraph` → reorder `rows` to the derived order → `revalidateExtractionRefs` on the *reordered* array (order can change what counts as "earlier") → the imported `validateAndConvertRows` → on any error, a persistent `saveError` banner (distinct from the transient connection-attempt `toast`, never auto-clears) naming the problem, save aborted before any network call. On success: the same `useUpdateWorkflow().mutateAsync` `EditWorkflowPanel` already calls, then the imported `saveStaticParams` for any step with static params (covers a brand-new node's own statics in the same submit that creates it), then local `rows`/`edges`/`savedStaticKeys` rebuilt from the confirmed persisted response — the canvas reflects the real saved order/ids immediately, `hasUnsavedChanges` clears. The extraction-persistence gap B-067 left open closes itself here with zero new logic for that half: once the graph is validated and correctly ordered, the existing, unmodified `validateAndConvertRows` builds `input_mapping` from the reordered rows exactly as it always has.
**A real, low-reachability defensive gap found and closed during the mandatory reviewer pass (not left as a known issue):** `validateGraph`'s original reachability check (`order.length < rows.length`) only guarded under-coverage — if `edges` ever contained an edge targeting an id not present in `rows` (not reachable via any current UI interaction, since `addNode`/`removeNode`/`onConnect` all keep `rows`/`edges` in sync), the walk could push that phantom id into `order`, producing `error: null` alongside a corrupted `order`, and `handleSave` would crash with an uncaught exception outside its `try/catch` rather than a graceful `saveError`. Hardened: the walk now only ever advances onto a real row id, and the length check changed to `!==` (impossible to overshoot now, not just unlikely to).
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean.
**Reviewer + security subagent pass, mandatory per this brief's own standing rules (unlike B-064–067):** independently re-traced `validateGraph` against every malformed/valid topology in scope (disconnected components, over-connected nodes, pure cycles, multiple starts/ends, self-loops, out-of-array-order chains) — all correct except the phantom-edge gap above, now fixed. Confirmed no path reaches `mutateAsync` without a non-null `validateGraph` order first. Confirmed `eami-api/internal/api/workflows.go`'s `UpdateWorkflow`/`resolveStepIDs` are genuinely unmodified (`git diff` empty). Traced the real trust boundary directly in code, not just by comment: the backend deliberately does NOT validate `input_mapping.from_step` ordering/existence at write time (`validateWorkflowSteps` only checks UUID well-formedness) — but this is safe by construction, not a gap, since `eami-gateway`'s `resolveParams`/`priorResults` is scoped per-run to only that run's own already-completed steps (`params.go`), so a forward or cross-org reference simply isn't found and fails the run cleanly (`blocked`, not corrupted data) rather than resolving to anything. Cross-org step-id adoption independently confirmed impossible via `resolveStepIDs`'s `GetWorkflow(id, orgID)`-scoped existing-steps map. No vulnerability found.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh, served-module `curl` confirmed the real `validateGraph`/`handleSave`/both newly-exported `WorkflowsPage.tsx` functions are the code actually deployed, at B-063's original full rigor standard (not the lighter request-sequence-equivalence substitute used since B-064):** created a real throwaway 2-step workflow (`b046-live-verify`/`echo_post` × 2), sent the exact request sequence `handleSave` generates for a real canvas save (static-param PUT, structural PATCH carrying `input_mapping`, static-param re-PUT) — **AC1/AC2**: confirmed byte-identical on reload. `validateGraph` itself was extracted verbatim from the served module and executed for real (not just read) against six concrete topologies — valid chain, valid chain drawn in reverse array order (order correctly reflects graph topology, not array position), the brief's own example (a deleted connecting edge leaving a step disconnected), an over-connected node, a pure cycle, and two competing start nodes — every rejection named the correct specific step(s), every acceptance produced the correct order. Confirmed backend-side defense in depth directly: a structurally incomplete step submitted straight to the PATCH endpoint (bypassing the frontend entirely) was independently rejected with a real `400`, and the stored workflow was unaffected. **AC3, full B-063-standard proof**: configured a real extraction, saved, confirmed `input_mapping` persisted via `GET`, ran the workflow for real via a real agent JWT (`POST /v1/gateway/tokens` + `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run`), and traced the extracted value through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column directly in Postgres — step 1's real dispatched value (`"lookup_key":"b068-canvas-value"`) reappeared as step 2's real dispatched `forwarded_key`, genuinely extracted at run time, not simulated. **AC4**: `EditWorkflowPanel`'s and `WorkflowCanvasPage`'s row-init blocks confirmed byte-identical in construction from the same `GetWorkflow` response (both read `s.id`/`s.gateway_tool_id`/`s.action`/`s.input_mapping` the same way into the same `StepRow` shape), and the canvas-saved workflow's `GET` response (already confirmed correct above) round-trips through that identical logic either editor uses. **AC5**: `git diff --stat` confirms `WorkflowsPage.tsx` stays to the two new `export` keywords. Test workflow, its two real runs, and the scratch agent JWT/admin-token files all deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Dependencies:** B-066/067 (the canvas this extends), B-058 (`UpdateWorkflow`'s full-replace `steps` contract, unmodified), B-059/063/064 (`saveStaticParams`/`validateAndConvertRows`/`revalidateExtractionRefs`, all reused verbatim). **Closes the Workflow Canvas epic's own two-layer validation requirement from the investigation** — draw-time (B-067) + save-time (this brief) are both now real. **Out of scope, as specified:** cutover from cards to canvas as the primary editor; free node dragging/position persistence; branching (still linear-chain-only, matching Thread B's own current backend scope).

### B-069 — Remove Workflow Canvas, revert to card-only editor — **DONE, 2026-08-13**
**Objective:** discontinue the Workflow Canvas epic (see the epic header above for the full reasoning) after real hands-on browser testing found multiple confirmed-broken interactions that this environment's code-inspection-only verification method never caught. Remove the canvas entirely; B-065's card editor becomes the sole workflow editor again.
**Removed:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` (deleted outright), its route (`router.tsx`), the "view canvas" `Waypoints` link in `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s table, and the `@xyflow/react` dependency (`npm uninstall`, not a hand-edit — `package.json`/`package-lock.json` genuinely regenerated).
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`, reverted (not deleted) — stripped `export` from all 7 identifiers the canvas epic had added across B-066/067/068 (`ParamRow`, `StepRow`, `summarizeParams`, `StepConfigPanel`, `revalidateExtractionRefs`, `validateAndConvertRows`, `saveStaticParams`) — confirmed via grep each is still used internally (5-15 occurrences beyond its own declaration) before touching it, so none of the underlying functions/types were deleted, only their canvas-only visibility.** Also reverted an in-session, never-committed defensive z-index bump on `StepConfigPanel` (a mitigation for a hypothesized, never-confirmed canvas/React-Flow stacking conflict — moot with the canvas gone) and removed now-stale doc comments that explained why something was exported "for the canvas."
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean, zero remaining `xyflow`/`WorkflowCanvasPage` references anywhere in `eami-ui/src` (grepped). **Bundle size returned to the exact pre-B-066 baseline** — 933.80 kB / 256.72 kB gzipped JS (identical to B-066's own recorded "before" measurement), CSS back to 27.39 kB (React Flow's precompiled stylesheet gone) — proving the dependency is genuinely removed, not just dead-code-eliminated. Rebuilt/redeployed `eami-ui`; confirmed directly inside the built image (`docker run` + grep) that `package.json`/`package-lock.json`/`node_modules` are all clean of `@xyflow`, not just trusting a cached-looking build log.
**Live-verified the card editor at B-065's original rigor, proving zero regression:** created a workflow via the real Create request shape, configured a real static param (`PUT /workflow-steps/{id}/params`) and a real extraction (`PATCH` with `input_mapping`) via the exact request sequences `StepConfigPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` generate, activated via the standalone status-only `PATCH`, confirmed byte-identical round-trip on reload, ran the workflow for real via a real agent JWT, and traced the extracted value through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` Postgres column — the same standard B-063/064/065 used. Test workflow and its run deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Not done here (deliberately, per this brief's own scope):** no attempt to fix the canvas bugs first — the decision was to remove, not repair, given an unknown remaining bug count and no way to verify fixes without literal browser interaction in this environment.

### B-070 — Rate limiting on login, setup wizard, and workflow-run — **DONE, 2026-08-17**
