**Objective:** login (brute-force credential attacks), the setup-wizard bootstrap endpoint (B-055's console-token exchange), and the workflow-run endpoint (B-059, fully synchronous, can block up to 10 minutes on escalation) had no application-level rate limiting — a real resource-exhaustion/brute-force vector across two services (`eami-api`, `eami-gateway`).
**A scope correction found during research, before building:** the task brief's own premise ("no rate limiting exists anywhere in this stack") wasn't accurate — B-055's `bootstrap.go` already rate-limited its setup routes per-IP in-handler, just without a `Retry-After` header. That existing limiter was extracted, generalized, and reused rather than duplicated.
**eami-api:** new `internal/api/ratelimit.go` (shared `rateLimiter` type, extracted from B-055's `setupRateLimiter`) + `ratelimit_login.go` (chi middleware on `POST /v1/auth/login`, per-IP AND per-account, both required — per-IP alone doesn't stop credential stuffing across accounts, per-account alone doesn't stop one IP hammering many accounts). `Login()`/`bootstrap.go`'s validation/DB-write logic: zero lines changed beyond the middleware wrapper and a one-line `Retry-After` header add per existing 429 site. New `config.RateLimitConfig`, 6 env vars, all defaulted and individually overridable.
**eami-gateway:** new `internal/workflow/ratelimit.go` (a small, deliberately duplicated limiter — separate Go module, matching B-025/B-044 precedent) + `RateLimitRunMiddleware`, wrapping `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}/run` per-agent-identity in `cmd/gateway/main.go`. This module has zero chi dependency (stdlib `http.ServeMux`) — the brief's "reuse chi's rate limiter" assumption didn't apply here. `HandleRun`/`workflow/http.go`: byte-for-byte unmodified.
**Three real bugs found by this brief's own mandatory security review, all fixed before shipping:** (1) a fail-open bug where a body-read error skipped rate limiting entirely even when the full valid request had already been captured; (2) a parser-disagreement bug (`json.Unmarshal` vs. `Login()`'s streaming `Decoder`) that let one appended byte bypass the tighter per-account limiter in favor of the looser per-IP one; (3) a cross-org rate-limit collision in the gateway middleware, keying on the raw JWT subject (agent name, unique only per-org) instead of the resolved agent's real globally-unique registry ID — plus a shared non-positive-limit panic risk in both modules' `rateLimiter.Allow`, closed with a fail-closed guard and startup config validation in both. Full detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway`/`eami-api` B-070 entries.
**Test coverage:** 20 new tests across both modules (black-box + white-box regression tests for all 3 security findings, config validation tests, real-Postgres bootstrap tests). **Verified 2026-08-17: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across both full modules.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before AND after the security fixes):** all 6 acceptance criteria proven with real HTTP round trips — real 429s with real `Retry-After` headers on all three routes after threshold, real below-threshold success (including a genuine 2-typo-then-correct login), real per-IP cross-account trip, real per-agent workflow-run trip. Full detail in `BUILT.md`.
**Dependencies:** none. **Out of scope, as specified:** distributed/multi-instance rate limiting (ADR-020 Model A is single-instance); rate limiting on any other endpoint (natural follow-up, not this brief); WAF/DDoS-layer protection.

### B-071 — TLS termination for internal service-to-service and external traffic — **DONE, 2026-08-17**
**Objective:** `postgres`/`eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-collector`/`eami-ui` all communicated in plaintext HTTP, both internally and on ports exposed to the customer's network — the last major gap from the original infra audit still fully open.
**Topology: proxy-only TLS termination via a new `eami-proxy` (Caddy) container, not full internal mTLS** — justified against ADR-020 Model A's actual threat model (single-tenant, single-VM appliance; an attacker already inside that private docker network has far worse access available regardless, e.g. direct Postgres/secret access — mTLS defends against a zero-trust/multi-tenant model this product isn't). `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `eami-ui`/`eami-api`/`eami-gateway` no longer publish ports directly at all; only `eami-proxy`'s `:443` (UI+API), `:8443` (gateway, confirmed to already be one single port serving both the REST control API and MCP SSE), and a redirect-only `:80` are reachable from outside the docker network. `docker-compose.yml` (dev) deliberately left unchanged — its Vite dev-server `eami-ui` target is a fundamentally different shape than the nginx production target this brief fronts.
**Certificates:** self-signed by default via Caddy's built-in `tls internal` (zero custom crypto scripting, auto-persisted). Customer cert: drop `fullchain.pem`/`privkey.pem` into `/data/eami/certs/custom/` (data disk, matching B-053's precedent) + restart `eami-proxy` — no rebuild, no UI upload flow built (deliberately simpler v1 default, matching this appliance's existing file-based-config precedent). Public CA/Let's Encrypt explicitly out of scope per the brief.
**A real, scoped exception to B-070's own rate-limiting code, confirmed with the user before touching it:** `eami-api`'s `clientKey()` now trusts `X-Forwarded-For` (previously deliberately ignored — B-047 established there was no single trusted ingress; this brief's own port-closure changes are what create one). Narrowly scoped to `clientKey()` alone — the global `middleware.ClientIPFromRemoteAddr` every other consumer uses stays completely untouched.
**Four real bugs found by this brief's own mandatory security review, all fixed before shipping:** (1) closing `eami-api`'s port only blocks *external* access — any other container on the same unsegmented compose network (e.g. `eami-collector`, itself externally-exposed) could still reach `eami-api` directly and spoof `X-Forwarded-For`; closed with `trustedProxyPeer`, a live-DNS check that the actual peer resolves to `eami-proxy` before trusting anything. (2) an open-redirect: the `:80→:443` redirect originally echoed the client-supplied `Host` header verbatim into the `Location`; fixed with a fixed `EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` value. (3) `tls internal` on a bare-port site address gives Caddy's issuer no fixed identifier for the self-signed cert's SAN, ambiguous for raw-IP access; fixed by qualifying the address with `EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` in the self-signed branch only. (4) `EAMI_CERT_DIR`/`EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` were only written on first boot, silently breaking the customer-cert path for any appliance upgraded from a pre-B-071 image; fixed with an idempotent backfill. Full detail in `BUILT.md`'s Cross-cutting section.
**Test coverage:** 7 new white-box tests for `clientKey()`'s new trust logic (including the untrusted-peer regression test and a real fail-closed DNS-resolution check). **Verified 2026-08-17: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against a real, isolated, fully-rebuilt instantiation of the new topology** (not the stale pinned `ghcr.io` images `docker-compose.prod.yml` references): real TLS 1.3 handshakes inspected directly (`openssl s_client`/`curl -v`) on both listeners; direct-port plaintext access confirmed refused; self-signed cert confirmed working out of the box, then a real customer cert dropped in and confirmed switched to live; real bootstrap+login+agent-registration+workflow-run all working over HTTPS; B-070's rate limiting confirmed functioning correctly through the proxy, including a definitive anti-spoofing proof (21 requests, each with a different forged `X-Forwarded-For` and a different account, all collapsed into one real per-IP bucket and tripped together).
**Dependencies:** none. **Out of scope, as specified:** full internal mTLS (justified above); public CA/Let's Encrypt automation; VM appliance packaging changes beyond cert handling.

### B-072 — TLS for eami-collector's endpoint-agent-facing traffic — **DONE, 2026-08-18**
**Objective:** B-071 terminated TLS for the UI, API, and gateway surfaces per its own precise CONTRACTS/ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA — but `eami-collector`'s port `8888`, where `eami-agent` endpoint scanners upload their reports, was explicitly out of that brief's scope and remained plaintext HTTP, published directly. This traffic crosses the customer's own network from potentially many endpoint machines, not just a browser or a single admin — the last major surface named in the original infra audit's "every service communicates in plaintext" finding.
**The real threat this closes, stated precisely (not just "TLS added"):** before this brief, any device with LAN network position to the collector could passively read the shared `COLLECTOR_API_KEY` and full report contents (including discovered AI-tool-usage details) off the wire in plaintext. After: that eavesdropping/plaintext-key-harvesting path is closed. **Not closed** (per-agent auth's job, see B-073 below): an attacker who obtains the shared key through any *other* means (a compromised endpoint's own local config, an insider) can still impersonate any other agent to the collector.
**Auth investigated before assuming shape:** confirmed via code, not assumed — `eami-collector` already had a *dormant* DB-backed per-key mechanism (`api_keys` table, `internal/api/middleware.go`), but `RegisterKey` had zero callers anywhere and the schema had no agent-identity linkage (just a free-text label). Making this real needed its own scoping pass separate from the TLS work — deferred, see B-073.
**Mechanism:** extended B-071's `eami-proxy` (Caddy) with a 4th site block (`:8888` → `eami-collector:8888`) rather than wiring native Go TLS into the collector (which already had dormant, unused `TLSCertPath`/`TLSKeyPath` config fields) — one CA/trust mechanism reused, not a second parallel one. `eami-collector` itself: zero Go code changes.
**Cert-trust distribution — the real usability constraint, closed via `eami-agent`'s existing ADR-014 registry-fallback pattern:** new `CollectorConfig.CACertPath` (Windows registry `CollectorCACertPath`, Linux/macOS `agent.yaml`'s `ca_cert_path`), and `internal/collector/sender.go`'s `Sender` now optionally trusts a custom CA via `tls.Config{RootCAs: ...}` (additive to the OS trust store, never replacing it; unset behaves byte-identical to before this brief). New install-time parameter (`COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` MSI property / `EAMI_COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` env var) flows through the same per-invocation pipe as `COLLECTOR_URL`/`COLLECTOR_API_KEY` — a **file path**, not inline PEM content (avoids real quoting/length fragility). `appliance/README.md` documents extracting the appliance's CA from the running `eami-proxy` container and staging it for installer invocations.
**Four real bugs found by this brief's own mandatory security review, all fixed before shipping:** (1) a B-ID mismatch — this work was built under a freshly-minted "B-073" before realizing B-072 already existed, reserved for exactly this scope, by this session's own prior work; renamed throughout rather than leaving a stale open B-072 alongside an unreserved B-073 (the exact class of mistake this repo's own standing feedback memory exists to prevent — the counter alone isn't sufficient, existing reservations must be checked too). (2) the documented CA-staging path (`/tmp`) was world-writable, letting a local unprivileged attacker pre-plant a malicious CA before the real install ran and have it silently trusted — closed with a real ownership/permission check (root-owned, not group/other-writable, correct bitwise — not numeric-magnitude — permission-bit testing) in both Linux/macOS postinstall scripts before trusting any staged file. (3) the Windows MSI wrote the registry pointer straight at the original staged path with no persistent copy, unlike Linux/macOS — SCCM/Intune commonly clean up staging caches after install, which would silently break CA trust later; fixed with a new `CopyCollectorCACert` custom action copying into a permanent location under `INSTALLFOLDER`. (4) a misleading copy-pasted comment claiming a short-lived native-messaging-host invocation was a "long-lived background service."
**A second real bug, self-inflicted, only caught by live installer testing (not by reading the WiX XML):** the property-copy fix above (finding 3) initially scheduled its `SetProperty` `Before="CostFinalize"` — but `INSTALLFOLDER` isn't resolved to a real path until `CostFinalize` itself runs, so the registry value came out as a bare relative filename (`collector-ca.pem`) even though the actual file copy (scheduled correctly, after `InstallFiles`) succeeded. Caught by literally installing the built MSI and reading the resulting registry value back with `Get-ItemProperty`, not by inspection; fixed by rescheduling to `After="CostFinalize"`, rebuilt, reinstalled, reconfirmed correct.
**Per-agent authentication: investigated, deliberately deferred, not silently skipped — see B-073 below.**
**Test coverage:** `eami-agent/internal/collector/sender_test.go` (5 new tests, using a real `httptest.NewTLSServer` — genuine TLS handshakes, not mocks — including a positive case, a negative-control proving an untrusted server genuinely fails, and error-path tests for a missing/invalid cert file); `eami-agent/internal/config/config_test.go` gained 3 tests mirroring the existing `CollectorURL`/`CollectorAPIKey` registry-fallback coverage for the new field. **Verified 2026-08-18: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across both `eami-agent` and `eami-collector`.**
**Live-verified end-to-end, including a real Windows MSI install (not just Go unit tests) against a real, isolated, fully-rebuilt instantiation of the stack:** real TLS 1.3 handshake inspected directly (`openssl s_client`) on the collector's new port, both self-signed default and customer-cert-override paths confirmed live; a real `eami-agent-1.0.0-windows-amd64.msi` built with the actual agent binary, installed silently via the real documented `msiexec` flow with `COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` set, service started automatically — **zero manual certificate approval**, confirmed by the collector's own logs showing a real `report buffered`/`202` from the live Windows agent. **Negative control, live**: clearing the CA trust registry value and restarting the service produced zero new requests at the collector at all (the TLS handshake failed client-side before any data was sent) — true fail-closed, not a silent plaintext fallback; restoring the correct value immediately resumed successful reporting. Confirmed the collector's own `/v1/ingest` ingestion logic is completely unaffected (AC3) — a separate, pre-existing, unrelated gap in `docker-compose.prod.yml` (no `COLLECTOR_SERVICE_KEY` for the collector→eami-api forwarder hop specifically, absent from prod compose since before this brief, confirmed via diff) caused an unrelated `401` on that downstream leg, not on the agent→collector path this brief actually changed. Full teardown/uninstall verified clean afterward (service, registry, and MSI-tracked files all removed; the one file `CopyCollectorCACert` creates outside WiX's own File table is a disclosed, minor known limitation — an orphaned `collector-ca.pem` survives uninstall, harmless but untidy, not fixed this round).
**Dependencies:** B-071 (the proxy/cert infrastructure this extends). **Out of scope, as specified:** per-agent authentication (see B-073); any change to what data the collector/agent report.

### B-073 — Per-agent authentication for eami-collector (replacing the shared API key) — **DONE, 2026-08-19**
**Objective:** B-072 closed the transport gap (encryption in transit) but every endpoint agent still authenticated with the same single, static `COLLECTOR_API_KEY`. Anyone who obtained that key through any means other than passive network sniffing (a compromised endpoint's own local config/registry, an insider) could impersonate any other agent to the collector.
**Re-verified directly against current code before designing anything, per this brief's own assumption to check rather than trust the prior investigation summary — confirmed still accurate:** `api_keys` had zero agent-identity linkage (`id, key_hash, label, created_at, revoked_at`), `RegisterKey` had zero callers anywhere, and production always set `COLLECTOR_API_KEY`, taking exclusive precedence over the dormant DB-backed path.
**Distribution mechanism: admin-generated key pool, chosen over self-enrollment, with reasoning.** B-053/B-072 already establish a per-machine install-command pattern (an admin generates a distinct install command per machine, exactly like B-072's `COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` parameter) — a key pool reuses that existing channel with zero new protocol. Self-enrollment was investigated and rejected: it would need its own new anti-abuse mechanism (a time-limited bootstrap window, or an admin-approval queue) just to avoid *moving* the "anyone can claim to be anyone" problem into a different form, for no real benefit given the install flow already requires per-machine admin action.
**`gateway_agents` investigated and correctly NOT reused** — it's a Postgres table in the SaaS DB modeling AI tool-calling agents (model/owner/risk_tier/token_ttl, used throughout B-039 onward); `eami-collector` is a separate on-prem SQLite service with no Postgres connection at all, so reusing it would be both conceptually wrong (the exact class of forced collision B-042/B-074 already taught this codebase to avoid) and technically infeasible.
**A genuine additional finding, not in the original scoping notes:** the report body's self-declared `agent_id` was never checked against anything — per-agent credentials alone would have been cosmetic without also rejecting a credential submitting a report under a mismatched `agent_id`. This is the actual mechanism that closes the impersonation gap, not just the schema change.
**Zero installer changes needed on any platform** — hostname is already the de facto agent identity everywhere today (Linux's postinstall bakes `$(hostname)` into `agent.yaml` directly; Windows/macOS fall back to it in `payload/builder.go`), so binding keys to hostname required no new install-time parameter.
**Schema (`eami-collector/internal/db/db.go`):** `api_keys` gained `agent_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` — baked into `CREATE TABLE` for fresh installs, idempotently backfilled via a new `ensureColumn` (PRAGMA table_info check + ALTER TABLE) for pre-existing DBs. New partial unique index `idx_api_keys_agent_active ON api_keys(agent_id) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL AND agent_id != ''` — one active key per agent, but revoke-then-reissue (rotation) for the same agent_id works since revoked rows are excluded.
**`internal/api/middleware.go`:** `RegisterKey` now requires/stores `agentID`; new `RevokeKey` (the real revocation path — previously `revoked_at` sat completely unused) and `ListKeys` (admin visibility). `APIKeyMiddleware` tries the legacy static key first (unchanged behavior — zero regression for anyone still using it), then falls through to a DB lookup resolving the key's bound `agent_id` into request context via a new `AgentIDFromContext`. `IngestHandler`/`ConfigProxyHandler` reject (403) when the authenticated request carries a bound identity that doesn't match the report body's/path's own claimed `agent_id` — the actual impersonation fix.
**Minting/revocation: new CLI subcommands on the existing `cmd/collector/main.go` binary** (`collector mint-key --agent-id X --label Y`, `revoke-key --agent-id X`, `list-keys`), dispatched from `os.Args[1]` before `flag.Parse()`. Runs via `docker compose exec eami-collector /app/collector mint-key ...` directly against the container's own data volume — no extra tools needed, unlike `scripts/create_api_key.sh` (needs `sqlite3`/`openssl`/`python3`, none present in the minimal Alpine runtime image; updated for `agent_id` parity as a host-side/dev alternative anyway, with its pre-existing unsafe shell-interpolation pattern hardened via `''`-quote-escaping for the two admin-supplied fields, a low-confidence finding flagged for awareness by the security review).
**Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security review: zero HIGH-confidence findings on the production Go surface — confirmed the identity-binding check has no bypass, the static-key path never leaks a bound identity, all new SQL is parameterized (including explicitly ruling out `ensureColumn`'s string-concatenated `ALTER TABLE`/`PRAGMA` as a real risk, since its table/column arguments are hardcoded Go literals with no path from user input), and revocation takes effect immediately with no caching/race. Code review found 2 real issues, both fixed: (1) `resolveDBPath` ignored the YAML config file's `buffer.db_path`, silently opening/creating a *different* SQLite file than the running server's own when a deployment configures the DB path via YAML instead of the `COLLECTOR_BUFFER_DB_PATH` env var — fixed with a new `--config`-aware `configuredOrDefaultDBPath`, matching `runServer`'s own exact precedence (env var > config file > hardcoded default), live-verified by pointing a throwaway config file at a custom path and confirming `mint-key` wrote there, not `./data/buffer.db`; (2) `revoke-key` exited `1` on "no active key to revoke," which would abort a `set -e`-style idempotent automation script on a perfectly normal outcome — fixed to exit `0`.
**Test coverage:** `eami-collector/internal/api/middleware_test.go` (11 tests: `RegisterKey`/`RevokeKey`/`ListKeys` correctness including rotation and duplicate-rejection, `APIKeyMiddleware`'s identity resolution, revoked-key rejection, static-key path unaffected, static+per-agent coexistence) and `ingest_test.go` (7 tests covering all 5 acceptance criteria at the real HTTP level, using a real on-disk SQLite DB via `db.Open` — the actual migration/index logic, not a hand-rolled schema). **Verified 2026-08-19: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-collector` module**, zero regressions in the pre-existing `forwarder` test suite.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before and after the code-review fixes):** two real agents minted via the real CLI (`docker exec` into the running container) and reporting via real `curl` POSTs with two genuinely different credentials — both `202` (AC1). A real impersonation attempt — Agent A's real credential submitting a report claiming `agent_id: live-verify-agent-b` — rejected with a real `403`, and confirmed via direct SQLite query that **zero trace** of the attempt landed in either `report_buffer` or `dead_letter` (AC2). The admin-generated-key-pool mechanism demonstrated live via the real CLI, gated behind container/shell access only — no network-exposed minting endpoint exists (AC3). The pre-existing legacy static `COLLECTOR_API_KEY` path confirmed still working, unaffected, for an unenrolled agent (AC4/contract). Agent A's key revoked live via `revoke-key`, and the very next attempt with that same key returned a real `401` — while Agent B's still-active key kept working unaffected, proving blast radius really is one agent (AC5). The config-proxy identity check verified live both ways (cross-agent fetch rejected `403`; own-agent fetch passes the check, reaching the expected downstream `404`/"not yet registered"). All live-verification keys revoked/cleaned up afterward; collector/API/Postgres containers confirmed healthy throughout.
**Dependencies:** B-072 (TLS — a per-agent credential now travels over an already-encrypted channel), B-074 (confirmed `gateway_agents` was solid before investigating whether to reuse it — it wasn't the right fit anyway, but the investigation needed a stable target to evaluate against). **Out of scope, as specified:** any change to what data agents report; AI-agent (`gateway_agents` tool-calling) identity itself.

### B-074 — Clean 409 on duplicate gateway_agents.(org_id, name) — **DONE, 2026-08-19**
**Objective:** task brief arrived assuming `gateway_agents.name` had no real per-org uniqueness enforcement and asked for a new migration to add `UNIQUE (org_id, name)`. **Re-verified directly against the live running database first, per the brief's own instruction to check rather than trust a prior investigation summary — the premise was wrong.** The constraint already exists and is already enforced (`gateway_agents_org_id_name_key`, confirmed live via `\d gateway_agents` against the running Postgres container, zero existing duplicate rows), declared identically in `schema/schema.sql`, `schema/migrations/001_init.sql`, and `schema/migrations-v2/000001_baseline.up.sql` (the file B-051's real migration runner applies). The brief's cited culprit, `resolveDynamicTool`, is unrelated — it resolves `gateway_tools` (B-044), not agent identity; the real agent-lookup code (`eami-gateway/internal/registry/registry.go`'s `LookupByName`/`LookupByNameAndOrg`) already documents this exact constraint from B-042 and is already correct. Confirmed with the user before building; scope narrowed to no migration, no `registry.go` changes.
**The one real gap:** `eami-api/internal/api/agents.go`'s `CreateAgent` didn't classify a Postgres unique-violation (23505) — any insert error fell through to a raw `500` with the leaked driver error string. Fixed with a new `isUniqueViolation` helper (mirrors `workflows.go`'s `isForeignKeyViolation`), returning a clean `409 conflict` for a duplicate name, unchanged 500 path for everything else.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security: zero findings (the new error message only echoes the caller's own already-submitted input, org-scoped by verified JWT claims — not a disclosure). Code review found 3 minor issues: `api/openapi.yaml`'s new 409 undocumented (correctly left to Architect-EAMI per `BOUNDARIES.md`, logged in `NOTES.md`, matching B-045's precedent) and 2 `gofmt` violations (fixed).
**Test coverage:** 3 unit tests for `isUniqueViolation`; 1 real-Postgres HTTP-level test proving a duplicate create gets 409 with the first row provably untouched (row count + re-fetched field check). **Verified 2026-08-19: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`** (full module, zero regressions); `eami-gateway` (untouched) confirmed still builds/vets clean.
**Live-verified** against the real shared dev Postgres container, test data confirmed cleaned up afterward.
**Dependencies:** none. **Out of scope, as specified (correctly, given the corrected premise):** any schema/migration change, `registry.go` changes, endpoint-agent identity (B-073, separate).

### B-075 — MCP Discovery Part C: OpenAPI auto-discovery for connector actions — **DONE, 2026-08-20**
**Objective:** let an admin register a `rest_api` connector's actions by supplying its real OpenAPI spec (upload/paste or URL) instead of hand-typing `action_paths` (B-046) entries one at a time.
**CRITICAL FIRST STEP, investigated before any design work:** confirmed B-061's `tools/list` is **not** compatible with a generic external MCP client (Claude Desktop or any spec-conformant SDK client) — `eami-gateway/internal/mcp/handler.go` has zero handling for the mandatory `initialize` handshake, and its tool-invocation method is the non-standard `tool_call`, not the real spec's `tools/call`. Both are independently fatal for a real client. Never built against real external clients before, only this codebase's own internal test harness. Per the brief's own scope boundary, NOT fixed here — logged as **B-076** below.
**A second finding, traced before designing the dispatch-facing half:** `toolrouter.Forward` has no path-parameter substitution and always wraps arguments in a fixed `{tool,action,params,session_id}` envelope — a spec-generated action against a real third-party API's native shape won't dispatch correctly regardless of parse quality. Disclosed via a per-action warning (both in the API response and the actual UI), not silently glossed over; live-proven by deliberately activating and dispatching a path-param action and confirming its predicted real `404`.
**What shipped:** new `eami-api/internal/openapidiscovery` package (`github.com/getkin/kin-openapi`) parses a spec into candidate `action_paths` entries with real per-action `input_schema`; new stateless `POST /v1/gateway/openapi/discover` (writes nothing); `ActionPathMapping`/`toolrouter.ActionPathEntry` gained an optional `InputSchema` field (dispatch-inert, decode-only); `listGatewayTools` surfaces it in `tools/list` when present, else keeps B-061's honest generic fallback. New `OpenAPIDiscoverySection` UI wired into both `AddToolPanel` and `EditToolPanel` — "Add selected" only merges into the existing local `action_paths` editor state; nothing activates until the admin's own pre-existing Save/Create click.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran, both found and this brief fixed real issues.** Security (MEDIUM, fixed): `spec_content`'s 5 MiB bound was enforced only after the full body was already buffered — fixed with `http.MaxBytesReader` ahead of decode, live-verified with a real 10MB+ POST. SSRF, YAML-bomb/recursion, injection, auth, XSS all traced and ruled out with concrete evidence (redirect re-dials through the guard; kin-openapi's `IsExternalRefsAllowed=false` blocks local-file/relative/remote `$ref`s at parse time, empirically verified; `SchemaRef` marshaling prefers `$ref` over inlining on repeat, bounding cyclic-schema recursion by design — independently reproduced live with a 10^9-scale YAML anchor structure). Code review (3 fixed): a stale `hasPathParams` UI flag now derives live from the row's current path; a parameter/body-property name collision that silently overwrote the parameter's schema now keeps it and warns explicitly; discovery wired into `AddToolPanel` too (was edit-only).
**Test coverage:** 18 `openapidiscovery` unit tests (including 3 real, empirically-verified `$ref`-rejection tests), 7 handler-level tests (including the security-fix regression), a real-Postgres `input_schema` round-trip test in `eami-gateway`. **Verified 2026-08-20: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api` and the relevant `eami-gateway` packages; real `npm run build`/`tsc --noEmit` clean.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real stack, rebuilt before and after the fixes:** SSRF guard confirmed genuinely active on the live endpoint (real loopback/link-local rejections) alongside a real successful public-spec fetch (Swagger Petstore); local-file `$ref` rejected live through the real endpoint; a real spec parsed → admin reviewed/selected 2 of 3 actions → activated via the existing PATCH → real `tools/list` over a real MCP SSE session showed the rich schema for the new actions **and** the pre-existing `b046-live-verify` connector's original generic fallback unchanged in the same response (AC3+AC5 together); real dispatch succeeded for both activated actions and failed exactly as predicted for the (deliberately tested) path-param one. Path-parameter warning confirmed present in the actual served UI module (browser automation unavailable this session, disclosed).
**Incidental finding during test cleanup, not fixed (out of scope):** `DeleteAgent` 500s (unclassified FK violation) for any agent with real episode/approval/workflow-run history — logged as **B-077** below.
**Dependencies:** B-044/046, B-061 (response shape unchanged). **Out of scope, as specified:** external MCP-client connectivity (B-076), `mcp`/`database`/`ai_provider` connector types, any B-044/046 dispatch-logic change.

### B-076 — External MCP-client connectivity (real `initialize`/`tools/call`)
**Objective:** B-075's investigation confirmed B-061's `tools/list` only works against this codebase's own internal test harness — a real external MCP client (Claude Desktop, any spec-conformant SDK client) cannot connect at all today. Needs: a real `initialize`/`notifications/initialized` handshake, renaming or dual-supporting `tool_call`→`tools/call`, and a real auth model an external client can actually obtain a credential through (today's Bearer JWT is minted via an internal-only `/v1/gateway/tokens` endpoint with no path a real remote client's user could reach). **Not scoped or sized yet — needs its own investigation before a brief is written**, per this repo's standing "investigate before scoping" convention.
**Severity:** Medium-High — this is the literal gap between "EAMI is a real MCP server" (the aspiration B-075's own task brief named) and current reality.

### B-077 — DeleteAgent 500s on an agent with real episode/approval/workflow-run history — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** `DELETE /v1/gateway/agents/{id}` (`eami-api/internal/api/agents.go`) issued a raw SQL `DELETE` with no FK-violation classification. `episodes.agent_id`/`approval_requests.agent_id`/`workflow_runs.agent_id` are all `ON DELETE NO ACTION` — any agent that ever made a real tool_call, was escalated, or ran a workflow could not be deleted via the API at all; it 500'd with a leaked raw Postgres error.
**Resolution (fixed as part of B-087, landed together):** did not cascade — losing episode/approval/workflow-run history on agent deletion would be wrong for a compliance-relevant audit trail, per this item's own original reasoning. Instead, `DeleteAgent` now classifies the FK violation (SQLSTATE `23503`) into a clean `409`, reusing `workflows.go`'s existing `isForeignKeyViolation` helper — same pattern as this item's own cited B-074 `isUniqueViolation` precedent. `status='suspended'` (already fully enforced at dispatch time, confirmed) is the real, working alternative the error message now points to. Live-verified against the exact original failure: a real episode seeded against a real agent, delete returned a real `409` not a `500`, agent survived untouched; removing the episode made delete succeed cleanly. Full writeup, including the new agent-lifecycle UI this fix was built alongside, in `BACKLOG.md`'s B-087 entry and `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api`/`eami-ui` sections.

### B-078 — Data-handling visibility for AI provider connectors — **DONE, 2026-08-20**
**Objective:** record, per `ai_provider` connector, what the customer's actual data-retention agreement with the provider is ("Zero Data Retention enabled" / "Standard retention (no ZDR)" / "Unknown/not configured") — visibility only, mirrors B-047's `audit_mode` pattern. EAMI cannot enforce what Claude/Gemini/OpenAI does with dispatched data; this makes the contractual reality visible and auditable within EAMI's own governance layer.
**Re-verified the prior investigation's precedent mapping directly against code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** `schema/migrations-v2/000004`'s structure, `aiprovider.ToolRow`/`main.go`'s shared `auditEntry` construction, and the audit hash-chain formula all matched exactly. One thing the investigation hadn't surfaced: no `eami-api` endpoint exposes `audit_log` reads at all today, so live verification of the audit-snapshot claim used direct `psql` reads plus a real, independently-recomputed hash-chain check, not a UI.
**Schema:** `gateway_tools` gains `data_handling_designation` (enum CHECK, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unknown'`) and `data_handling_note` (free text, nullable) — both structured and free-text, per the investigation's "both, not either" reasoning. `audit_log` gains a third, deliberately unconstrained/nullable copy — a per-call snapshot, not a live-validated field.
**What shipped:** `eami-api`'s `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool` validate against a new allowlist, defaulting to the fail-safe `"unknown"`, extending the same `ai_provider`-only type-consistency guard `provider`/`audit_mode` already use (closing on the first pass the exact class of gap B-047's own review caught afterward). `eami-gateway`'s `aiprovider.ToolRow` gains the field; `main.go`'s single shared `auditEntry` construction snapshots it once, at the same call site `AuditMode`'s redaction already uses — this is what makes the "historical records stay accurate" contract real. `eami-ui` gains a Data Handling dropdown + note field in both Add/Edit panels, plus a non-blocking amber badge on any `unknown`-designation connector, visible in the Tools table itself.
**Reviewer + security passes both came back with ZERO findings.** Security review's explicit, required task (per this brief's own standing rules) was independently re-deriving the hash-chain-exclusion claim from the actual code — confirmed `DataHandling` is genuinely absent from the hash content string, confirmed `InsertEntry` is a single parameterized `INSERT` with no `UPDATE`/`DELETE` anywhere in the diff, confirmed `GetLastHash` is unaffected. Code review re-traced every SQL column list against its Scan/args list for off-by-one errors and found none.
**Test coverage:** 16 handler tests + 2 real-Postgres round-trip tests in `eami-api`; a real `Resolve` round-trip test + 3 new `internal/audit` real-Postgres tests in `eami-gateway`, including a centerpiece test (two real `Write()` calls through the same long-lived Writer, second carrying a different designation, proving the first row's value survives untouched) and a real hash-chain-recomputation test (5 real rows, independently re-derived SHA-256 matching stored values). **Verified 2026-08-20: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across both modules; real `npm run build` clean.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real stack, migration applied via the real `golang-migrate` runner:** a real dispatch while designation was `unknown` wrote `unknown` into a real `audit_log` row; the connector's designation was then changed to `zero_retention` and dispatched again — the *first* row's `data_handling_designation` was confirmed via direct `psql` to still read `unknown`, completely unaltered, while the second correctly read `zero_retention`. Hash-chain integrity independently re-verified in a third language (Node.js) against the two real rows, byte-for-byte match. `audit_mode`'s existing redaction confirmed unchanged on both rows. UI badge conditional confirmed present in the real served module and evaluated correctly against real API data for two real connectors (pixel-level browser confirmation not performed, disclosed, standing limitation).
**Dependencies:** B-047 (`provider`/`audit_mode` pattern), B-074 (error-classification precedent). **Out of scope, as specified:** any technical enforcement (not possible), the dashboard widget (optional follow-up), redaction/tokenization (genuinely independent, confirmed unaffected).

### B-079 — Tools list row: hover affordance implies clickability but only the pencil icon opens EditToolPanel
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/ToolsPage.tsx`'s tools-table `<tr>` (`ToolsPage.tsx:866`) has `className="hover:bg-gray-50"`, which visually signals the row is clickable, but no `onClick` has ever been bound to it in any commit touching this file (confirmed via `git log -p --follow`) — only the pencil/Edit icon button (`ToolsPage.tsx:907`, `onClick={() => setEditTarget(tool)}`) actually opens `EditToolPanel`. Discovered live: a user reported "clicking a row does nothing," initially suspected as a B-062-style stale-serving issue; ruled out by fetching the actual served module from the running `eami-ui` container and confirming it matches source exactly (including B-078's data-handling badge, i.e. genuinely current) — the row really has never had a click handler.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Wire the row's `onClick` to `setEditTarget(tool)`, matching the pencil icon's existing behavior
- [ ] Icon buttons (Test, Edit/pencil, Remove/trash) call `e.stopPropagation()` in their `onClick` so they don't also trigger the new row-level handler (double-fire)
**Severity:** Low — real UX mismatch (misleading affordance), not urgent, no data-correctness or security impact.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-080 — Remove misleading "live Serf mesh cluster" claims from the Nodes page — **DONE, 2026-08-21**
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/NodesPage.tsx` presented its data as live infrastructure telemetry ("Serf mesh cluster -- refreshes every 15 s") when it's entirely fabricated — a customer-trust issue, not a feature gap.
**Context:** Found by the Dead Clicks Audit (the systematic UI-to-backend pass triggered by B-079). Re-verified directly before building, not assumed from the audit: `eami-api/internal/store/nodes.sql.go` has only `ListNodes`/`DeleteNode`, zero INSERT/UPSERT anywhere; zero `Serf`/`serf` references in any Go code across `eami-gateway`/`eami-api`; the only writer to `gateway_nodes` in the repo is `scripts/seed-db.sh:96`'s static demo `INSERT`. Corroborated by the already-open **B-043**, which found the identical false "broadcast via Serf" claim in `schema.sql:168`'s comment for a different feature.
**Resolution:** copy-only fix, three strings in `NodesPage.tsx` — subtitle, empty state, delete-confirm description — all reworded to state what's real (a table an admin can view/manually manage) without implying live multi-node telemetry or automatic failover that doesn't exist. Framing chosen (confirmed with the user before building): honest-but-functional, keeping the page's real, working Refresh/Delete plumbing visible and usable, not a "coming soon" empty state — see `BUILT.md`'s full entry for the reasoning.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean. Live-verified against the real stack: served module confirmed current via `curl` (zero "Serf" left), then Refresh (`GET /v1/gateway/nodes`) and Delete (`DELETE /v1/gateway/nodes/{id}`) both exercised against a real throwaway test row — both confirmed still working, no regression.
**Dependencies:** none for this fix. **B-043** remains open, separate, future work — real multi-node registration/heartbeat was explicitly out of scope here.

Same bug class as B-079 (Tools), found by the Dead Clicks Audit. The "Configure" button's own `onClick` now calls `e.stopPropagation()` first.

**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/PoliciesPage.tsx`'s policy-table `<tr>` had `className="hover:bg-gray-50"` with no `onClick` bound — only the pencil/Edit icon opened the edit panel. Same bug class as B-079, found by the Dead Clicks Audit.

**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s workflow-table `<tr>` had `className="hover:bg-gray-50"` with no `onClick` bound — only the pencil/Edit icon opened `EditWorkflowPanel`. Same bug class as B-079, found by the Dead Clicks Audit.

### B-084 — Audit list row: hover affordance implies clickability, but no detail view exists to open
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/ops/AuditPage.tsx`'s audit-table `<tr>` (`AuditPage.tsx:196`) has `className="hover:bg-gray-50"` with no `onClick` bound. Found by the Dead Clicks Audit, worse than the row-click findings above: unlike Tools/Agents/Policies/Workflows, there is no existing per-row detail view/drawer anywhere in this file for a click to open — this isn't a missing handler on an existing feature, it's a hover style with nothing built behind it at all.
**Acceptance criteria:**

### B-085 — Paste Detection list row: hover affordance implies clickability with nothing to show even if fixed — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/ops/PasteEventsPage.tsx`'s event-table `<tr>` had `className="hover:bg-gray-50"` with no `onClick` bound. Found by the Dead Clicks Audit, which recommended removing the affordance rather than wiring a click, since the page is deliberately read-only by design.
**Re-verified before fixing, not assumed:** traced the real data model end to end (not just the frontend type) — the real backend response struct (`eami-api/internal/api/types.go` `PasteEventResp`) has exactly the six fields already rendered as table columns (`id`/`destination_domain`/`occurred_at`/`content_length`/`content_hash`/`os_username`); the two additional `paste_events` schema columns (`org_id`, `source_endpoint_id`) are never exposed by the read handler at all. `PasteEventResp`'s own doc comment states directly: "there is no raw-content column anywhere upstream of this struct, so it cannot expose pasted text even by accident." **Confirmed: nothing a click could reveal that isn't already visible in the row — "remove" is the correct fix, not "build a detail view."**
**Resolution:** one-line change — `<tr key={e.id} className="hover:bg-gray-50">` → `<tr key={e.id}>`. No `onClick` added, no `cursor-pointer` (never had one, correctly). `HashCell`'s copy button, filters, and pagination are completely untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`: 1 file, 1 line changed).
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. Live-verified via served-module `curl` (the B-062 lesson): confirmed zero `hover:bg-gray-50` remains anywhere in the served module, the row `<tr>` renders with no `className` at all, and `HashCell`/`handleCopy` are byte-identical and still correctly wired. The real `GET /v1/paste-events` endpoint (backing filters/pagination) confirmed unaffected via a direct live call.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-086 — Policies drag-handle icon not wired to the real, tested reorder endpoint — governance correctness issue — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Priority:** **HIGH — not cosmetic.** Policy evaluation order determines which rule fires first/wins on an overlapping match — an admin who drags policies expecting to change evaluation order and sees nothing happen has a real, silent governance-correctness gap, not just a UI nit.
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/gateway/PoliciesPage.tsx`'s `GripVertical` icon (`:341-343`) strongly implied drag-to-reorder but did nothing. Found by the Dead Clicks Audit.
**Two real bugs found before the UI fix could even be proven, neither known when this item was originally logged:**
1. **`useReorderPolicies()` sent the wrong request body key.** `usePolicies.ts` POSTed `{order: [...]}`, but the real handler (`eami-api/internal/api/types.go:167`, `PolicyReorderRequest`) expects `policy_ids` — proven live, the real endpoint 400'd on the exact body the hook sent. Root cause: `api/openapi.yaml:1260-1265` itself documents `order`, out of sync with the real Go implementation — the frontend's generated-client precedent had faithfully followed a wrong spec. Fixed in `usePolicies.ts` (now sends `policy_ids` via `apiFetch`, not the generated client, since the generated types follow the wrong spec); the spec mismatch itself is out of this brief's scope (`openapi.yaml` is Architect-EAMI-owned) — logged separately as **B-089**.
2. **`ReorderPolicies` (`eami-api/internal/store/policies.sql.go`) 500'd on ordinary reorder attempts**, including the exact adjacent-pair swap an up/down click produces. Root cause: `policies`' `UNIQUE (org_id, priority)` constraint is declared `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` (`schema.sql:191`) specifically so a full reorder is only checked at commit — but the function looped plain `q.db.Exec` calls with no transaction, so each row auto-committed individually and the deferral never applied. Reproduced live (`SQLSTATE 23505`) before touching anything. **Fixed by wrapping the loop in a real transaction** via the codebase's existing `Queries.Begin()`/`WithTx()` pattern (same shape as `bootstrap.go`'s only other user of it) — no schema change, no change to the endpoint's request/response contract or the policy evaluation engine.
**Resolution, UI:** `PoliciesPage.tsx`'s `GripVertical` replaced with up/down move buttons (matching `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s `StepsEditor` precedent — no drag-and-drop library added, consistent with B-069's DnD-library-removal history) calling the fixed `useReorderPolicies()` with the org's **full** policy-id list in the new order every time (not just the two swapped ids) — required, since the endpoint renumbers priority sequentially from array position for every id it's given; a partial submission would leave untouched policies' priorities stale and risking collisions.
**Test coverage:** new `eami-api/internal/api/policies_reorder_pg_test.go` (real-Postgres, `t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle per `CLAUDE.md`'s mandatory pattern) — one test proving the exact adjacent-pair-swap shape that used to 500 now succeeds and persists correctly; one test proving a genuinely-invalid final state (not just an old-bug artifact) still correctly fails and rolls back with zero partial state. Full `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`.
**Reviewer + security passes, mandatory per this brief (real enforcement behavior):** security review: zero findings — the transaction wrap only changes atomicity around already-parameterized, already org-scoped SQL, no new attack surface. Code review: two real, lower-severity findings, disclosed not fixed here (narrow, out of this brief's approved scope) — logged as **B-090**.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, the centerpiece proof (AC2):** two real throwaway policies (`deny` priority 1000, `allow` priority 1001, identical `tool_names` condition) created via the real admin API. A real `tool_call` dispatched via a real agent JWT + real MCP SSE session — confirmed **deny wins** (`-32600 "Request denied by policy"`, correct `policy_id`, real `audit_log` row `decision=denied`). Reordered via the real, fixed endpoint (full org policy list, adjacent-pair swap — the exact shape that 500'd pre-fix, confirmed succeeding post-fix). The **identical** `tool_call` dispatched again on a fresh session — confirmed **allow wins**: a real response from `postman-echo.com` came back, real `audit_log` row `decision=allowed` with the allow policy's id. **This is the real, literal proof that reordering through the UI's real request shape changes real enforcement outcomes**, not just that the API call succeeds. All throwaway policies deleted afterward; only the two original real policies remain (their priority *numbers* changed from 1/99 to 1/2 as a side effect of being included in the full-list test call — relative order and enforcement behavior unchanged, disclosed not hidden).
**Dependencies:** none. **B-089** (openapi.yaml spec-drift) and **B-090** (ReorderPolicies deadlock-risk-under-concurrency + N-round-trips) are new, separate, low-priority follow-ups discovered during this brief, not blocking.

### B-087 — Agents page has no create/suspend/delete UI despite real, tested backend endpoints — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** `AgentsPage.tsx` had no "Add agent" button, no delete action, no suspend toggle, despite real, tested backend endpoints for all three. Fixed alongside **B-077** (delete-with-history), landing together per this item's own original dependency note.
**B-077's real root cause, fixed:** `DeleteAgent` (`eami-api/internal/api/agents.go`) issued a raw SQL `DELETE` with no FK-violation classification — `episodes`/`approval_requests`/`workflow_runs`.`agent_id` are all default `NO ACTION` (deliberately not cascaded: losing that history on agent deletion would be wrong for a compliance-relevant audit trail). Fixed by classifying the FK violation (`23503`) into a clean `409` — "cannot delete an agent with existing episode, approval, or workflow-run history -- suspend it instead" — reusing `workflows.go`'s existing `isForeignKeyViolation` helper, same pattern as B-074's `isUniqueViolation` precedent.
**Suspend, confirmed to already be fully real and enforced — zero schema/gateway change needed:** `gateway_agents.status` already has a working `CHECK (status IN ('active','suspended','revoked'))`; `eami-gateway/internal/registry/registry.go` already rejects `suspended`/`revoked` agents (`ErrAgentSuspended`), and `internal/mcp/handler.go` already turns that into a real `403` at SSE session creation. Suspend was only ever missing its UI trigger. Live-verified: suspending a real agent produced a genuine `403 agent not registered or suspended: ... status=suspended` on the very next new-session attempt — immediate, not eventually-consistent. Disclosed precisely, not overclaimed: this blocks new sessions only; an already-open session isn't killed mid-flight (`registry` lookup runs at session creation, not per-message).
**A false premise in the task brief, found by investigation and confirmed with the user before building against it:** the brief assumed lifecycle actions should write to `audit_log`, "consistent with how this session has treated every other meaningful admin action." Grepped every existing admin-action handler (`policies.go`/`tools.go`/`approvals.go`/`agents.go`) for an `audit_log` write — zero exist. `audit_log` is written exclusively by `eami-gateway`'s hash-chained dispatch-decision path, never by `eami-api`, and no existing admin CRUD action (policy/tool/approval) logs to it either. **Resolution, confirmed with the user:** new, small, separate `agent_lifecycle_events` table (migration `000009`) — explicitly not a write into the hash-chained ledger, a different concern (admin-action history) with its own simple schema (`org_id, agent_id, agent_name, event_type, performed_by, detail, created_at`, no FK to `gateway_agents` so a delete event survives the row it describes). `CreateAgent`/`UpdateAgent` (suspend/reactivate only)/`DeleteAgent` (success path) each insert one row, best-effort (mirrors `NotifyPolicyReload`'s established "side effect must not fail the primary action" convention).
**What shipped, `eami-ui`:** `AgentsPage.tsx` gains an "Add agent" panel (react-hook-form + zod, matching `ConfigPanel`'s existing structure), a per-row Suspend/Reactivate button, and a per-row Delete button with `ConfirmDialog` — all three new in-row buttons `stopPropagation`-guarded against B-081's row-click. A dismissible error banner surfaces the real backend message on a failed action (e.g. the new 409's actionable text) instead of a generic failure.
**A real bug found by the mandatory code-review pass, fixed before shipping:** the blocked-delete error was being set correctly but was structurally invisible — `ConfirmDialog` is a `fixed inset-0 z-50` full-screen overlay, and the error banner rendered in normal page flow underneath it, completely hidden while the dialog (deliberately kept open on error, per the code's own intent) stayed up. Fixed by rendering the error inside `ConfirmDialog`'s own `children` slot instead, no change to the shared component needed. **Two more findings, disclosed not fixed here:** `ConfirmDialog.tsx` declares an `isLoading` prop that its own implementation never uses (Confirm button never disables mid-request) — a pre-existing gap in the shared component affecting every page that already passes it (Policies/Workflows/Approvals, now Agents too), logged separately as **B-091** rather than side-patched into a shared component outside this brief's file scope. A single page-level `useUpdateAgent()`/`useDeleteAgent()` instance means `isPending` is shared across all rows (suspending one agent briefly disables every other row's Suspend button) — matches this codebase's own existing precedent (`PoliciesPage`'s `reorder.isPending`, B-086), an accepted tradeoff, not fixed.
**Test coverage:** new `TestDeleteAgent_RealDB_WithRealHistory_Returns409NotRawError` in `agents_pg_test.go` (real Postgres, `t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle) reproduces B-077's exact original failure — a real episode row referencing a real agent — and proves the clean `409`, that the agent survives a blocked delete untouched, that no spurious `deleted` lifecycle row is written for a delete that didn't happen, and that suspend still works as the real alternative. **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`** (full suite, zero regressions); real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean.
**Reviewer + security passes, mandatory per this brief (real agent-identity lifecycle/access change).** Security: zero findings — the new insert is fully parameterized, `PerformedBy` comes from authenticated JWT claims never client input, no authorization logic touched, the classified 409 is a disclosure *reduction* (no more leaked raw driver error) not an increase. Code review: three real findings, all addressed above (one fixed, two disclosed/logged).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt fresh:** AC1 — a real agent created via the real endpoint, immediately usable (a real MCP SSE session opened successfully with a freshly minted token for it). AC2 — suspended via the real endpoint, a real new SSE-session attempt for the same agent returned a real `403` with the exact registry-level rejection message. AC3 — B-077's exact scenario reproduced live (a real episode seeded against a real agent), delete returned a real `409`, not a `500`; removing the episode and retrying produced a real `204`. AC4 — `agent_lifecycle_events` confirmed via direct `psql` to hold exactly `created`/`suspended`/`deleted` rows for the test agent, correct `performed_by`, zero spurious `deleted` row from the earlier blocked attempt. AC5 — `GetAgentConfig` (Configure's real data source, untouched code) confirmed still returns `200`; the row `onClick`/`stopPropagation` wiring from B-081 confirmed unchanged via served-module `curl`. All throwaway resources (test agent, seeded episode) deleted afterward.
**Dependencies:** **B-077**, closed together with this item. **B-091** (new, `ConfirmDialog.isLoading`) is separate, not blocking. One additional minor observation, not logged as its own item (no actual bug — disclosed only): `api/openapi.yaml`'s `AgentCreate.risk_tier` enum documents `[low, medium, high]`, but the real backend's `validRiskTiers` also accepts `critical` — this brief's Add-agent form deliberately offers only the three documented values, so no user-facing gap exists; noted for whoever next touches that spec.

### B-088 — Approvals "All" tab pagination is stuck on page 1 — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/pages/ops/ApprovalsPage.tsx:212`'s `const [allPage] = useState(1)` never destructured a setter, so the "All" tab's server-side query (`useApprovals({ page: allPage, per_page: 25 })`) could never request page 2 or beyond. If an org's total approvals exceed 25, everything past the first page was silently unreachable through the UI.
**A correction to the original audit's own characterization, found by re-reading `DataTable.tsx` before fixing, not assumed:** the "All" tab's pager wasn't just "stuck on page 1" — it was **structurally invisible whenever total approvals exceed 25**. `DataTable` computes its own `totalPages` purely from the length of the `data` array it's handed (`Math.ceil(data.length / pageSize)`), designed for client-side pagination over an already-fully-loaded array. `allData` is already server-paginated down to at most 25 rows before `DataTable` ever sees it, so `totalPages` was always exactly `1` and `DataTable`'s own `{totalPages > 1 && ...}` pager never rendered at all — not "controls that don't work," but no controls, no error, no indication anything was missing.
**Resolution:** `allPage` now has a real setter. `DataTable`'s own (structurally incompatible) pager is left unused for this tab — a new, separate pagination control was added directly in `ApprovalsPage.tsx` below the table, driven by the server's real reported `meta.total` (the same field already powering the Pending tab's count badge), matching this codebase's established pattern for server-paginated list pages (Audit/Paste Detection already build their own Previous/Next outside `DataTable` rather than through it, for the same reason).
**Live-verified against the real running stack, per the brief's own requirement to prove real records on page 2, not just that the click works:** seeded 25 throwaway `approval_requests` rows directly via `psql` (data only, no schema/backend change) with timestamps newer than the org's 7 real pre-existing approvals — confirmed via the real `GET /v1/approvals` that `meta.total` reports `32`, page 1 returns exactly the 25 seeded rows, and page 2 returns exactly the 7 real original approvals (2 `claude`, 5 `manual_verify_tool`), genuinely different records. Confirmed via the served module (`curl`'d, not assumed) that `setAllPage`/the new pagination block are exactly what's deployed. All 25 seeded rows deleted afterward; confirmed back to the original 7. Pending tab uses a completely independent query (`useApprovals({status:'pending'})`, no `page`/`allPage` involved) — unaffected by construction, confirmed via direct code read.
**Test coverage:** no automated frontend test added — matches this repo's existing 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006), consistent with every other `eami-ui` brief's precedent; verification is the live proof above.
**Severity, resolved:** was Medium (real, silent data-reachability bug). No regression to the Pending tab or page 1 of the "All" tab.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-089 — `api/openapi.yaml`'s reorder-policies request schema documents the wrong field name
**Objective:** `/v1/gateway/policies/reorder`'s documented request body (`api/openapi.yaml:1258-1265`) requires `order`, but the real handler (`eami-api/internal/api/types.go:166-168`, `PolicyReorderRequest`) expects `policy_ids`. Discovered during B-086 when the frontend hook, faithfully generated against the documented `order` field, 400'd against the real running endpoint.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Update `openapi.yaml`'s schema to `policy_ids`, matching the real implementation (the frontend was already fixed in B-086 to send the real field name directly, bypassing the generated client for this one call — once the spec is corrected, that workaround could be reverted back to the generated `api.POST` client, though that reversion isn't required)
**Severity:** Low — the frontend workaround already closes the practical impact; this is a documentation-accuracy gap. Owner: Architect-EAMI (per `BOUNDARIES.md`, `openapi.yaml` changes are theirs, matching B-017's precedent).
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-090 — `ReorderPolicies`'s new transaction introduces a narrow concurrent-reorder deadlock risk; also does N sequential round-trips — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** B-086's mandatory code-review pass (reviewing the fix that wraps `ReorderPolicies` in a transaction) found two real, lower-severity issues in the fix itself, disclosed rather than fixed in that brief (narrow, out of its approved scope — "wrap in a transaction," not "redesign the query").
**Re-verified directly against current code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** confirmed `ReorderPolicies` still ran N sequential single-row `UPDATE`s wrapped in an explicit `tx.Begin/Rollback/Commit`, unchanged since B-086.
**What shipped:** `eami-api/internal/store/policies.sql.go`'s `ReorderPolicies` rewritten as one batched `UPDATE ... FROM (SELECT unnest($1::uuid[]) AS id, unnest($2::int[]) AS priority) AS v WHERE p.id = v.id AND p.org_id = $3` — a fixed 3-parameter shape regardless of `len(ids)`, no dynamic SQL string-building. **The explicit transaction wrapper was removed entirely**, not just shrunk — investigated empirically before deciding, not assumed: ran a real 2-item adjacent swap and a real 3-item rotation directly against `psql` as bare single statements with no surrounding `BEGIN`, both landed correctly with zero spurious unique-violations. B-086's original bug required N *separate* auto-committing statements for an intermediate state to trip the `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint; a single `UPDATE...FROM` statement computes every row's new value from one consistent read of old data before writing, so there's no second command for an intermediate state to be checked against — Postgres validates the statement's net effect atomically regardless of whether an outer transaction wraps it. This achieves the maximum possible round-trip reduction (N+2 round trips → 1), not just N → fewer.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes both ran and both found real issues, all fixed:**
- **Security review:** confirmed sound — array-parameter binding via pgx's extended protocol (never string-spliced), org isolation from the `WHERE p.org_id = $3` clause alone (verified this is the *only* enforcement on the production path, see next point), and no injection/DoS vector in the batched query itself. Flagged two Low findings, both fixed: (1) a stale comment claiming a handler-side per-id org pre-check exists — traced and confirmed **false on the production path** (`s.queries != nil` branch in `policies.go` calls `ReorderPolicies` directly with no per-id loop; that loop exists only on the mock-backed test path) — comment rewritten to state the WHERE clause is the sole enforcement, not a backstop for a check that doesn't run. (2) Duplicate IDs in the request produce Postgres's documented *arbitrary* pick between competing source rows (`UPDATE...FROM` semantics) where the old N-loop was deterministic last-write-wins — closed by rejecting duplicate `policy_ids` outright with a 400 in the handler (`policies.go`'s `ReorderPolicies`), rather than accepting silent nondeterminism on an authorization-relevant ordering.
- **Code review:** independently re-derived the transaction-removal reasoning from Postgres's `DEFERRABLE`/autocommit semantics rather than trusting the comment, confirmed sound. Caught one real overclaim: an earlier version of this comment said the fix "closes B-090's deadlock-window finding by construction, not just narrows it" — false, since two concurrent single-statement calls can still acquire row locks in opposite order and deadlock (`40P01`), which is exactly why the new concurrency test explicitly tolerates a real `40P01` rather than asserting it never happens. Fixed: comment now says the window is *narrowed* to one statement's execution time, not eliminated. Also caught sqlc annotation drift (`-- name: ReorderPoliciesBatched` in the query doc while the actual Go function stayed `ReorderPolicies`) that would silently break both real call sites on a future `sqlc generate` — fixed by aligning the annotation to `ReorderPolicies`.
- Two further code-review findings — no retry on the accepted `40P01` deadlock (surfaces as a 500 with the raw driver error text) and the deeper question of whether `model_pricing`-style admin-CRUD-adjacent systemic error-message hygiene needs its own pass — were **not** fixed here (broader than this brief's one-function scope, touches the app-wide `writeError(...,"internal_error", err.Error())` convention) and logged fresh as **B-117** below.
**Test coverage:** new `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_ConcurrentOverlappingReorders_NoCorruption` (`policies_reorder_pg_test.go`) — 20 iterations of two goroutines concurrently calling `ReorderPolicies` with an id list and its full reversal, asserting any error is either nil or a genuine `40P01` (accepted, not corruption) and that `ListPolicies` always shows exactly N distinct priorities in `1..N` with zero duplicates afterward. New `TestReorderPolicies_DuplicateID_BadRequest` (`policies_test.go`, mock-backed) for the security-review dedup fix. **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-api`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions — including both pre-existing `policies_reorder_pg_test.go` tests (`AdjacentSwap`, `GenuineCollision`) re-run clean after the transaction removal.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** the real 4-policy Dev Org list (2 pre-existing + 2 throwaway test policies) reordered via a real `POST /v1/gateway/policies/reorder` swapping the last two — real 200, correct final priorities confirmed via direct `psql`, zero spurious unique-violation — the exact B-086 bug shape, proven end-to-end through the real HTTP API, not just the Go test. Re-run again post-rebuild (after the security/code-review fixes) with the same result. The new duplicate-ID rejection also live-verified: a real request with a repeated id returned a real 400 with the expected message. All fixtures cleaned up afterward.
**Dependencies:** B-086 (the fix this hardens). New discovered work: **B-117** (deadlock error-text/retry, disclosed not fixed).

### B-091 — `ConfirmDialog`'s `isLoading` prop is declared but never used — Confirm button never disables during a pending mutation — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** `eami-ui/src/components/common/ConfirmDialog.tsx`'s `ConfirmDialogProps` interface declares `isLoading?: boolean`, and at least four pages (Policies, Workflows, Approvals, and now Agents — B-087) already pass it expecting the Confirm button to disable/show a pending state while the mutation is in flight — but the component's function signature never destructures `isLoading` at all, and the Confirm `<button>` has no `disabled` attribute tied to anything. A double-click on Confirm fires the mutation twice.
**Re-verified directly against current code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** confirmed unchanged since logged — `isLoading` was still absent from the destructured props and the button JSX.
**What shipped:** `ConfirmDialog` now destructures `isLoading = false`, applies `disabled={isLoading}` to **both** Cancel and Confirm buttons (not just Confirm — cancelling mid-flight is the same class of race), and shows a `RefreshCw`-spin icon on Confirm while loading, matching `SettingsPage.tsx`'s existing `SaveButton` convention exactly (`animate-spin` + `disabled:opacity-60`) rather than inventing a new loading-state visual language. Zero prop-shape change, so every existing `isLoading={x.isPending}` caller (Policies/Workflows/Approvals/Agents) started working with no changes on their end.
**Test coverage:** real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build`, both clean (Node/npm confirmed genuinely available this session, not a one-off — see `CLAUDE.md`'s updated toolchain note). Live verification: served-module confirmation via the running `eami-ui` dev container (`claude-in-chrome` confirmed not connected this session, so a real click-through wasn't available — stated plainly, not overclaimed).
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-092 — Cross-page deep-linking/highlighting by ID (Agents/Policies/Approvals) — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** Let a link from elsewhere in the app (e.g. a future Audit detail panel's `agent_id`/`policy_id`/`approval_id` references) land on and highlight the specific row it points to, not just navigate to the list page.
**Re-verified directly against current code before building:** confirmed unchanged since logged — still only `SettingsPage.tsx`/`AlertsPage.tsx` used `useSearchParams`, both for tab selection.
**A real architectural wrinkle found during re-verification, not in the original investigation:** `ApprovalsPage.tsx` is genuinely more complex than Agents/Policies. Its default "pending" tab isn't a `DataTable` at all (a card list), and a highlighted approval could be decided/expired — not pending. Its "all" tab is **server-paginated** (25/page via `useApprovals`), unlike Agents/Policies' fully client-loaded `pageSize={1000}`.
**What shipped:** `DataTable.tsx` (shared by all three post-B-104) gains `getRowId`/`highlightRowId` props — assigns a stable `id` attribute per row, applies an amber highlight ring, auto-scrolls it into view, and auto-advances `page` to whichever page actually contains the match (relevant mainly for Approvals). `AgentsPage.tsx`/`PoliciesPage.tsx` read `?highlight=<id>` via `useSearchParams` and pass it straight through — `pageSize={1000}` already made pagination a non-issue there. `ApprovalsPage.tsx`: a deep link forces the "all" tab and fetches with `per_page: 1000` instead of 25 (same "make pagination a non-issue" convention as Agents/Policies) rather than building a new find-which-server-page-has-this-id backend capability.
**Test coverage:** real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build`, both clean. Live verification: served-module confirmation (same `claude-in-chrome`-not-connected disclosure as B-091).
**Dependencies:** none. Consuming this from B-084's Audit detail panel (linking `agent_id`/`policy_id`/`approval_id` references to `?highlight=`) is explicitly out of scope here — this brief builds the capability, not its first real consumer.

### B-093 — `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` linkage in `audit_log` — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** Let an `audit_log` row indicate whether (and where) it was part of a multi-hop workflow run, so the audit trail can show a governed call's workflow context instead of looking identical to a standalone call.
**Re-verified directly against current code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** confirmed `audit_log` still had no `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` anywhere. Traced the exact plumbing gap precisely: `mcp.ActionContext` (shared by both the standalone MCP handler and `workflow/executor.go`'s `runStep`) had zero workflow fields, so `dispatcher.go`'s `auditEntry` construction had no way to know it was inside a run.
**What shipped:** `schema/migrations-v2/000012_audit_workflow_linkage.up.sql` — `workflow_run_id UUID`/`step_index INTEGER`, both nullable, **no FK** (matches `audit_log`'s own existing convention exactly — `policy_id`/`approval_id`/`data_handling_designation` are all plain FK-less columns already). `mcp.ActionContext` gains `WorkflowRunID string`/`StepIndex *int32`; `executor.go`'s `runStep` sets both right alongside its existing `stepAC.Tool =`/`.Action =` assignments; `audit.Entry`/`writer.go`'s `InsertEntry` thread them through as two more nullable columns — **deliberately NOT added to the hash `content` string**, same B-078 `DataHandling` precedent, confirmed directly against that code before writing this migration, not assumed.
**A real, previously-masked pre-existing bug found while writing this brief's own tests, not introduced by it:** `eami-gateway/internal/workflow`'s shared test helper `allowAllRules()` uses the literal string `"allow-all"` as its rule ID — but `audit_log.policy_id` is a real `uuid` column, so a matched-rule audit write with that ID fails with `invalid input syntax for type uuid`. Every existing test using this helper has silently swallowed this exact write failure (`_ = auditWriter.Write(...)`, discarding the error) since none of them previously read back an `audit_log` row on the allow path — B-093's own new tests were the first to actually check, and hit it immediately. Confirmed test-fixture-only (a real loaded policy's ID from the `policies` table is always a genuine UUID in production) — worked around locally in the two new tests via a UUID-ID rule set (`allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()`), not fixed in the shared helper (would touch every other passing test's fixture for no benefit). Logged fresh as **B-115** below.
**Test coverage:** `audit/writer_pg_test.go` gains `TestWriter_RealDB_WorkflowLinkage_PersistsCorrectly` (round trip, including a real "step 0" surviving as non-NULL, distinct from "absent") and `TestWriter_RealDB_HashChainRemainsValid_WithWorkflowLinkage` (AC4 — mirrors B-078's own `..._WithDataHandling` test exactly: independently recomputes the chain from stored columns using the documented formula, proving the new columns aren't silently included). `workflow/executor_test.go` gains `TestExecutor_Run_AuditLogRecordsWorkflowRunIDAndStepIndex` (AC3 centerpiece — a real 2-step run's two real dispatches each produce a correctly-tagged `audit_log` row) and `TestExecutor_Run_StandaloneDispatch_AuditLogWorkflowLinkageIsNull` (a non-workflow dispatch's row is genuinely NULL). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module**, zero regressions; `schema/migrationtest`'s full suite (fresh/incremental parity, idempotent re-run, seeded-DB upgrade) passes with the new migration in place.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** a real 2-step workflow (`rest_api` connectors to `postman-echo.com`) dispatched via a real agent JWT against `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run` — the resulting real `audit_log` rows confirmed via direct `psql` to carry the exact `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` (0, 1) (AC3). A real standalone `tool_call` through the same agent confirmed a genuinely NULL `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` row. **AC4 hash-chain re-verification done live, not just in the Go test:** pulled the 3 real rows this session's live dispatches produced, independently recomputed each hash in bash (`sha256sum`, using Go's exact `RFC3339`-without-fractional-seconds timestamp formatting) against the documented formula — all 3 matched exactly, confirming the new columns are genuinely excluded from the chain on real production-shaped data. `audit_log` rows deliberately **not deleted** afterward (append-only by design — RLS `FOR INSERT` only, no `DELETE` policy; same "keep as real audit trail evidence" precedent as prior live-verification sessions) — every other fixture (API key, workflow, 2 connectors) was cleaned up; the agent was suspended rather than hard-deleted (real audit/workflow-run history).
**Dependencies:** B-078 (hash-chain-exclusion precedent), B-063 (workflow execution engine, `runStep`'s call site).

### B-115 — `eami-gateway/internal/workflow`'s `allowAllRules()` test helper uses a non-UUID rule ID, silently breaking `audit_log` writes on the allow path — **DONE, 2026-08-27**
**Objective:** found while writing B-093's own tests (see that entry above for the full trace) — `allowAllRules()`'s literal `Rule{ID: "allow-all", ...}` isn't a valid UUID, but `audit_log.policy_id` is a real `uuid` column. Any allow-path dispatch that matches this rule and then tries to write its `policy_id` into `audit_log` fails with `invalid input syntax for type uuid`. Every existing test using this shared helper has silently absorbed this exact failure (`_ = auditWriter.Write(...)`), since none of them previously asserted anything about the resulting `audit_log` row.
**Severity going in:** Low — test-fixture-only, confirmed not a production concern (a real loaded policy's ID from the `policies` table is always a genuine UUID).
**What shipped:** `allowAllRules()`'s rule `ID` changed from `"allow-all"` to `uuid.NewString()` (kept `Name: "allow-all"`) — confirmed via repo-wide grep no test asserted against the literal ID string. New test `TestExecutor_Run_AllowPath_UsingAllowAllRules_AuditLogRowPersists` proves a real `audit_log` row now persists with the correct `decision`/`policy_id` on the allow path, using `allowAllRules()` directly (not B-093's `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()` workaround). Per the task brief's explicit MUST NOT MODIFY ("any other test helper"), `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()` and its 2 callers were left untouched even though this fix makes it redundant — logged fresh as **B-123** below.
**A real, disclosed side effect found by this brief's own mandatory security-review pass, not fixed here:** `audit_log` has no FK to `orgs` (deliberate, append-only design) and every pre-existing test using `allowAllRules()` now succeeds at writing a real row where it previously failed silently — meaning every `go test ./internal/workflow/...` run now leaves permanent orphaned rows in the shared dev `audit_log` once the test's own org is deleted by `t.Cleanup`. Measured live: one test run took matching rows from 43 to 59. Deleting them isn't a safe reflexive fix (`audit_log` is a tamper-evident hash chain — removing a mid-chain row breaks `verify-audit-log.sh`), so this needs a real design decision (isolated test DB, or accept-and-document), not a quick patch — logged fresh as **B-122** below.
