**Test coverage:** re-ran the full `eami-gateway` module (`go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...`) against a real Postgres, zero regressions, including `TestExecutor_Run_AllStepsAllowed_ExecutesInOrder`/`TestExecutor_Run_MiddleStepDenied_StopsRun` (pre-existing tests using `allowAllRules()`, still pass, now with a real underlying audit write instead of a silently-discarded failure).
**Dependencies:** none. Found by, not blocking, B-093 (worked around locally there via `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()`). New discovered work: **B-122** (orphaned audit_log rows), **B-123** (redundant helper cleanup).

### B-094 — Audit entry detail view, with honest hash-chain verification — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** Real detail view for Audit page rows, closing B-084 (previously correctly left unclickable per B-081/082/083's scope decision, since no detail view existed yet). Built directly from the B-084 investigation's findings.
**What shipped:** slide-out `AuditEntryDetailPanel.tsx` opened by a real row `onClick`, showing full `parameters`/`hash`/`prev_hash`/raw ids (all already sent by `GET /v1/audit`, just never rendered), live-resolved policy/approval names via `GetPolicy`/`GetApproval`, and the newly-added `data_handling_designation` (see `eami-api` change below). Full writeup in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api`/`eami-ui` sections.
**The one real backend change:** `data_handling_designation` (B-078's column) added to `ListAudit`'s SELECT, `store.AuditEntry`, and `AuditEntryResp` — confirmed genuinely absent before this brief, not assumed.
**The differentiating piece — two hash-chain claims, deliberately never blurred:** an always-on, client-side-only **"Self-consistent (this entry only)"** indicator (explicitly worded to disclaim the full-chain guarantee), and a separate **"Verify chain to this entry"** button calling the real, unmodified `GET /v1/audit/verify?to=<timestamp>`. Security review's explicit task was independently re-deriving that this labeling can't be misread as the full-chain guarantee — zero findings.
**Real bug found and fixed by code review:** the panel lacked a React `key`, so switching rows without closing it could show a stale "Chain verified" result next to a different entry's fields — fixed with `key={selected.id}`, forcing a remount per entry.
**Test coverage:** new `audit_pg_test.go` (real Postgres, 3 tests) proving the `data_handling_designation` round-trip and `VerifyAuditChain` called the panel's exact way against both an intact and a genuinely tampered real chain.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real stack:** real throwaway org/admin, real hash-chained rows, real `/v1/audit` and `/v1/audit/verify` calls (intact and post-tamper), real client-side hash formula independently re-derived in Node.js against a real row. **Visual/UI confirmation of the panel's rendering explicitly deferred to the user's own manual check** — no browser-automation tool was available this session.
**Dependencies:** none. **B-092**/**B-093** (both logged from the B-084 investigation) remain separate, correctly not folded in.

### B-095 — `VerifyAuditChain` produces false "chain broken" results on genuine, untampered data — HIGH — investigation not started
**Objective:** Fix `eami-api/internal/store/verify.go`'s `VerifyAuditChain` so it stops reporting a broken chain against real, untampered `audit_log` history. **Not a security incident** — a verification-tool bug, confirmed not a data-integrity problem (see below).
**Context:** Found 2026-08-22 when B-094's new "Verify chain to this entry" button — the first caller to ever exercise this endpoint against real multi-org, concurrently-written history rather than an isolated single-writer test org — reported Dev Org's real audit history as broken at row `382aa232-2d0b-42a8-95d8-799b06aba7c6`. Investigated directly, not assumed:
- Recomputed all 39 of Dev Org's real audit_log rows' hashes from their own recorded fields: **0/39 self-inconsistent.**
- Reconstructed the true chain order via `hash`→`prev_hash` pointers (independent of the `timestamp` column): **one single unbroken sequence covering all 39 rows, zero cycles, zero orphans.**
- **Root cause, confirmed, two distinct pre-existing bugs in `VerifyAuditChain` itself (predates B-094 entirely — B-094 only calls this function, per its own explicit scope boundary, never modifies it):**
  1. `eami-gateway/internal/audit/writer.go`'s `GetLastHash` has no `org_id` filter — the real hash chain is a single global sequence across every org in the table, not one chain per org. `VerifyAuditChain`, when called with no `?from` (which B-094's panel always does), assumes an org's chronologically-first row must chain directly to the genesis hash — true only for whichever org happened to write the literal first row ever in the whole table. Confirmed directly: Dev Org's first row's real `prev_hash` exactly matches a real row's `hash` from a *different* org (`b044-live-verify-agent`, from B-044's live verification the same day) — not genesis, and not tampered.
  2. `VerifyAuditChain` walks rows via `ORDER BY timestamp ASC`, implicitly assuming that reflects true append order. It doesn't always: `audit.Writer.Write()` (`eami-gateway/internal/audit/writer.go`) sets `e.Timestamp` before acquiring its hash-chain mutex, so under near-simultaneous concurrent requests, two writes' relative timestamp order can differ from their relative insert (and therefore hash-chain) order. Confirmed directly: 3 real rows from B-046's live verification, written ~165ms apart, chain in a different order than their `timestamp` column suggests.
  3. Compounding both: once `VerifyAuditChain` finds one break, it locks `firstBroken` and stops re-verifying subsequent rows (it only keeps counting) — so once bug (1) trips on an org's very first row, **every subsequent "verify to entry" call for that org reports the identical `first_broken_at`, regardless of which entry is actually being checked.** This produces exactly the "every entry now shows broken" symptom that prompted this investigation, and is why it reads like a cascading tamper when it isn't one.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [ ] Investigate the correct fix — likely verifying against the TRUE hash-pointer-derived chain (walk by `prev_hash`/`hash` linkage, not a `timestamp`-ordered, `org_id`-filtered slice), not merely patching the genesis-seeding assumption in isolation
- [ ] Decide whether `VerifyAuditChain` should stay org-scoped at all, given the underlying chain is genuinely global — an org-scoped UI feature (like B-094's per-entry button) needs a verification story that doesn't require pretending the org's slice is a self-contained chain
- [ ] Confirm the fix against Dev Org's real 39-row history specifically: must report `valid:true` end-to-end once corrected
- [ ] Add real-Postgres test coverage reproducing this exact false-positive shape (multi-org interleaved writes + a rapid concurrent-write burst), not just the single-org synthetic chains `audit_pg_test.go` (B-094) already covers
**Severity:** **HIGH** — not a security incident (no data was tampered, confirmed by independent reconstruction), but a real, confirmed correctness bug in a customer-facing tamper-evidence feature: as shipped, it can tell an admin their real audit trail is compromised when it is not, on any deployment that has ever had more than one org write to `audit_log` or any two audit events land within milliseconds of each other. Undermines trust in the exact feature meant to build trust.
**Dependencies:** none blocking investigation. Explicitly needs its own investigation before any brief is written or scoped — do not start implementation from this entry alone.

### B-096 — Centralized branding mechanism (logo/name/theme), rheoARC as first instance — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** Build a real, config-driven branding mechanism (not a one-off logo swap) so logo/name/theme can be changed later by editing one place. Ship rheoARC as the first instance: logo asset + theme colors extracted from it, "EAMI" kept as the displayed name per the deferred full-rebrand epic. Preceded by an investigation-only session inventorying every EAMI branding surface repo-wide.
**What shipped:** `eami-ui/src/branding/config.ts` (single source of truth: `displayName`, `logo.full`/`logo.mark`, `favicon`, `theme.brand`) and `eami-ui/scripts/generate-theme.mjs` (colorthief + sharp, build-time only) which extracts real dominant colors from `Logo/rheo logo name.png` via MMCQ quantization and builds an OKLCH-interpolated 50/100/500/600/700/900 ramp anchored to the exact extracted navy hex — output as `theme.generated.ts` (consumed by `tailwind.config.ts`, which now sources its `brand` colors from this file instead of a hardcoded map) and `public/favicon.svg`. New `Logo.tsx` component reads the config; `Sidebar.tsx` and `LoginPage.tsx` (added mid-session, see below) now render it in place of a `Shield` icon + hardcoded "EAMI" text. `main.tsx` sets `document.title` from `branding.displayName` at runtime.
**Real, disclosed gap:** the source logo is a wordmark only (no icon glyph) — `logo.mark`/`Logo variant="mark"` render a generated placeholder monogram in the real extracted colors, explicitly documented as needing a proper icon-only asset from design later, not treated as solved.
**Mid-session scope addition:** user asked for the Login page too; asked which of two options (logo-only vs. also dropping "EAMI" from the heading text) and built the user's chosen option — icon swapped for the logo, "Sign in to EAMI" left untouched.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. AC5 (config change propagates without touching components) proven by temporarily changing `displayName`, rebuilding, and grepping the sentinel out of the emitted JS bundle, then reverting and confirming clean. Generated theme's hexes confirmed present in the compiled Tailwind CSS. No browser screenshot taken — user declined installing the Chrome extension; dev server was started and curl-verified instead.
**Out of scope, per the brief:** actual display-name change, runtime/admin-editable branding UI, agent installers, appliance boot banner, browser extension manifest.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-097 — `GET /v1/finops/summary` 500 error, root-caused and fixed — **DONE, 2026-08-22**
**Objective:** Diagnose and fix the persistent 500 on `GET /v1/finops/summary`, disclosed as "known, pre-existing" throughout the session but never actually diagnosed.
**Root cause (confirmed via live reproduction, not assumed):** `FinOpsSummary`'s `teamQ` did `GROUP BY team`, an alias colliding with `token_usage`'s own real, never-populated `team TEXT` column — Postgres's GROUP BY name resolution prefers the input column over the alias, leaving `ga.owner` ungrouped: `ERROR: column "ga.owner" must appear in the GROUP BY clause ... (SQLSTATE 42803)`. A genuinely different bug from the already-fixed B-016 (nil-`s.queries` panic), confirmed still intact.
**Fix:** `GROUP BY team` → `GROUP BY ga.owner` (`eami-api/internal/api/finops.go`) — one line, no schema change, `token_usage`'s write path untouched.
**AC4 finding:** `token_usage` is genuinely written by real dispatch activity (25 real rows in Dev Org from past live-verification sessions), but every existing row has zero/empty usage fields — `extractTokenUsage` only populates real values from a downstream response shaped `{model, usage:{input_tokens,output_tokens}}`, and every historical dispatch went through non-AI-provider test connectors. Traced the real `ai_provider`/Claude path and confirmed it *does* return Anthropic's raw response (genuinely shaped correctly) undisturbed to `extractTokenUsage` — so this is an untested-path gap, not a second broken code path. Not fixed (nothing to fix); no live Anthropic API call made to avoid incurring real external cost without clear authorization.
**Test coverage:** 3 new real-Postgres tests in `finops_pg_test.go` (regression + data-correctness, org isolation, empty-range). **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-api`.**
**Live-verified:** the exact original failing request now returns real `200`; every response field cross-checked byte-for-byte against a hand-written `psql` aggregate over the same real rows.
**Dependencies:** none. Full detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api` section.

### B-099 — Escalated (approval-held) tool calls never write to `token_usage` at all — **DONE, 2026-08-23**
**Severity:** Medium-High — this is a real, live gap in FinOps/cost visibility, not a hypothetical: **every** call to an `ai_provider`/Claude connector in this org is currently escalated (the active "Escalate Claude connector calls" policy, priority 2, `action=escalate`), meaning **zero** real AI-provider spend has ever been or will ever be recorded, regardless of B-097's fix.
**Found live, during B-097's own AC4 investigation** (not assumed, not part of B-097's original scope): dispatched a real `tool_call` to the org's real `claude` connector, approved the resulting escalation via the real `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide`, and confirmed via SSE a genuine, successful Anthropic response (real `usage: {input_tokens:8, output_tokens:1}`) came back. But no new `token_usage` row appeared, and neither `eami-gateway` nor `eami-api`'s logs showed any write attempt or failure at all — not a silent write failure, a code path that's never reached.
**Root cause, traced precisely:** `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `extractTokenUsage`/`safeWriteTokenUsage` call (line 428-429) exists **only** inside the `default: // policy.ActionAllow` switch branch. The `case policy.ActionEscalate:` branch (lines 304-375) calls `approvalRouter.Hold(...)` and `return`s its result **directly**, never falling through to the Allow branch's post-processing. Confirmed via a repo-wide grep that `extractTokenUsage`/`safeWriteTokenUsage` have exactly one call site in the entire `eami-gateway` module — `approval.Router.Hold`/the `resolve()` path that actually re-dispatches an approved call has no token-usage-writing logic of its own either. So this isn't a bug in the extraction *logic* (traced separately, confirmed correct for a real `ai_provider` response shape) — it's that the entire FinOps recording step is architecturally skipped for the escalate/approve code path.
**Resolution:** new shared `recordTokenUsage` helper (`main.go`), called from both the Allow branch and the Escalate branch (gated on `holdErr == nil`, right after `Hold()` returns) — chosen over duplicating the two-line snippet since a shared helper structurally prevents the exact "one branch silently missing the other's step" failure this item exists to fix. Zero changes to `approval/router.go` — the fix only reads `Hold()`'s existing return values, so B-057's TOCTOU/resume logic is untouched by construction, not just by discipline.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security: zero findings (no new input source, no new trust boundary, reuses the exact already-reviewed write path). Code review found a real, pre-existing, HIGH-severity bug independent of this fix — logged separately as **B-100**, not fixed here. Also logged **B-101** (Low, disclosed test-coverage gap).
**Test coverage:** 2 new unit tests for the shared helper (`main_test.go`); the full escalate→approve→dispatch cycle isn't independently unit-testable (`dispatch` is an inline closure, matching this exact code path's established live-verification-only precedent), so AC1/AC2/AC4 were proven live instead. **Verified 2026-08-23: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-gateway`**, including every pre-existing TOCTOU/`dispatchApproved` test — zero regressions.
**Live-verified, all 4 ACs, against the real redeployed stack:** AC1 — the exact original bug scenario re-run, now produces a real `token_usage` row (`tokens_in=8, tokens_out=1, cost_usd=0.000010`) where before it produced none. AC2 — a denied escalation produced zero new rows. AC3 — a non-escalated `rest_api` dispatch still records usage, no regression. AC4 — the real TOCTOU credential-rotation scenario re-run (credentials backed up/restored entirely inside Postgres, never exposed outside the DB): resume correctly refused with the real "configuration changed" error, and correctly recorded zero usage for the refused call.
**Dependencies:** B-097 (found this gap). Full detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section.

### B-100 — Approval resume can dispatch to the downstream connector TWICE in a narrow timing race — **DONE, 2026-08-23**
**Severity: HIGH — same severity class as B-057's original TOCTOU finding.** This is a genuine duplicate-execution vulnerability in the core approval mechanism, not a FinOps visibility gap: **a governed action can execute twice against a real downstream system in a narrow timing window, silently, with no error surfaced anywhere.** Do not let this get triaged as routine cleanup or folded into a smaller brief without its own careful investigation first.
**Found by the mandatory code-review pass on B-099**, not assumed — traced directly against the real code in `internal/approval/router.go` before logging, not taken on the reviewer's word alone.
**The race, precisely:** `Router.Hold()`'s timeout backstop (`router.go:334-352`) and `resolve()` (triggered via `LISTEN approval_decision` when eami-api commits a decision) can both end up calling `outcomeFromStatus` → `dispatchApproved` — **the real downstream call** — for the same `approvalID`. Sequence: (1) `resolve()`'s goroutine calls `dispatchApproved` (the real call fires) and prepares to push the result into `entry.ch` (buffered, size 1); (2) if `Hold()`'s `holdCtx.Done()` fires (default 600s timeout) before that push lands — e.g. because `dispatchApproved`'s downstream call is still in flight, or the decision landed right at the deadline — `Hold()`'s non-blocking recheck of `entry.ch` finds nothing; (3) `Hold()` then runs `UPDATE approval_requests SET status='expired' ... WHERE status='pending'`, which matches 0 rows since status is already `'approved'`; (4) on that `ErrNoRows`, `Hold()` re-fetches the real status and calls `outcomeFromStatus` **again** (`router.go:350`), which calls `dispatchApproved` **a second time** — a genuine second real call to the downstream connector (potentially a second real charge, a second real action taken against a customer's system, not just double-counted cost).
**Not a B-099 bug, not fixed there:** B-099 only adds token-usage *recording* after `Hold()` returns; `Hold()` itself only ever returns once, so B-099's own code can't itself double-record — but this race means the *actual downstream execution* can double-fire regardless, and whichever of the two real dispatches doesn't win the return race has its real spend silently unrecorded (a downstream symptom, not the root cause). The root cause is entirely inside `Hold()`'s timeout-backstop logic, out of B-099's explicit `MUST NOT MODIFY: ... the resume-path fix itself` scope.
**Investigated before building, per this item's own instruction:** reproduced the race deterministically (5/5 runs) using a slow fake downstream + short hold timeout, directly driving `Hold()`/`resolve()` concurrently. Confirmed the real-world danger window equals `dispatchApproved`'s own real network latency, and that a decision landing near a 600s deadline (e.g. "finally saw the Slack ping right before it expired") is a normal human-timing scenario, not contrived. Confirmed the race never interacts with or weakens B-057's TOCTOU guarantee (`dispatchApproved` unconditionally re-verifies `ComputeConfigHash` on every call, both racing calls included).
**Resolution: `sync.Once` on `pendingEntry`, not a DB-level lock.** New `once sync.Once` + `result decisionResult` fields; both call sites reaching `outcomeFromStatus` (`resolve()`, `Hold()`'s `ErrNoRows` fallback) wrapped in `entry.once.Do(...)`. Correct specifically because the race is provably intra-process (`resolve()` only ever acts on a *local* `pendingEntry`) — a DB-level lock would solve a cross-node problem that cannot occur here.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran, same rigor as B-057.** Security: zero findings. Code review's finding — that the losing caller's block is no longer bounded by its own `holdTimeout`/ctx — was investigated and its specific premise (a disconnected caller aborting faster pre-fix) found factually inaccurate for this codebase (`Hold()`'s one production caller is fed via `context.WithoutCancel`, B-039's own design, so disconnect never cancelled either version); the narrower real point (bounded by the winner's ~30s downstream timeout instead) was confirmed to be pre-existing, not a new regression, and documented explicitly in code. A second finding (tight test timing margins) was fixed directly (widened 50ms/300ms → 150ms/750ms).
**Test coverage:** new adversarial reproduction test, proven meaningful by reverting the fix and confirming it fails (`hitCount=2`) before re-confirming green — not a vacuous test. `-race` unavailable (no C compiler on this machine), disclosed. Full `internal/approval` suite re-run clean, zero regressions.
**Live-verified:** normal approve/deny cycles unaffected; the real TOCTOU-drift scenario re-run and still correctly refuses; B-099's `recordTokenUsage` confirmed firing exactly once per real dispatch.
**Dependencies:** B-099 (found this gap). Full detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section.

### B-101 — `holdErr == nil` gate (B-099) not independently unit-tested outside live verification — **DONE, 2026-08-24 (resolved as a direct consequence of B-102)**
**Severity:** Low — disclosed test-coverage gap, not a known defect.
**Found by the mandatory code-review pass on B-099.** B-099's `recordTokenUsage` helper was unit-tested in isolation (`main_test.go`), but no automated test drove `run()`'s actual `dispatch` closure end-to-end through the escalate branch to prove the `if holdErr == nil { recordTokenUsage(...) }` gate itself survives a future edit — `dispatch` was an inline closure inside `run()`, not independently testable without the refactor B-099 explicitly put out of its own scope.
**Resolved by B-102, not a separate fix.** `dispatch` is now `Dispatcher.Dispatch`, a real method on a real type, directly constructable in `cmd/gateway/dispatcher_test.go` with a real test Postgres pool and a fake `policy.Evaluator`. `TestDispatch_TokenUsageHook_FiresForApprovedEscalate` drives a genuine escalate→approve→resume cycle end-to-end and asserts the token-usage hook fires with `Dispatched=true`; `TestDispatch_TokenUsageHook_FiresForAllow` proves the same assertion helper for the immediate-Allow path, so the gate is now proven by the same test structure for both instead of by live verification alone. See B-102's entry for full detail.
**Dependencies:** B-102 (the extraction this depended on).

### B-102 — Extract `dispatch` into a testable function + branch-convergence hook mechanism, closing B-101 — **DONE, 2026-08-24**
**Objective:** Close B-099's bug class structurally (a cross-cutting post-dispatch step manually wired into some but not all of `dispatch`'s parallel branches) via the smallest disciplined mechanism that actually solves it — not a generic pub/sub framework.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] `dispatch`'s logic extracted into `Dispatcher`/`Dispatcher.Dispatch` (`cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go`), independently constructable via the exported `NewDispatcher`, callable from a test with a real Postgres pool and a fake `policy.Evaluator` (`cmd/gateway/dispatcher_test.go`) — closes B-101.
- [x] Every branch converges on one shared exit via a typed `DispatchOutcome` struct (real Go struct, not `map[string]any`) — including the Escalate branch's `Submit()`-failure case, which the mandatory code-review pass on this brief itself caught still `return`ing independently in the first draft (fixed: converges via `break` into the same exit as every other case; regression-tested by `TestDispatch_Escalate_SubmitFails_StillConverges`).
- [x] A short, explicit, literal slice of two hooks (`recordTokenUsageHook`, `recordEpisodeHook`) built once in `NewDispatcher`, plus a variadic `extraHooks` test seam (never populated in production) — mirrors `aiprovider.Router`'s explicit `map[string]Adapter`, not a Publish/Subscribe registry, not channels, not global/`init()`-time registration.
- [x] `TestDispatch_NewHook_FiresForAllThreeDecisionTypes` proves the actual point of this brief: a test-only counter hook, added only via `extraHooks` at construction, fires exactly once for Deny/Escalate/Allow with zero branch-specific code in `Dispatch` to register it.
- [x] Zero behavior change to `approval/router.go` (untouched — not modified at all by this brief) — B-057's TOCTOU pinning and B-100's `sync.Once` fix are both exercised unchanged by the existing `internal/approval` suite, re-run clean.
- [x] `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-gateway` (real Postgres, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set) — zero regressions, including every pre-existing TOCTOU/`dispatchApproved`/`TestHold_ResolveRace_*`/workflow test.
**Mandatory code-review pass (general-purpose subagent, effort "high") found 4 real findings, all fixed before shipping:** (1) the Escalate branch's `Submit()`-failure case still `return`ed independently, bypassing the hook list entirely — the exact bug class B-102 exists to eliminate, reintroduced inside the mechanism meant to prevent it; fixed by converging it through the same exit via `break`, with a new regression test proving the hook now fires for that case too (and that it correctly reports `Dispatched=false` and an empty `EpisodeSteps`, preserving the pre-existing "no episode written on a Submit failure" behavior exactly). (2) The ~400-line `Dispatcher` mechanism was added inline into `main.go` instead of its own file, inconsistent with `CLAUDE.md`'s "one file per concern" convention and with `dispatcher_test.go` already being split out — fixed by moving it into a new `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go` (same package, zero behavior change). (3) `DispatchOutcome.Dispatched` was being set independently per branch even though it was always exactly `Err == nil` in every case — the same "two things that must stay in sync" duplication risk this whole refactor exists to eliminate; fixed by computing it once, centrally, right before the hook loop, rather than as a fifth per-branch literal. (4) The four episode.Step literals were duplicated verbatim across branches; extracted into a shared `newEpisodeStep` helper. Security review: zero findings (pure structural refactor — no new input source, trust boundary, or destination; `approval/router.go` untouched).
**Test coverage:** 7 new tests in `cmd/gateway/dispatcher_test.go`, all real-Postgres integration tests (no live-stack dependency) — `TestDispatch_Deny/Allow/Escalate_Approved_NoFullServerNeeded` (AC1), `TestDispatch_TokenUsageHook_FiresForAllow`/`FiresForApprovedEscalate` (AC3, one shared assertion helper for both), `TestDispatch_NewHook_FiresForAllThreeDecisionTypes` (AC4, the central proof), `TestDispatch_Escalate_SubmitFails_StillConverges` (the code-review regression test). **Verified 2026-08-24: `go build`/`go vet` clean; `go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** against a real Postgres (`docker compose up -d postgres`), including every pre-existing `internal/approval` TOCTOU/`dispatchApproved`/`TestHold_ResolveRace_*` test and the full `internal/workflow` suite (AC6 — zero code changes to that package; `workflow.New(pool, dispatcher.Dispatch, ...)` type-checks identically to the old closure).
**Explicitly out of scope, per the investigation's own over-engineering guard:** any repo-wide event-bus/registry abstraction — the 2026-08-23 investigation's exhaustive inventory found exactly one site (`dispatch`'s policy switch) with the multi-branch fan-out shape this problem needs solved. Do not generalize beyond this one site without a second, independently-confirmed instance of the same bug class.
**Dependencies:** B-099 (found the bug class), B-100 (confirmed unaffected), B-101 (resolved as a side effect — see its own entry above).

### B-103 — Document B-057's Adapter pattern as the standing convention for new connector/integration types — **DONE, 2026-08-24**
**Objective:** `aiprovider.Adapter` (`eami-gateway/internal/aiprovider/types.go:59`) + `Router.registry map[string]Adapter` (`router.go:49-62`) is a real, already-proven pattern — confirmed by the 2026-08-23 investigation, not newly discovered — for adding a new pluggable integration TYPE without touching dispatch/policy/audit/TOCTOU wiring. It had never been written down as a standing convention outside its own doc comments.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] The convention is documented — but NOT in `ARCHITECTURE.md`/a new ADR as originally scoped. Investigation before building found `BOUNDARIES.md`'s own ownership table assigns `ARCHITECTURE.md` to Architect-EAMI and `DECISIONS.md` (the ADR log) to PM-EAMI — both outside this session's file boundary per that doc's own "Golden Rule" ("cross-boundary changes require a written handoff task"). Documented instead as a new bullet in `CLAUDE.md`'s `## Conventions` section (not listed in `BOUNDARIES.md`'s ownership table at all, and read by every future session, guaranteed) — confirmed with the user before writing, per this brief's own plan-then-approve kickoff.
- [x] Explicitly names intended future users — additional AI providers, Skills, A2A agents — and explicitly distinguishes this ("new backend TYPE behind the dispatch surface") from B-102's mechanism ("new cross-cutting reaction to an existing call"), so a future reader doesn't conflate the two.
- [x] No code changes — `CLAUDE.md`/`BACKLOG.md`/`BUILT.md`/`CONTEXT.md` only.
**Also discovered, flagged, not fixed (outside this brief's file boundary):** `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.3 (eami-gateway's "Key packages" list) is missing `internal/aiprovider` entirely — consistent with the same staleness pattern `ADR-020`/`B-080` already found elsewhere in that file. Not touched here.
**Dependencies:** none. Independent of B-102/B-104.

### B-104 — Adopt shared DataTable component on Agents/Policies/Workflows pages, closing the root cause behind B-081/082/083's bug class — **DONE, 2026-08-24**
**Objective:** `DataTable.tsx`'s `onRowClick`-conditional hover (line 109) already correctly centralizes "hover implies clickable" — B-081/082/083 happened not because that component is wrong, but because `AgentsPage.tsx:384-386`, `PoliciesPage.tsx:361-363`, and `WorkflowsPage.tsx:810-812` each hand-roll their own `<tr>` instead of using it. Adopting the shared component on these three pages closes the actual root cause, preventing a 4th recurrence of this exact bug class.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] `AgentsPage.tsx`, `PoliciesPage.tsx`, `WorkflowsPage.tsx` each replace their hand-rolled `<table>`/`<tr className="cursor-pointer hover:bg-gray-50" onClick=...>` markup with `<DataTable onRowClick=.../>`. `DataTable.tsx` itself was investigated and confirmed not to need any extension.
- [x] No behavior change to each page's existing row-click targets/actions or in-row action buttons (`stopPropagation` guards preserved on every button, including Policies' up/down reorder chevrons). Policies' reorder needed the row's true array index, which `Column.render(row)` doesn't receive — solved via `policies.indexOf(policy)` inside `render` (correct regardless of `DataTable`'s pagination, valid because no column here is `sortable`), not a `DataTable.tsx` change.
- [x] No parallel duplicate sort/pagination introduced — none of the three pages had sort or pagination before; all three now pass `pageSize={1000}` so `DataTable`'s own built-in pager never engages, preserving the prior "show everything" behavior exactly instead of silently starting to paginate at 25 rows.
- [x] Real `tsc && vite build` via `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .`, clean, zero type errors.
- [x] Live browser click-through explicitly disclosed as **not performed** — the seeded dev users (`scripts/seed-db.sh`) carry a placeholder bcrypt hash, not a real password, so no authenticated session was reachable. Verified instead via the real running `eami-ui` dev container (bind-mounted, Vite HMR): confirmed the served module is the current code for all three pages and that the old hand-rolled hover-without-click string is gone from all three.
**Real complication found before building, not assumed trivial:** neither `ApprovalsPage.tsx` nor `AlertsPage.tsx` — offered as "the reference" — actually uses `onRowClick`; both render `DataTable` read-only. This migration constructs the row-click + action-buttons combination for the first time (mechanically low-risk regardless).
**Also discovered, flagged, not fixed (out of scope — "any other page"):** `DashboardPage.tsx`/`DiscoverPage.tsx` still have the identical hand-rolled hover-without-click pattern.
**Dependencies:** none. Independent of B-102/B-103, no backend dependency.

### B-098 — Gate AI-agent token issuance with scoped API keys — **DONE, 2026-08-24**
**Objective:** `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` (`identity.Manager.HandleIssue`) had zero authentication — anyone reaching the gateway's port could mint a valid AI-agent token for any existing agent name, with `req.AgentID` trusted as opaque client input straight into the JWT `Subject`. Closed by extending the existing, previously-inert `api_keys` feature (real UI, real storage, `GetAPIKeyByHash` had zero callers anywhere) to actually gate this endpoint. `api/openapi.yaml` already documented `security: ApiKeyAuth` for this route — the contract was always meant to be key-gated, just never implemented.
**Re-verified all investigation findings directly against current code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** `api_keys` confirmed org-scoped only (no `agent_id` column), `GetAPIKeyByHash` confirmed still uncalled anywhere, `req.AgentID` confirmed unvalidated. **No legitimate internal caller of the unauthenticated endpoint exists** — repo-wide grep found only `Manager.Issue()` called directly by tests (bypassing HTTP), never a real HTTP call to this route from any internal service.
**Schema (`schema/migrations-v2/000010_gateway_token_auth.up.sql`):** `api_keys` gains `agent_id UUID REFERENCES gateway_agents(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (nullable — an org-scoped-only key, e.g. today's collector-ingest usage, stays valid without being agent-scoped; only an agent-scoped key can authorize gateway token issuance, enforced in application code, not a schema constraint) + a partial index. New table `ai_token_events` (org_id FK-cascaded; `agent_id`/`agent_name`/`api_key_id` are snapshot columns with **no FK**, mirroring B-087's `agent_lifecycle_events` reasoning exactly — a machine-presented API key issuing/revoking a JWT has no human `performed_by` and is a different concern from that table's admin-CRUD-history scope, which is why this isn't a write into `agent_lifecycle_events` or `audit_log`).
**`eami-gateway/internal/identity` (new files, following `revoke_http.go`'s established `AgentResolver`-interface-as-test-seam pattern):** `apikey.go` (`APIKeyValidator` interface + Postgres impl — same-DB query, not a new cross-service dependency; hashes the presented `X-API-Key` with SHA-256 hex, matching `eami-api/internal/auth.APIKeyFromRaw`'s exact format, empirically verified against real minted keys); `events.go` (`TokenEventStore` interface + Postgres impl, `RecordIssued`/`RecordRevoked`); `issue_http.go` (new `IssueHandler`, replacing the deleted insecure `Manager.HandleIssue`) — validates the key, requires it to be agent-scoped, resolves the requested agent via `registry.LookupByNameAndOrg` scoped to the **validated key's own org** (never client input), and rejects when the resolved agent's real UUID doesn't equal the key's bound `agent_id` — the actual cross-agent scoping proof. The JWT `Subject` is rebuilt from the resolved record's canonical name, never echoed from client input. `tokens.go` gains a `JTI` field on `IssueResponse` (additive) so the handler can log what it minted without re-parsing. `revoke_http.go` gains a `TokenEventStore` param and now writes a `revoked` event after a successful `Revoke()`.
**`eami-api`:** `CreateAPIKeyRequest`/`APIKeyResp` gain `agent_id`/`expires_at` (undocumented deviation from `openapi.yaml`'s current shape — Architect-EAMI-owned per `BOUNDARIES.md`, disclosed not silently edited, same precedent as B-086's `usePolicies.ts` deviation — logged as **B-106** below). `CreateAPIKey` validates `agent_id` against a real `gateway_agents` row scoped to the caller's own org (never trusted as opaque, mirroring the gateway's own principle) and — per this brief's own mandatory code-review finding — rejects binding to an agent that isn't `active`. `expires_at` (a column that existed since migration 002 but was never settable at the API layer) now accepts RFC3339 or `YYYY-MM-DD`, matching `finops.go`'s existing dual-format convention. `GetAPIKeyByHash` now also filters `expires_at` (another code-review finding — it previously only checked `revoked`, an inconsistency with the gateway's own new validator for the identical row; latent since this query has zero production callers in `eami-api`, but fixed for future callers).
**`eami-ui`:** `useApiKeys.ts`'s create mutation gains `agent_id`; `SettingsPage.tsx`'s API Keys tab gains an agent `<select>` (populated from the existing `useAgents()` hook) and a "Scoped agent" table column (falls back to a short id fragment, not a raw UUID, when the agents list hasn't loaded yet or the agent was deleted — a third code-review finding, fixed).
**Mandatory reviewer + security subagent passes, both run, both found real issues, all fixed before shipping:** Security review (independent fork, full trace of the 6 highest-risk questions — cross-agent/cross-org bypass, SQL injection, hash-algorithm correctness, cross-org agent-binding at key-creation time, timing side-channel, events-table injection): zero HIGH-confidence findings; all six traced and confirmed closed, several empirically (live-verified against the real running gateway, not just read). **A 7th issue found independently during this same pass** (not part of the security fork's brief, found by direct trace): the two "agent not registered" vs. "not scoped to this agent" 403 branches used different messages, letting a valid-but-wrongly-scoped key enumerate which agent names exist in its own org — low severity (same-org only) but free to close; unified into one message before the security fork even finished, live-reverified. Code review (general-purpose subagent, "high" effort) found 4 issues: the `GetAPIKeyByHash`/expiry gap and the suspended-agent-binding gap above (both fixed, both now covered by new real-Postgres tests), the UI raw-UUID fallback (fixed), and a **disclosed-not-fixed** observation that `HandleIssue` now costs 3–4 serial Postgres round trips per issuance versus the previous single in-process JWT sign, copying `revoke_http.go`'s cache-bypass reasoning (written for a low-frequency admin action) onto what could become a higher-frequency path — logged as **B-107** below, out of this security-fix brief's scope to address via caching/parallelism now.
**Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/identity/issue_http_pg_test.go` (9 new real-Postgres tests covering AC1–AC6 plus an org-scoped-only-key case); `revoke_http_pg_test.go` updated for the new `TokenEventStore` param (all 5 existing tests still pass); `eami-api/internal/api/auth_apikey_pg_test.go` (6 real-Postgres tests: store-level round trip, cross-org rejection, full HTTP round trip, suspended-agent rejection, expired-key-excluded-from-lookup). **Verified 2026-08-24: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entirety of both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions; the new migration verified via `schema/migrationtest`'s full suite (fresh-path/incremental-path parity, idempotent re-run, seeded-database upgrade) — all 4 tests pass with the file-count-derived version check, no hardcoded number to go stale. Real `tsc`/`vite build` clean.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and restarted before and after the code-review fixes:** all 6 acceptance criteria proven via real `curl` against the real containerized gateway on its real port — no key → 401; a key scoped to a different real agent in the same real org → 403 (**the centerpiece proof**, AC2); a correctly-scoped key → real 200 with a real signed JWT; a revoked key → 401; an expired key → 401; a not-yet-expired key → 200. Both `issued` and `revoked` `ai_token_events` rows confirmed live via direct `psql` query, matching the real `jti` values returned. The unified-error-message fix re-verified live after the rebuild: cross-agent and unknown-agent-name requests now return byte-identical 403 bodies. All live-verification fixtures (orgs, agents, keys, events — cascaded via `org_id ON DELETE CASCADE`) confirmed fully cleaned up afterward.
**Dependencies:** B-042 (`RevokeHandler`/`AgentResolver` pattern this extends), B-057 (structural precedent for a small interface + real implementation, cited for the pattern shape though this isn't a new `Adapter`). **Out of scope, as specified:** OAuth 2.1 for interactive external clients (B-076), streamable HTTP/MCP handshake work (B-076).

### B-106 — `api/openapi.yaml` doesn't document `CreateAPIKeyRequest.agent_id`/`.expires_at` or `AITokenResponse.jti`
**Objective:** B-098 added `agent_id`/`expires_at` to `POST /v1/auth/api-keys`'s request/response and `jti` to `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`'s response, but `openapi.yaml` is Architect-EAMI-owned per `BOUNDARIES.md` and out of this session's file boundary — same disclosed-not-silent precedent as B-086's `usePolicies.ts`/`openapi.yaml` mismatch (logged as B-089, still open).
**Severity:** Low — purely a spec-documentation gap; the real Go handlers and the real frontend already agree on the actual shape, verified by real integration tests.
**Dependencies:** none.

### B-107 — `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` now costs 3–4 serial Postgres round trips per issuance — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** B-098's `IssueHandler.HandleIssue` performs `ValidateAPIKey` → `LookupByNameAndOrg` → `Manager.Issue`'s DB write → `RecordIssued`, with no parallelism.
**Re-verified directly against current code before building — the brief's own description didn't fully hold up:** `Manager.Issue()` does **zero** DB work (pure in-process JWT signing via RWMutex + RSA sign, confirmed by reading `tokens.go`), so "`Manager.Issue`'s DB write" doesn't exist. The real round-trip count was 3 (`ValidateAPIKey`, `LookupByNameAndOrg`, `RecordIssued`), not 3–4.
**What shipped:** (1) `ValidateAPIKey` + `LookupByNameAndOrg` combined into one new `ValidateAndResolveAgent` query (`eami-gateway/internal/identity/apikey.go`) — a `LEFT JOIN gateway_agents ga ON ga.org_id = ak.org_id AND ga.name = $2` against the `api_keys` row matched by key hash, so the agent lookup is scoped by the **same validated key row's own org_id**, never a second round trip that could diverge from it. LEFT JOIN (not INNER) so "valid key, no matching agent" is still distinguishable from "no such key at all." (2) `RecordIssued` (the audit-event write) made fire-and-forget, mirroring `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s established `safeWriteTokenUsage`/B-099 pattern — the caller already has a valid, usable token once issuance succeeds, so this write no longer blocks the response. Net: 3 blocking round trips → 1.
**The old, now-dead `ValidateAPIKey` interface method was removed entirely** (code review confirmed via repo-wide grep it had exactly zero callers left anywhere, including tests) rather than left as an untested extra surface on a security-relevant interface.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes both ran and both found real issues — most severe was a genuine bug this brief introduced, not a hypothetical:**
- **Code review (HIGH):** the new fire-and-forget `RecordIssued` goroutine omitted the `safego.Guard` panic-recovery wrapper that the exact precedent it claimed to mirror (`safeWriteTokenUsage`) actually uses — `safego`'s own package doc states plainly that an unrecovered panic in a detached goroutine crashes the *entire process*, not just the request. **Fixed:** wrapped in `safego.Guard("token-issuance-event-recorder", ...)`, matching the mirrored pattern exactly instead of copying half of it.
- **Code review + security review (both, independently, Medium):** `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())` on the detached goroutine never expires, so a stalled events-store DB would hang the goroutine (and its pool connection) indefinitely. **Fixed:** switched to `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)`, bounding the worst case while still surviving the handler returning.
- **Security review (Medium):** B-107 reordered body-decode ahead of API-key validation (needed since the combined query takes `agentName` as a parameter), which meant an **unauthenticated** caller could make the handler buffer an arbitrarily large body before any credential check — and an in-code comment incorrectly waved this off as "not a security-relevant change." **Fixed:** `r.Body = http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, 8<<10)` added as the first statement (`IssueRequest` is six short strings + an int, so 8KiB is generous headroom); the incorrect comment rewritten to credit the actual fix instead of dismissing the concern.
- **Code review (Medium, test gap):** the cross-org join correlation (`ga.org_id = ak.org_id`) had no test proving two different orgs each having an agent with the *identical* name (an explicitly expected state — `gateway_agents.name` is unique only per-org) doesn't let a key from one org resolve the other org's same-named agent. **Fixed:** new `TestIssueHandler_SameAgentNameDifferentOrgs_ResolvesOwnOrgOnly` proves both directions — a key correctly resolves its own org's same-named agent, and a different key in the same org (bound to a different agent) is still rejected for that same-named agent.
- **Code review (Low):** a stale comment on `policies.sql.go` (shared with B-090, see that entry) credited a handler-side org pre-check that doesn't exist on the production path — fixed there, not duplicated here.
- **Not fixed, logged fresh:** client-controlled `Scope`/`Task`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` fields pass straight from client JSON into the signed JWT claims (only `Subject` is rebuilt from the resolved DB record) — traced by security review to be **not currently exploitable** (only `Subject` and `ExpiresAt` are read anywhere downstream, confirmed by grep), but a latent self-service-claims risk the day any policy consumer starts trusting `claims.RiskTier` or similar. Pre-existing since B-098, not introduced or touched by B-107, out of this brief's scope to fix — logged as **B-116** below. Also not fixed: no rate limiting on this endpoint (pre-existing, matches the rest of the gateway) — logged as **B-118**.
**Test coverage:** `TestIssueHandler_OversizedBody_Returns400Bounded` (the `MaxBytesReader` fix) and `TestIssueHandler_SameAgentNameDifferentOrgs_ResolvesOwnOrgOnly` (the cross-org test gap) both new in `issue_http_pg_test.go`. All 10 pre-existing `TestIssueHandler_*`/`TestRevokeHandler_*` tests in the package re-run clean, including `TestIssueHandler_KeyScopedToDifferentAgent_Returns403` (the original B-098 cross-agent-scoping proof, re-confirmed unaffected) and `TestIssueHandler_SuccessfulIssue_RecordsAITokenEvent` (updated to poll for the now-async write via a `waitForTokenEvent` helper instead of asserting immediately). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-gateway`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** a real API key created via `POST /v1/auth/api-keys` scoped to a real existing agent (`manual-test-agent`); a real cross-agent attempt (same key, requesting a different real agent `claude-walkthrough-agent`'s identity) returned a real 403 — re-proving B-098's AC2 centerpiece unaffected by the combined-query rewrite. The same key issuing for its own bound agent returned a real 200 with a real signed JWT, and the resulting `ai_token_events` row was confirmed present via direct `psql` within 1 second — proving the `safego.Guard`+bounded-timeout rework of the async write still lands correctly under real conditions. All fixtures cleaned up afterward (test API key revoked via `DELETE /v1/auth/api-keys/{id}`, which the real endpoint implements as a soft `revoked=true`, not a hard delete — confirmed, not assumed, before reporting cleanup complete).
**Dependencies:** B-098 (the code this optimizes), B-099 (the fire-and-forget/`safego.Guard` precedent this now correctly mirrors). New discovered work: **B-116** (client-controlled JWT claims, latent), **B-118** (no rate limiting on this endpoint).

### B-108 — FinOps: connector breakdown + fix silent metric gaps — **DONE, 2026-08-25**
**Objective:** two independent, small fixes found by the Token Usage Optimization re-investigation: (1) `token_usage.tool_name` existed in the schema but was never populated or queried, so no connector-level cost breakdown was possible; (2) two silent metric bugs — `avg_cost_per_outcome` documented in `openapi.yaml` and read by the frontend but never computed by the backend (a permanently blank KPI card in production), and `by_team` computed correctly by the backend since B-097 but never rendered by the frontend (dead data).
**Re-verified all three findings directly against current code before building, per this brief's own assumption:** confirmed `tokenUsagePayload`/`TokenUsageRequest`/`InsertTokenUsageParams` all omitted `tool_name` despite `ac.Tool` being available at the dispatch call site; confirmed `TokenSpendSummary` had no `AvgCostPerOutcome` field despite `openapi.yaml:616` documenting it and `FinOpsPage.tsx:122` reading it; confirmed `ByTeam` was real and correct (`finops.go`) but never referenced anywhere in `FinOpsPage.tsx`.
**Decision, investigated before building:** `avg_cost_per_outcome` — **compute it for real**, not remove. It's a well-defined metric (`total_cost_usd / count of token_usage rows in the period` — each row already is one recorded dispatch outcome, exactly what `by_agent.request_count` counts per-agent) and cheaper to fix than to remove (computing needs zero `openapi.yaml` changes; removing would, and that file is Architect-EAMI-owned, out of this session's boundary). `by_team` — **render it, not remove**. The backend computation was already correct and already covered by a real-Postgres regression test (B-097's `finops_pg_test.go`); removing already-correct, already-tested work to avoid a small frontend addition was the wrong trade.
**What shipped:** `eami-gateway`'s `extractTokenUsage` sets `ToolName` from `ac.Tool` unconditionally (unlike `Model`/token counts, which depend on the downstream response body parsing). `eami-api`'s `TokenUsageRequest`/`InsertTokenUsageParams`/`InsertTokenUsage` thread it through to the already-existing nullable `tool_name` column — no migration needed. `finops.go` gains a `toolQ` query mirroring `modelQ` exactly (`COALESCE(tu.tool_name, 'unknown')` for an unresolved-tool dispatch, mirroring `by_team`'s identical `COALESCE(ga.owner, 'unknown')` fallback), and `totalQ` gains a `COUNT(*)` feeding a guarded `avgCostPerOutcome := totalCost / totalRequests` (0 for an empty period, not NaN). `FinOpsPage.tsx` gains "Spend by Team" and "Spend by Connector" tables (same JSX pattern as the existing "Spend by Agent" table); the "Avg Cost / Outcome" `MetricCard` needed no JSX change — it already read the field.
**`ToolSpend` isn't yet in `api/openapi.yaml`** — disclosed, not silently edited, same B-098/`agent_id` precedent (logged as part of the already-open **B-106**, same class of gap).
**Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/cmd/gateway/main_test.go` — `ToolName` assertion added to the existing `TestRecordTokenUsage_ExtractsAndWrites`, plus a new `TestExtractTokenUsage_ToolNameSurvivesUnparseableBody`. `eami-api/internal/api/finops_pg_test.go` — 3 new real-Postgres tests (`TestFinOpsSummary_Real_ToolBreakdown_RecordsAndAggregatesByConnector`, `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_UnresolvedToolMapsToUnknown`, `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_AvgCostPerOutcome`), plus the existing `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_NoDataInRange` extended for the two new fields. **Verified 2026-08-25: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`** against a real Postgres — 13/13 FinOps tests pass, zero regression to `by_agent`/`by_model` (AC5). Real `tsc`/`vite build` clean.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and restarted:** a real `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` (B-098) → real MCP SSE session → real `tool_call` through a real `rest_api` connector to `postman-echo.com` — the resulting real `token_usage` row confirmed via `psql` to carry `tool_name = 'b108-echo-connector'` (AC1). Two more rows seeded with distinct connectors/costs; a real admin login then a real `GET /v1/finops/summary` returned `total_cost_usd: 0.37`, `avg_cost_per_outcome: 0.12333...` (= 0.37/3 exactly), `by_tool` correctly split $0.30/$0.07 across the two connectors, `by_team` correctly present — all hand-verified against the seeded values (AC2, AC3, AC4). The real running `eami-ui` dev container confirmed serving the new sections via a direct fetch of the served module. All fixtures cleaned up afterward, including 3 `token_usage` rows requiring explicit manual deletion (`token_usage.org_id` has no FK, doesn't cascade with the org).
**Dependencies:** B-097 (`by_team`'s underlying query), B-098 (live-verification login/token path). **Out of scope, as specified:** `model_pricing` admin CRUD, caching cost-accounting, budget enforcement, workflow-level cost attribution (all separate, already-logged from the same re-investigation).

### B-109 — Concurrent test-helper data race: shared `float64` cost variable mutated across goroutines without synchronization
**Objective:** found during the caching cost-accounting investigation (not yet started as a fix) — a test helper mutates a shared `float64` cost accumulator from concurrent test goroutines with no synchronization (mutex/atomic/channel). Real but narrow: test-only code, not a production path, so no live-system correctness or security impact — the risk is flaky/misleading test results (a torn read/write on a non-atomic `float64`), not a shipped bug.
**Severity:** Low.
**Re-verification (2026-08-26): exhaustive search could not locate the described race.** This entry was originally minted straight from an investigation summary without independently confirming the file/line existed — a gap in the logging process that session had, not a claim this session re-confirmed. A thorough re-verification pass for the B-090/107/109 hardening batch searched: every `*_test.go` file for `float64`, every goroutine-spawning test file (`go func()`/`go <ident>(` patterns), every `sync.WaitGroup` usage repo-wide, `cost`/`total`/`sum +=` accumulator patterns repo-wide, and the one non-Go load-test script (`tests/load/gateway.js`). None match "a shared `float64` cost variable mutated across concurrent goroutines without synchronization." Left QUEUED rather than closed — a negative search result isn't proof the race never existed (it may have been fixed incidentally by an unrelated later change, described imprecisely, or simply missed) — but the next session picking this up should treat "confirm it still exists" as the real first step, not "find the file," since this pass already tried and failed at the latter.
**Dependencies:** none known yet.

### B-110 — `finops.go` fallback-rate collision: an unrecognized model's requests can be silently misattributed into a recognized model's aggregate totals
**Objective:** found during the same caching cost-accounting investigation (not yet started as a fix) — in FinOps' cost-summary calculation, a request from a model `model_pricing` doesn't recognize can be silently folded into a different, recognized model's aggregate cost/token totals rather than being excluded or bucketed as unknown. Unlike B-109, this is a real correctness gap in FinOps' own reporting accuracy (production code, not test-only) — a real dispatch's spend could misreport against the wrong model line.
**Severity:** Medium.
**Connection to item 3 (`model_pricing` admin CRUD, tracked separately):** worth doing alongside that work rather than deferred indefinitely — admin CRUD will let operators add/rename model rows directly, which is exactly the lever that changes how often an "unrecognized model" case is hit in practice; fixing the collision in isolation, before CRUD exists, risks re-solving it once CRUD reshapes how models get registered.
**Investigation not started:** exact query/join responsible for the misattribution not yet root-caused; scoping that is the first step of whichever session picks this up.
**Dependencies:** related to item 3 (`model_pricing` admin CRUD, not yet B-ID'd) — see note above.

### B-111 — Fix caching cost-accounting: parse and price cache tokens correctly — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** `extractTokenUsage` only ever parsed `usage.input_tokens`/`usage.output_tokens` — Anthropic's real Messages API `usage` object also returns `cache_creation_input_tokens`/`cache_read_input_tokens` (plus a nested `cache_creation.{ephemeral_5m_input_tokens,ephemeral_1h_input_tokens}` breakdown) whenever an agent uses standard prompt caching, silently dropped by Go's unmarshal. Confirmed via investigation and directly re-verified against two independent live sources before building: 5 genuinely distinct pricing tiers — base input, 5-minute cache write (1.25x), 1-hour cache write (2x), cache read (0.1x), output — not a 2-tier structure.
**Re-verified before building, not assumed stale:** the multipliers matched the prior investigation exactly. One thing that WAS stale: 1-hour caching no longer needs a beta header (a `"ttl":"1h"` field in the request's `cache_control` block is sufficient) — since `ClaudeAdapter.Dispatch` forwards `req.Params` verbatim, 1h caching is fully reachable through EAMI's dispatch path today, not a theoretical edge case. Checked whether a same-family rate collision (Sonnet 5 vs. 4.6) was real: it wasn't — `model_pricing` has no `claude-sonnet-5` row today, only `claude-sonnet-4-6`; the real requirement was deriving each row's cache rates from **that row's own** `cost_per_1k_in`, never one shared multiplier, which the backfill does per-row by construction.
**Schema (`schema/migrations-v2/000011_cache_token_pricing.up.sql`):** `token_usage` gains 3 raw count columns (`cache_creation_5m_tokens`, `cache_creation_1h_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, all `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — base/output tiers already existed as `tokens_in`/`tokens_out`). `model_pricing` gains 3 nullable rate columns (`cost_per_1k_cache_write_5m`, `cost_per_1k_cache_write_1h`, `cost_per_1k_cache_read`), backfilled **per-row** for `claude-%` models from that row's own `cost_per_1k_in * {1.25, 2.0, 0.1}` — non-Claude rows stay NULL (their responses never populate these fields).
**Query-time computation, not write-time, per this brief's own explicit design:** `extractTokenUsage`/`TokenUsageRequest`/`InsertTokenUsageParams` only ever store the 3 raw cache counts — no cost is computed for them at write time, and `IngestTokenUsage`'s existing base/output cost formula is **completely untouched** (this is what makes "no regression to non-caching pricing" easy to guarantee). All 5 `finops.go` queries (`totalQ`/`agentQ`/`teamQ`/`modelQ`/`toolQ`) gain an additive cache-cost term inside the same per-row `SUM(...)`, computed fresh every query from **current** `model_pricing` rates (`COALESCE(mp.cost_per_1k_cache_*, 0)`), appended on top of the existing (untouched) frozen-`cost_usd`/NULL-fallback base+output CASE — so a rate correction re-prices every historical row the next time the query runs, without touching the base+output pricing mechanism at all.
**A genuinely unresolved question, resolved empirically rather than guessed:** whether Anthropic's `cache_creation` breakdown sub-object is present for a single-TTL (not just mixed 5m+1h) response wasn't settled by documentation either way. Live-verified directly: a real `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` dispatch with a pure 1h-only `cache_control` (no 5m mixed in) returned `"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4812}` — the breakdown **is** present for single-TTL responses. `extractTokenUsage`'s flat-total-only fallback branch (attributing an ungrouped `cache_creation_input_tokens` to the 5m tier) is kept as defensive-only, documented as not observed reachable against the real API, not deleted — a cheap insurance for a response shape variant that wasn't actually seen.
**Test coverage:** `main_test.go` gains 4 new extraction tests, including one built from the exact real response JSON captured live (breakdown-present split, cache-read-only, defensive-fallback, and a no-caching regression guard). `finops_pg_test.go` gains 3 new real-Postgres tests: `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_CacheTokens_RecordedRawAndQueriedAtAllFiveTiers` (AC1+AC2+AC5 — raw counts verified via direct pool query, cost hand-computed across all 5 tiers, cross-checked against `ByAgent`/`ByModel`), `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_NoCaching_PricesExactlyAsBefore` (AC3), `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_RateChangeThenRequery_ReflectsNewRate` (AC4 centerpiece — same historical row, cost changes purely from a `model_pricing` update, not a new write). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`** against a real Postgres — all pre-existing FinOps/token-usage tests still pass (zero regressions), plus `schema/migrationtest`'s full suite (fresh/incremental parity, idempotent re-run, seeded-DB upgrade) passes with the new migration in place.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and restarted with the fix before verification (an earlier same-session dispatch against the pre-fix binary was correctly excluded from evidence once noticed):** a real `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` dispatch with 1h `cache_control` recorded `cache_creation_1h_tokens` correctly via direct `psql` (AC1); a repeat of the identical cached content within the TTL window recorded a real cache **hit** (`cache_read_tokens: 4812`), independently confirming the read tier too; a real `GET /v1/finops/summary` against this data returned `total_cost_usd: 0.00047896`, hand-verified exactly against all 3 rows' tier-specific rates (AC2); a real plain (non-`cache_control`) dispatch priced at exactly the pre-B-111 base formula, `0.000032` (AC3); **AC4 proven live, not just in the Go test:** doubled `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`'s live `cost_per_1k_cache_read` rate via `psql`, re-queried the exact same already-recorded historical rows, total moved to `0.00086392` — exactly the hand-computed delta — then restored the original rate and confirmed the total reverted to `0.00047896` byte-for-byte; `by_agent`/`by_team`/`by_model`/`by_tool` all consistently reflected the same corrected total throughout (AC5). All live-dispatch approvals required real (sub-cent) Anthropic spend, explicitly user-approved before each one. All fixtures cleaned up afterward: API key and the stale pre-fix pending approval both closed; the agent couldn't be hard-deleted (real episode/approval history from the live dispatches — `DeleteAgent`'s audit-trail guard correctly refused) so it was suspended instead; all 3 `token_usage` rows deleted manually (`token_usage` has no FK to `orgs`/`gateway_agents`, same pre-existing gap B-108 already documented).
**Discrepancy flagged, not silently accepted:** this brief's `MUST NOT MODIFY` list named two "separately logged" items (a finops.go fallback-rate collision, a concurrent test-helper race) that did not actually exist anywhere in `BACKLOG.md`/`CONTEXT.md`/`NOTES.md` at the time this brief was issued — logged fresh as **B-109** (Low, the test-helper race) and **B-110** (Medium, the fallback-rate collision) in a prior session turn, before this brief's own build work began.
**Dependencies:** none new. **Out of scope, as specified:** `model_pricing` admin CRUD (item 3), the B-110 fallback-rate collision, the B-109 test-helper race, any caching orchestration/management feature.

### B-112 — Model pricing admin CRUD + fix fallback-rate collision — **DONE, 2026-08-26**
**Objective:** two related fixes to `model_pricing`: (1) no admin CRUD path existed — adding/repricing a model required a raw SQL statement, unlike `api_keys`/agents/policies. (2) B-110's fallback-rate collision.
**Re-verified B-110's actual mechanism empirically before building, not assumed from its own description:** the kickoff line named it "B-109's finding" — wrong, it's B-110 (confirmed directly against `BACKLOG.md`). More importantly, a rolled-back real-Postgres reproduction (one recognized model row + one deliberately-unrecognized model row, queried through the exact `by_model`/`totalQ` SQL) showed the unrecognized model was **already** kept in its own, correctly separate `by_model` bucket — never merged into the recognized model's. The real bug is narrower: an unrecognized model's cost silently becomes exactly **$0** (Postgres `SUM` ignores the `NULL` produced by `tokens × an unmatched rate`), under-reporting `total_cost_usd`/`by_agent`/`by_team`/`by_tool`, and rendering as an indistinguishable plain "$0.00" in `by_model` rather than something flagged. Built the fix for the bug that's actually real, not the one as originally described.
**Org-scoped vs. global pricing, investigated before building:** `model_pricing` has no `org_id` — confirmed genuinely global (no doc anywhere mentions per-org negotiated rates). Kept global. **This surfaces a real, disclosed-not-fixed gap:** EAMI has exactly one role tier (`admin`/`operator`/`approver`/`viewer`, all org-scoped) and no platform-admin concept, so any org's `admin` can change pricing affecting every other org's cost reporting. Introducing a platform-admin tier is a much larger, unrequested RBAC feature — logged as **B-113** below, not fixed here. Mitigated as far as reasonably possible within scope: writes gated **admin-only** (stricter than agents/policies/tools' admin+operator gating).
**What shipped — CRUD (`eami-api`):** new `model_pricing.go` + `store/model_pricing.sql.go` (`ListModelPricing`/`CreateModelPricing`/`UpdateModelPricing`/`DeleteModelPricing`, mirroring B-098's `api_keys` structural template): `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /v1/admin/model-pricing[/{model}]`, all 8 columns (base + B-111's 3 cache-rate tiers) exposed; PATCH uses the same `COALESCE($n, column)` partial-update pattern as `UpdateAgent`; negative-rate validation; duplicate-model create → 409, not a 500.
**What shipped — B-110 fix (`finops.go`/`types.go`):** `ModelSpend` gains `PricingConfigured bool` (from a new `bool_and(mp.model IS NOT NULL)` in `modelQ`'s `GROUP BY tu.model` — constant per group since the join key IS `tu.model`); `TokenSpendSummary` gains `UnrecognizedModelRequestCount int64` (`COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE mp.model IS NULL)` in `totalQ`). Neither the base/output frozen-`cost_usd` mechanism nor B-111's cache-tier query-time computation was touched, per this brief's own `MUST NOT MODIFY`.
**What shipped — UI (`eami-ui`):** new "Model Pricing" Settings tab (`useModelPricing.ts`, `apiFetch` escape hatch — not yet in `openapi.yaml`, same B-106-class disclosed deviation), full add/edit/delete UI. `FinOpsPage.tsx` gains an amber warning banner when `unrecognized_model_request_count > 0`, linking to the new tab, and flags an unpriced model's chart legend entry (`"model (unpriced)"`, neutral grey) instead of blending it in.
**A real discrepancy caught during live verification, not glossed over:** AC2 as originally phrased ("update pricing, historical rows re-price on next query, per B-111's design") is only true for B-111's cache-tier rates and for a base-cost row that was exactly $0/unrecognized at write time (stored as SQL `NULL`, hitting the read-time fallback) — **not** for a normal already-priced row's base input/output rate, which stays frozen by B-111's own explicit design. Live-verified directly: updated a live model's `cost_per_1k_in` via the new PATCH endpoint and re-queried an already-recorded non-cache row — cost did not change, exactly as this frozen-cost_usd mechanism predicts. Per explicit user decision: disclosed as-is, B-111's mechanism left untouched (this brief's own `MUST NOT MODIFY: B-111's query-time cost computation design`); **the real behavior going forward is:** a rate change applies immediately to all *future* dispatches; only cache-tier costs and zero/unrecognized-at-write-time base costs re-price live on already-recorded rows. Whether base-rate historical re-pricing should be added as a deliberate feature is a real, open product question (retroactive accuracy vs. stable historical billing) — logged as **B-114** below, not resolved as a side effect of this brief.
**Test coverage:** `finops_pg_test.go` gains `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_UnrecognizedModel_NeverMergesIntoRecognizedModelsCost` (the AC3 centerpiece — proves no merge AND proves the new flag/count). New `model_pricing_pg_test.go`: full CRUD round trip (create→list→update→delete→404-after-delete), duplicate-model 409, negative-rate 400, admin-only-for-writes/operator-allowed-for-reads (AC4), and a new-model-then-real-ingestion-prices-correctly test (AC1). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across `eami-api`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions. Real `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vite build` both clean (Node/npm confirmed present on this machine now, contradicting `CLAUDE.md`'s stale "still not installed" toolchain note — flagged, not fixed, out of this brief's file boundary).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** `GET /v1/admin/model-pricing` returned all 9 real seeded rows correctly, cache columns present for `claude-*`/absent for `gpt-*`; created a real new model via `POST`, ingested a real row via the real `POST /v1/internal/token-usage` endpoint, `GET /v1/finops/summary` priced it exactly (AC1); the AC2 discrepancy above, caught live; a real recognized+unrecognized-model pair via real ingestion showed the recognized model's cost exactly un-contaminated (`0.0028`, matching hand-computed math), the unrecognized model separately flagged (`cost_usd:0, pricing_configured:false`), and `unrecognized_model_request_count:1` (AC3). AC4 (admin-only gating) verified via the real-Postgres HTTP-level Go test rather than a new throwaway user in the shared dev database (avoided adding more permanent state than this well-tested `requireRole` check warrants). The live `eami-ui` dev container confirmed serving the new Settings tab and FinOps banner via direct fetch of the served modules. All live-verification fixtures (a test model_pricing row, several token_usage rows across both recognized/unrecognized models) cleaned up afterward, along with unrelated orphaned residue accumulated from this session's own iterative Go test runs (same pre-existing `token_usage` has-no-FK gap B-108 already documented).
**Dependencies:** B-098 (CRUD structural template), B-111 (cache-rate columns, query-time design). **Out of scope, as specified:** budget-based enforcement, model-tier routing, workflow-level cost attribution.

### B-113 — `model_pricing` is a global table but writes are gated to any org's ordinary "admin" role — cross-tenant blast radius
**Objective:** found while building B-112 — `model_pricing` has no `org_id` (genuinely global, confirmed via schema + doc search), but EAMI has no platform-admin role tier distinct from a per-org `admin`. Any org's admin can change pricing that affects every other org's FinOps cost reporting. B-112 mitigated this as far as reasonably possible in-scope (admin-only gating, stricter than agents/policies/tools' admin+operator gating) but did not — and per its own scope, could not — close the underlying gap.
**Severity:** Medium — no single-org SaaS deployment is affected (today there is exactly one real org), but this is a real cross-tenant integrity gap the moment a second paying org exists.
**Why not fixed in B-112:** introducing a platform-admin tier distinct from org-scoped roles is a much larger, unrequested RBAC feature — a genuinely separate brief, not a side effect of adding CRUD to one table.
**Dependencies:** none yet.

### B-114 — Should `model_pricing` rate changes retroactively re-price already-recorded base-cost rows, not just cache-tier costs?
**Objective:** found during B-112's live verification — B-111's design intentionally freezes `cost_usd` for base input/output tokens at write time (only cache-tier costs are always query-time-live); a `model_pricing` rate change today only affects *future* dispatches for base costs, not history. This is a real, open product question — retroactive reporting accuracy (a rate correction fixes all historical numbers) vs. stable historical billing (a customer's past invoice shouldn't silently change) — not something to resolve as a side effect of B-112's CRUD-focused scope.
**Severity:** Low — no correctness bug; current behavior is internally consistent and matches B-111's explicit, deliberate design. This is a product-direction question, not a defect.
**Dependencies:** B-111 (the mechanism in question), B-112 (where this was caught).

### B-116 — `IssueHandler`'s signed JWT claims (`Scope`/`Task`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier`) are trusted from client input, not rebuilt from the resolved agent record — **DONE, 2026-08-27**
**Objective:** found by the mandatory security-review pass on the B-090/107 hardening batch, pre-existing since B-098 (not introduced or touched by B-107) — `IssueHandler.HandleIssue` (`eami-gateway/internal/identity/issue_http.go`) rebuilds only `req.AgentID`/`Subject` from the resolved, DB-authoritative `gateway_agents` row before calling `Manager.Issue(req)`; every other field (`Scope`, `Task`, `Model`, `Owner`, `RiskTier`) passes straight from client-supplied JSON into the signed JWT claims, even though `gateway_agents` has its own authoritative `scope`/`risk_tier` columns for the same agent.
**Severity going in:** Medium-High (re-flagged, 2026-08-27) — originally logged as Medium (latent), re-flagged as the same client-trusted-claim bug class B-098 exists to close.
**What shipped:** `apikey.go`'s `ResolvedAgent` extended with `Scope`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` (plus `TokenTTLSeconds`, see below), populated by extending `ValidateAndResolveAgent`'s existing single combined `SELECT` with 4 more `gateway_agents` columns — same query, no new round trip (B-107's constraint preserved). `issue_http.go`'s `HandleIssue` now silently overwrites `req.Scope`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` from the resolved record, same "rebuilt not validated-then-rejected" treatment `Subject`/`AgentID` already got — a deliberate choice (confirmed with the user before building): the DB wins unconditionally, no new 400/403 error surface for a forged/stale client value. `Task` stays client-supplied (no matching DB column; a per-request purpose string, not an identity attribute).
**A real finding beyond B-116's own original scope, from this brief's own mandatory review passes (both code review AND security review independently converged on it):** `IssueRequest.TTLSeconds` was the one client-controlled field actually enforced (`Manager.Validate` checks `exp`, derived from it) — unlike Scope/Model/Owner/RiskTier, which nothing downstream trusted yet. `gateway_agents.token_ttl_seconds` (admin-managed via the Agents UI, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 900`) was a real DB column eami-gateway never read at all. Before this fix, an operator setting a tight per-agent TTL as a risk control got silently overridden by any caller sending its own `ttl_seconds` (up to the global 14400s max) — the fix that would have mattered most was almost missed. Now bound the same way as the other four: `req.TTLSeconds = rec.TokenTTLSeconds`, unconditional. **Known, disclosed divergence:** `api/openapi.yaml`'s `AITokenRequest` still documents `ttl_seconds` as client-settable (60-14400s) — Architect-EAMI-owned (`BOUNDARIES.md`), disclosed as now-stale rather than edited, same B-086/B-107 precedent.
**Test coverage:** `TestIssueHandler_ForgedClaims_OverwrittenFromDB` (real Postgres) asserts all 5 fields — Scope/Model/Owner/RiskTier claims AND `exp - iat` TTL — reflect the DB record against a forged request, with `Task` proven to survive unmodified. Live-verified twice against the real rebuilt/restarted gateway container + real Postgres: once for the 4 original fields, once more after the TTL fix specifically (a real agent seeded with `token_ttl_seconds=300`, a real request forging `ttl_seconds:14400`, decoded the real returned JWT — `exp - iat = 300`, not 14400). All fixtures cleaned up, confirmed 0 remaining.
**Dependencies:** B-098 (where these fields were first introduced), B-107 (the session that found this while reviewing the surrounding handler), B-118 (built together, same brief).

### B-117 — `ReorderPolicies`'s accepted `40P01` deadlock surfaces as a raw-error-text 500 with no retry — **DONE, 2026-08-27**
**Objective:** found by the mandatory code-review pass on B-090 — B-090's own concurrency test explicitly accepts a real Postgres `40P01` deadlock as a safe, retryable outcome of two genuinely concurrent overlapping reorders, but nothing in the actual call chain (`eami-api/internal/api/policies.go`'s `ReorderPolicies` handler) retries it: every `ReorderPolicies` error becomes `writeError(w, 500, "internal_error", err.Error())`, so two admins reordering at the same moment get a real 500 carrying the raw Postgres driver error text in the response body.
**Severity going in:** Low.
**What shipped:** three new unexported additions to `policies.go`, wired only into the production (`s.queries != nil`) path: `isReorderDeadlock(err)` (detects `*pgconn.PgError{Code:"40P01"}` via `errors.As`, mirroring the existing test-only `isDeadlockError` in `policies_reorder_pg_test.go`), `reorderPoliciesExecFunc` (a package-level test seam wrapping the real `q.ReorderPolicies` call, mirroring `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `tokenUsageWriteFunc` convention in the sister module — lets a test inject a real `*pgconn.PgError{Code:"40P01"}` deterministically, avoiding reliance on genuinely concurrent goroutines probabilistically triggering a real deadlock), and `reorderPoliciesWithRetry` (bounded at `reorderPoliciesMaxAttempts = 3`, `reorderPoliciesRetryBackoff = 20ms` fixed backoff — retries ONLY on a detected `40P01`; any other error, including a genuine final-state priority collision, surfaces immediately unretried). The `storeIface`/mock test path was deliberately left un-retried (unreachable in production — `cmd/api/main.go` always constructs with non-nil `queries`).
**Explicitly NOT fixed, by design, matching the original fix sketch's own scope decision:** the raw-driver-error-text-in-response leak itself (an exhausted retry, or any other error, still returns `err.Error()` verbatim to the caller) — that's the separate, app-wide hygiene concern the original B-117 entry already scoped out of a one-endpoint fix. Only the frequency of hitting that pre-existing leak changed (now requires exhausting 3 attempts, not just 1).
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran (per explicit user correction — this brief's own STANDING RULES omitted them, a gap in how the brief was written, not a deliberate exception), both found real issues, all fixed:**
