A client-held-history design would create a real governance gap (the gateway blind to prior turns). **Real cost consequence, not an afterthought:** re-sending full history on every turn means a growing conversation gets progressively more expensive per message, not flat-cost. B-111's caching-cost-accounting work (already built and adversarially verified) becomes directly load-bearing here — real prompt caching should be a day-one design requirement for this epic, not a later optimization.
**Additional ideas captured for later refinement, not yet scoped:** transparent "here's why this was blocked" messaging to the end user (a real trust/adoption differentiator vs. competitors that fail silently); in-context policy warnings before an employee sends a likely-sensitive message; a deliberate, designed "gateway unreachable" behavior (fail closed vs. graceful degradation — a real choice, not a default); the client becomes the natural front door to all already-governed Tools/MCP connectors.
**Acceptance criteria:** none yet — this entry is a scoping placeholder, not an actionable brief. First step for whoever picks this up is splitting it into phase-sized briefs (starting with the chat-interface phase, explicitly excluding the coding-agent phase) with real ACs, per this epic's own "thin client" and "incremental, prove-it-in-stages" principles above.
**Dependencies:** none blocking investigation start. Reuses B-057/B-103's adapter pattern and the existing gateway dual-auth path (see `CONTEXT.md` Standing facts) once building starts.
**Severity/Priority:** per explicit user direction, the largest single epic identified this session — bigger than the multi-hop workflow engine or the MCP arc. Recommended as its own dedicated future session, not squeezed alongside other work.

**2026-09-02 addendum — dynamic agentic loop confirmed in-scope, RAG added in-scope, fine-tuning explicitly split out:**

**(1) Dynamic agentic loop is part of this epic's real scope, not a separate idea.** The "Model 2" pattern discussed in Thread A — an LLM autonomously deciding mid-conversation to invoke a tool, rather than the human driving each turn — was originally deferred there as 3-5x larger than what that thread actually built. It is now confirmed as a real part of B-130's scope: a chat client under this epic that only ever sends one message and waits for one reply is not the target experience: real agentic use needs the loop. Two implementation paths identified, deliberately not combined into one estimate:
  - **(a) Gateway-orchestrated loop — does NOT require B-076 resolved.** EAMI's own gateway drives the loop itself: call the LLM, inspect the response for tool-use, dispatch the requested call through the existing governed pipeline (policy/approval/audit/cost-tracking, unchanged), feed the result back to the LLM, repeat until a final answer. Entirely self-contained inside infrastructure already built or already scoped in this epic (thin-client principle, B-057/B-103 adapters). Recommended as the more tractable, self-contained path.
  - **(b) True remote-MCP integration — DOES require B-076 resolved**, and additionally depends on an external, unverified dependency: Anthropic's own infrastructure calling EAMI's MCP server directly, over a real `initialize`/`tools/call` handshake with a real external-client auth path. B-076 (QUEUED, not yet investigated) is the internal prerequisite; Anthropic's actual remote-MCP behavior is outside this repo's control and has not been verified against this codebase at all.
  - **New open design question, needs real design, not an assumed answer:** if a mid-loop tool call gets escalated for human approval, does the entire agentic loop block waiting on the human, or does something else happen (timeout-to-deny, partial-response-with-pending-marker, some other UX)? Not resolved by anything already built — `Hold()`'s existing approval-wait mechanism (B-100, B-124/125) was proven for single-call dispatch, not for a multi-turn loop sitting on top of it.

**(2) RAG (retrieval-augmented generation) added as a real, near-term-buildable capability within this epic.** Architecturally this is "one more governed tool call" — a vector-search connector dispatched through the same pipeline every other tool call already uses, no new dispatch mechanism needed. The "conversation context must live within the gateway, not the client" requirement already on record above is a natural consequence of this same architecture, not new complexity layered on top of it. **What is genuinely new, confirm real scope before assuming this is free:** an embedding/vector-store pipeline does not exist anywhere in this system today — no embedding model integration, no vector store, no ingestion/chunking pipeline. `pgvector` is already part of `eami-api`'s Postgres stack per `ARCHITECTURE.md`, which may reduce net-new infra, but that has not been verified against this specific use case (document ingestion for RAG is a different workload than whatever pgvector is currently provisioned for, if anything) — treat as unconfirmed until investigated.

**RAGFlow (2026-09-02) — candidate RAG framework, license reference (researched, not legal advice — verify with counsel before shipping commercially):** RAGFlow's default storage backend is Elasticsearch, which is triple-licensed (AGPLv3/SSPL/Elastic License 2.0) as of August 2024. Real, current research confirms embedding it unmodified as an internal backend (not reselling it as a hosted service) carries effectively zero licensing risk per informed industry sources, with the one real constraint being never modifying Elasticsearch's own source code (which would trigger AGPL's copyleft obligation). **Recommendation: don't use Elasticsearch anyway.** Two clean, zero-ambiguity Apache 2.0 alternatives exist — RAGFlow's own "Infinity" database (same maintainer, purpose-built for hybrid vector/full-text search) or OpenSearch (Linux Foundation-governed Apache 2.0 fork of Elasticsearch). For a compliance/governance-focused product, removing licensing ambiguity entirely is worth more than any marginal capability Elasticsearch might offer. If RAGFlow is adopted, configure it with `DOC_ENGINE=infinity`.

**(3) LLM training/fine-tuning is explicitly logged as its OWN SEPARATE, much-later, much-larger epic — NOT part of B-130.** Kept out of this epic's scope entirely; if picked up, needs a new B-ID of its own. Three genuinely different things "fine-tuning" could mean in practice, needing clarification before any real scoping work starts:
  - **(a) Full weight retraining** — very large, likely infeasible against frontier provider models (no customer or EAMI access to base weights), would need real research into which self-hostable open-weight models this makes sense for, if any.
  - **(b) Provider-API-supported fine-tuning where available** — smaller than (a) but still real, genuinely provider-specific, needs current research into what each provider actually offers today before sizing.
  - **(c) What "fine-tuning" usually actually means in practice for a product like this** — this IS RAG, already captured in item (2) above, or lightweight per-customer custom-instructions/system-prompt customization, which is much smaller and likely already trivially achievable within the existing gateway/adapter architecture with no new infra.

**Status:** fully discussed and scoped conceptually this session (2026-08-29); ZERO investigation or code started. Scope clarified further 2026-09-02 (see addendum above); still ZERO investigation or code started.

### B-147 — EPIC: Customer-controlled LLM training orchestration — **logged, investigation not started**
**Objective:** NOT an ML training engine built by EAMI — a state-of-the-art orchestration/management layer for training that the customer runs entirely on their own infrastructure, under their own control, matching the on-prem deployment model already used elsewhere in this product (ADR-020 Model A). EAMI's job is the guided, governed layer on top of a real training framework, not reimplementing the framework itself.
**Real, current tooling identified as the underlying framework to orchestrate, not reinvent:** Axolotl (Apache 2.0, actively maintained, YAML-config-driven, supports LoRA/QLoRA/full fine-tuning, real multi-GPU/multi-node support). Axolotl explicitly leaves GPU ops/monitoring/serving to the wrapping platform — that gap is exactly EAMI's opportunity here, not a limitation to work around.
**Real components identified:**
1. A guided UI that generates correct training configs (targeting Axolotl's YAML shape) and kicks off jobs against the chosen framework — the primary UX surface.
2. Dataset management — connects naturally to the RAG epic's (B-130 addendum, 2026-09-02) document ingestion pipeline; the two capabilities likely share infrastructure rather than duplicating it. See B-130's RAGFlow entry (2026-09-02 addendum) for the canonical RAGFlow/Elasticsearch/Infinity license research — not repeated here to avoid two copies drifting apart.
3. GPU resource visibility/allocation — genuinely new infrastructure category for this codebase, nothing precedented to extend.
4. Experiment tracking (runs, hyperparameters, metrics) — a real, honestly-scoped version of an established product category (à la MLflow/W&B), explicitly NOT full MLOps-platform depth.
5. Model registry/deployment — the key integration point: connects a newly-trained model into the SAME `ai_provider` Adapter pattern (B-057/B-103, reused again here exactly as B-130 reuses it for self-hosted models) already used elsewhere in the gateway. This is what makes a trained model immediately usable through EAMI's existing governed dispatch path rather than a standalone artifact.
6. Vendor-hosted fine-tuning-as-a-service support, where a provider offers it — smaller than the self-hosted path, fits the same adapter pattern, needs current per-vendor research (what's actually offered, and by whom) at scoping time, not assumed.
**Honest positioning, grounded in current industry consensus, to be kept honest in how this feature is actually presented to customers:** RAG is typically the faster, cheaper answer for "give the model our knowledge"; fine-tuning is the right tool for changing a model's behavior or style, not for injecting knowledge. This epic should not be marketed or scoped as a RAG replacement — the two are complementary, and B-130's RAG addendum should stay the default recommendation for knowledge-injection asks.
**Scale:** genuinely large, comparable to B-130 in scope — explicitly NOT a small add-on to either B-130 or the RAG capability. Pairs naturally with both (self-hosted model support in B-130; the dataset/document pipeline in RAG) but is its own epic, with its own investigation and its own MVP scoping.
**Recommended MVP framing, not yet a real brief:** likely single-framework (Axolotl), single-node LoRA/QLoRA first; defer distributed multi-node training complexity to a later phase — mirrors this repo's own established incremental-epic discipline (e.g. B-131's phased Workflow Canvas recommendation).
**Acceptance criteria:** none yet — this entry is a scoping placeholder, not an actionable brief, same status as B-130's own initial entry.
**Dependencies:** none blocking investigation start. Reuses the `ai_provider` Adapter pattern (B-057/B-103) at the model-registry integration point; dataset management likely shares infrastructure with the RAG capability logged under B-130's 2026-09-02 addendum, once either is actually scoped.
**Severity/Priority:** large epic, per explicit user framing comparable in scope to B-130; recommended as its own dedicated future investigation, not squeezed alongside other work.

**License reference (2026-09-02, researched — not legal advice, verify with counsel before shipping commercially):**
- **Axolotl:** Apache 2.0. Commercial/proprietary use allowed freely. Requires preserving copyright/license notices on redistribution and stating changes if modified. Includes an explicit patent grant from contributors (lower patent-litigation risk than a license without one) and a patent-retaliation clause (EAMI's license terminates if EAMI ever sues a contributor over a related patent — irrelevant otherwise).
- **Ludwig:** Apache 2.0, same terms as above.
- **Gitleaks:** the core detection engine/CLI is MIT-licensed (simpler than Apache 2.0 — use/modify/embed freely, only requirement is preserving the copyright notice). **Important distinction:** `gitleaks-action` (the GitHub Actions CI wrapper, a separate product) requires a paid license for organizational use — this does NOT apply if only the core detection engine is embedded as a dataset-scanning step, not the Action itself. **Also note:** Gitleaks is now feature-complete/maintenance-only; active development has moved to a successor called "Betterleaks" — its license has not been checked and needs separate verification before serious consideration.
- **Practical obligation if any of these ship in the product:** a real `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES` file (or equivalent) listing each dependency's license text — a routine, low-cost compliance task, same as already handled for `sequential-workflow-designer`'s MIT license (B-131).

**Status:** discussed this session (2026-09-02); ZERO investigation or code started. License reference added 2026-09-02 (see above); still ZERO investigation or code started.

### B-131 — INVESTIGATION: `sequential-workflow-designer` as a replacement for the Workflow card editor's presentation layer — **investigation complete, no build brief started**
**Objective:** evaluate `sequential-workflow-designer` (nocode-js/b4rtaz, MIT, zero-third-party-dependency, official React wrapper) as a purpose-built presentation-layer replacement for `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s card editor (B-065). **Explicitly excluded from evaluation, per the investigation's own scoping constraint:** the library's companion execution engine `sequential-workflow-machine` (xstate-based) — EAMI's own dispatcher/B-102 hook mechanism/B-057/059 TOCTOU pinning/B-124/125 resolution audit trail is real, adversarially tested, and was never in scope to replace. Only the DEFINITION-side designer component was evaluated.
**Licensing/feature-tier boundary, confirmed against the library's real docs (not assumed):** the free MIT core (`sequential-workflow-designer` + `sequential-workflow-designer-react`) includes the `Task`/`Container`/`Switch`/`Launch Pad` step components, Validation, Undo/Redo, read-only mode (`isReadonly`), Toolbox drag-add, and per-step `Editors` — all confirmed free on each feature's own doc page, no Pro badge. **Custom step *types* are confirmed free-tier**: the Task component's own free doc page uses a custom `type`/`properties` bag (`componentType:'task', type:'saveFile', properties:{...}`) as its own worked example — exactly the shape EAMI's v1 linear connector-step model needs. Pro (`sequential-workflow-designer-pro`, $1199/2-devs/1-app perpetual + $75/mo renewal) gates `Folder`/`Icon`/`Interrupting Icon`/`Interrupting Task` step components (each has an explicit "Pro:" badge) plus a cosmetic/extension layer (Custom Step Component Wrapper, Custom Theme/Viewport, External Toolbox/Editor/Control Bar, etc) — none of which EAMI's v1 scope needs.
**Flagged, unresolved discrepancy — do not treat either source as authoritative without a real trial:** the Pro pricing page's own bullet list names "Large Task Step Component" as Pro-exclusive, but the Large Task component's own doc page carries no Pro badge (only its "Customization" subsection is Pro-gated, same pattern as Task/Container/Switch). EAMI doesn't need Large Task, so this doesn't block anything today, but it's concrete evidence the library's own docs site contradicts itself in at least one place — nothing here should become load-bearing without a real `npm install` + import trial in Brief 1.
**Data model fit:** `Definition{properties, sequence:Step[]}` / `Step{id,componentType,type,name,properties}` maps cleanly onto `workflow_steps` (array position ↔ `step_order`, `Step.id`↔`workflow_steps.id`, `Step.name`/`type`↔connector/action) — no schema restructuring needed. The one real seam: EAMI splits a step's params across two backend concepts (`workflow_step_params.params` static, own table/endpoint since B-059; `workflow_steps.input_mapping` extraction refs, only ever written inside the full `UpdateWorkflow` PATCH since B-063) where the library expects one opaque `properties` bag per step — but B-064 already solved the equivalent unification problem once, in local editor state (`ParamRow[]` with a static/extract mode), so this is a precedented adaptation, not new risk. Zero conflict with B-063/064's extraction mechanism confirmed — the library is definition/UI-only and has no opinion on how `properties` gets populated; today's `StepConfigPanel` param-editing UI would become the library's `stepEditor` prop content, the same architectural slot it already occupies.
**Branching (`SwitchStep`/`branches`), confirmed informational-only:** the Switch component's `branches:{'<label>':Step[]}` shape lets an admin describe an if/else purely in the UI/JSON definition — it does not evaluate conditions, execute branches, or require connector-output typing. **Confirmed this does NOT touch or shrink EAMI's actual backend gap**: `workflow_steps`' `UNIQUE(workflow_id, step_order)` flat-integer schema (migration 000006's own comment: "no branching/conditional columns exist or are planned... architecturally unbuildable until connector output normalization exists") cannot represent a `branches` map today. v1 scope should use only the `Task`/linear-`sequence` half of the model; `Switch` stays informational, matching this investigation's own framing.
**Integration risk vs. the Workflow Canvas epic's lesson (B-066-069, discontinued after real hands-on browser testing found bugs no code-inspection-only verification caught):** the two confirmed canvas bugs (`onNodesDelete`/`onEdgesDelete` never wired to `<ReactFlow>`, an unexplained dropdown-interaction failure specific to canvas context) were both born from React Flow being a free-form draggable node/edge graph. `sequential-workflow-designer`'s data model has **no node positions and no manual edge-drawing** — a `sequence` array structurally cannot represent a disconnected node, a cycle, or a multi-in/multi-out node, so the entire two-layer draw-time+save-time graph-validity guard B-067/B-068 had to hand-roll (`isValidConnection`, `getOutgoers`, `validateGraph`) may collapse into near-nothing for this library, replaced by its own free per-step `Validation` feature. This is a real, evidence-grounded architectural risk reduction, not the same claim as "zero-dependency" (which is real but minor — npm registry metadata confirms exactly one runtime dependency, `sequential-workflow-model`, nocode-js's own sibling MIT package, so "0 dependencies" is more precisely "0 *third-party* dependencies"). **What is explicitly NOT verified:** whether this library's own interaction wiring (toolbox drag-drop, its own keyboard shortcuts, `stepEditor` mounting inside its DOM/SVG tree) has undiscovered bugs analogous to the canvas's dropdown failure — nothing here was hands-on browser-tested, same standing no-browser-automation limitation as every prior `eami-ui` brief.
**Tailwind/Vite/SSR compatibility — flagged as pending, not verified:** the library's own React-integration doc recommends a Next.js dynamic-import-with-SSR-disabled pattern for framework compatibility; `eami-ui` is a Vite SPA with no SSR, so this specific caveat is likely inapplicable, but — mirroring B-066's own corrected premise about Tailwind's JIT pipeline before building on it — this needs a real check against actual `tailwind.config.ts`/`vite.config.ts` in Brief 1, not an assumption carried over from this investigation.
**Recommended incremental sequence, explicitly modeled on B-066→067→068's own discipline (never one large brief, per the Canvas epic's own expensive lesson):**
- **Brief 1** — install `sequential-workflow-designer`+`-react`, read-only (`isReadonly`) render of an existing workflow's `sequence` at a new route, zero interactivity, zero backend writes, real measured bundle-size delta (mirroring B-066's real before/after `npm run build` numbers, not the npm registry's unpacked-size figure) — same shape and rigor as B-066.
- **Brief 2** — interactivity: toolbox add/remove/reorder (local-only), step click opens a `stepEditor` built from **`StepConfigPanel`'s existing logic reused**, static-param-saves-immediately-on-close persistence split (the same backend-contract fact B-067 already established, independent of library choice).
- **Brief 3** — save-time structural PATCH to the unmodified `UpdateWorkflow` endpoint, wiring the library's own free per-step `Validation` feature in place of a hand-rolled graph-topology validator — **likely smaller than B-068's `validateGraph`**, since the tree/sequence model can't represent the invalid topologies that validator existed to catch.
**Acceptance criteria:** none — this entry is the investigation record only. First step for whoever picks this up is Brief 1 above, with its own real ACs.

Brief 2 shipped as **B-148** (DONE, live-verified) — a general UX-refinement follow-up for the canvas interactions it shipped is parked as **B-149**. Brief 3 (save-time structural PATCH) not yet scoped. **This investigation's own "branching is informational-only, `SwitchStep` doesn't touch the real backend gap" finding is the direct starting point for B-155** (EPIC, conditional workflow branching, logged 2026-09-02) — that epic explicitly re-examines whether the underlying "no typed connector output schemas" blocker (originally from B-058, restated here) still holds now that B-063/064's extraction mechanism exists.

### B-145 — Workflow Canvas rebuild, Brief 1: `sequential-workflow-designer` read-only rendering — **DONE, 2026-09-01**
**Objective:** B-131's own recommended Brief 1 — install `sequential-workflow-designer`/`-react`, render a real existing workflow's steps read-only at a new route, zero interactivity, zero backend writes, before any interactivity is built. Distinct epic from the discontinued `@xyflow/react` Workflow Canvas (B-066-069, see that epic's header above) — a different library, chosen specifically because its `sequence`-array data model structurally cannot represent the free-form node/edge graph shapes that produced B-066-069's real, hands-on-testing-only-discovered bugs (no node positions, no manual edge-drawing).
**`sequential-workflow-machine` (the companion execution engine) explicitly NOT adopted, in any form** — EAMI's own dispatcher/policy/audit/approval pipeline remains the sole executor, exactly as B-131's investigation required.
**Free-tier custom-step-type question, resolved:** B-131's investigation flagged a real discrepancy — the library's own GitHub README lists "🌹 Custom Step Types" under its **Pro** examples section, while its Task-component doc page's own worked example uses a custom `type`/`properties` bag with no Pro badge. Re-checked live against the current pricing page before building (not carried over from the investigation): the Pro feature list is exclusively named Step *Components* (Icon/Interrupting Icon/Interrupting Task/Folder/Large Task) plus UI/theming extras — a `Task` step with a custom `type` string is not on it. **Built and confirmed empirically, not just re-read from docs**, per the brief's own explicit instruction to trust only the real build: a real custom step type (`eami-connector-action`, carrying EAMI's actual `gateway_tool_id`/`toolName`/`action`/`hasInputMapping` fields) renders correctly, including a real custom per-type icon via the free `steps.iconUrlProvider` config, using only the packages' free MIT core — no Pro package installed, no paywall hit. Resolved in favor of free-tier.
**Tailwind/Vite compatibility, confirmed empirically, not assumed:** the library ships its own pre-built CSS (`designer.css`/`designer-light.css`), imported directly and requested successfully through the real Vite dev server (`200`, valid HMR-wrapped CSS module, zero resolution errors in `docker logs eami-ui`) — no SSR dependency to worry about (irrelevant to this Vite SPA) and no Tailwind JIT conflict, since Tailwind's `content` glob only scans `.tsx/.ts/.js/.jsx` under `src/`, never `node_modules` CSS, and the library's own DOM carries none of Tailwind's utility classes.
**Data adapter (`workflowCanvasAdapter.ts`):** converts a real `Workflow`/`WorkflowStep[]` (unmodified `useWorkflow(id)`) into the library's `Definition{properties, sequence: Step[]}` — one `componentType:'task'` Step per `WorkflowStep`, deliberately linear-only (no `Container`/`Switch`/branches): `workflow_steps`' flat `step_order` schema still cannot represent branches (per B-131's own confirmed backend gap), so no UI implying that capability was built.
**Every interactive surface explicitly disabled, not just `isReadonly`:** `toolboxConfiguration={false}`, `controlBar={false}`, `contextMenu={false}`, `keyboard={false}`, `rootEditor={false}`, `stepEditor={false}`, in addition to `isReadonly`. Zero mutation hooks (`useCreateWorkflow`/`useUpdateWorkflow`/`useDeleteWorkflow`/`useSetWorkflowStepParams`) imported or called anywhere in the two new files — confirmed by direct grep, not just by design intent — so the new view cannot originate a write under any interaction path, and its only backend call at all is the single, necessary `GET /v1/gateway/workflows/:id` read the page exists to display.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx` (the card editor):** zero edits during the initial build — the new route (`/gateway/workflows/:id/canvas-preview`, `router.tsx` only) was deliberately not linked from anywhere in the existing UI, reachable only by direct URL, since the file was in this brief's original MUST-NOT-MODIFY scope. **A single, small, explicitly-requested follow-up** (same day, after real visual verification below) added one permanent entry point: a new `Eye`-icon "Preview (read-only)" button in the existing row-actions column, next to the pre-existing Edit/Delete icons, `navigate()`-ing to that row's canvas-preview route. Purely additive (`e.stopPropagation()` on the same pattern the existing buttons already use) — zero changes to any add/edit/delete/reorder/save logic, confirmed via a real full-file diff review (11 insertions, 1 line changed, one file) and a real `tsc`/`vite build` pass.
**Real bundle-size delta, measured, not the npm registry's unpacked-size figure** (mirroring B-066's own precedent): a real before/after `vite build` — `sequential-workflow-designer`+`-react`+their one real dependency `sequential-workflow-model` (nocode-js's own MIT sibling package, confirmed via `npm ls`, matching B-131's "0 third-party dependencies" finding) added **+29.3 kB gzipped JS, +1.3 kB gzipped CSS** (971.30→1,076.64 kB raw JS / 265.01→294.33 kB gzip; 29.28→36.66 kB raw CSS / 5.58→6.92 kB gzip).
**A real, visible bug found and fixed by actual human visual verification — the exact reason this brief was built read-only-first, and something no code-level review caught:** the initial "verified" build (module transforms cleanly, real data confirmed, `tsc`/`vite build` clean — all true) still rendered a **completely blank canvas** when the user actually looked at it in a browser. Root cause traced through the library's own shipped source, not guessed: `sequential-workflow-designer`'s core JS measures its canvas from real layout pixels (`this.canvas.clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, `this.workspace.offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`), its own CSS is `.sqd-designer { height: 100% }`, and the div its React wrapper mounts into (`sqd-designer-react`) carries **zero CSS of its own** (confirmed via direct grep of `designer.css`, 0 matches) — it depends entirely on its container having a genuinely definite, resolved size. This page's `flex h-full flex-col` wrapper relied on a percentage-height cascade through `AppShell`'s shared `main` element with no `min-height:0` anywhere in the chain — the first page in the app that ever needed to fill remaining viewport height, so no existing precedent had exercised this. **Fixed in two real, separately-confirmed passes, not one guess:** pass 1 (`h-[75vh]`, a viewport-relative unit immune to the ancestor chain) fixed the *height* half, confirmed via the user's own DevTools measurement (`sqd-designer-react` went from `0 height` to a real `550.5px`) — but **width was still exactly `0`**, propagated all the way to the real `<svg class="sqd-workspace-canvas" width="0">`, a genuine mount with genuinely zero width, not a mount failure (confirmed via the user's own DevTools inspection: real DOM children existed, `childElementCount: 1`, real `sqd-designer > sqd-workspace > svg > defs/rect/g` tree). Root cause of the width half: `flex` (row) only auto-stretches the **cross axis** (height, via default `align-items:stretch`) — the **main axis** (width) defaults to content-based sizing (`flex-grow:0`), and an empty, unstyled child has no content to size against, so it measured `0`. Pass 2 fixed it: swapped `flex` for `grid`, whose default `place-items:stretch` stretches **both** axes for an unstyled single-cell child, with zero CSS needed on the child. **Live-verified for real by the user** after pass 2: real flowchart with start/stop markers and connector lines, both step boxes visible with distinct `data-step-id` values.
**A real false alarm, correctly ruled out through evidence, not assumption — kept clearly separate from the actual fix above so it doesn't get conflated in this record:** the user's browser also logged an uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'startTime')` at `et.reportAllChanges`, all stack frames `<anonymous>`. Traced before touching any code: `reportAllChanges` is a real, named public option in Google's `web-vitals` library (whose internals read `entries[...].startTime` off `PerformanceObserver` entries — matching this error's exact shape) — but `web-vitals` is **not a dependency of this project at all**, direct or transitive (confirmed via `grep` across `package-lock.json`, zero matches), and `reportAllChanges` as a literal string appears **nowhere** in any installed package (grepped all of `node_modules`, zero matches). The only files anywhere in the project matching the `startTime`+`PerformanceObserver` pattern together are inside `rollup`'s own **Node.js-side, build-time** profiling code, which never ships to the browser. Confirmed to persist in Incognito too, so not this app's own extension-adjacent code — most consistent with an unrelated browser-extension-injected script (a common false-positive class in DevTools consoles), not a real bug in this brief's own code. **Correctly not fixed, because there was nothing in this codebase to fix.**
**Differential data-correctness test, not just a repeat of the same case:** the initial visual check used `b063-live-verify`, whose two real steps happen to share the identical connector+action in the database (confirmed via direct SQL, unchanged throughout) — a weak test of "does each step render its own label." Re-tested against `b059-live-verify` (`b046-live-verify`/`echo_get` step 1, `claude`/`messages` step 2 — genuinely different steps, confirmed via direct SQL against `workflow_steps`/`gateway_tools`) and **confirmed correct**: `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` had no bug — an earlier report of "both steps show the same label" traced to the user comparing two *different* workflows (the card editor's `b059` against the canvas-preview's `b063`), not a real adapter defect. This is real confirmation the adapter's per-step field mapping is correct, not assumed from the one coincidentally-identical test case.
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] A real existing workflow renders correctly via the new library, read-only, at the new route — **now genuinely browser-confirmed** (not just data/serve-path confirmed) after the CSS fix above, including a differential two-distinct-step test
- [x] Custom step type with real connector/action data confirmed working on the free tier — Pro-badge ambiguity resolved (free tier)
- [x] Zero conflict between the library's CSS and this project's real Tailwind/Vite pipeline, confirmed via the real dev server
- [x] The existing card-based editor completely unaffected — verified live (module re-served with zero errors) both before and after the small permanent nav-link follow-up; every add/edit/delete/reorder/save code path untouched
- [x] Zero backend calls beyond the one necessary read — confirmed by direct grep for every write hook
**Dependencies:** B-131 (investigation). **Status:** DONE, 2026-09-01 — genuinely browser-verified, not just build-verified. Brief 2 (interactivity, B-148) and Brief 3 (save-time validation) remain not yet scoped, per B-131's own recommended sequencing — no cutover decision from cards to this library has been made or is implied by this brief.

### B-148 — Workflow Canvas rebuild, Brief 2: local interactivity — **DONE, live-verified, closed 2026-09-02**
**Objective:** B-131's own recommended Brief 2 — add/remove/reorder steps on the canvas and click-to-configure via `StepConfigPanel`, entirely local, zero backend writes, mirroring the original discontinued canvas epic's own Brief 2 (B-067) boundary exactly.
**Two small, explicitly-approved exceptions to `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s untouched status, confirmed before building, same category as B-145's own Eye-icon addition:** `StepConfigPanel`/`StepRow`/`ParamRow`/`revalidateExtractionRefs`/`newStepRow`/`newParamRow` gained `export` (zero logic change — confirmed via `git diff`: exactly the 6 keywords + one doc comment) so this brief could reuse `StepConfigPanel` unmodified rather than forking it; the existing Eye-icon's tooltip updated from `"Preview (read-only)"` to `"Open canvas"`. Route path deliberately left unchanged (`/gateway/workflows/:id/canvas-preview`) for bookmark/share stability (B-146's own lesson) — only the file/component renamed to `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx`.
**Library-native interactions used directly (toolbox drag-add, context-menu/keyboard delete, drag-reorder, `selectedStepId`/`onSelectedStepIdChanged` for click-to-configure), confirmed against the library's real `.d.ts` files before writing code, not assumed** — per this brief's own investigation ask and B-145's own lesson about unverified library behavior. `stepEditor` stays `false`; `StepConfigPanel` renders as this page's own overlay, keeping it byte-for-byte the same component/props contract the card editor uses.
**Mandatory code-review pass (forked subagent) found 2 real state-management bugs, both fixed before shipping:** (1) step canvas labels could bake in a stale/empty tools list if a mutation happened before `useTools()` first resolved, unlike the card editor's fresh-every-render resolution — fixed by gating the one-time definition seed on tools readiness too, closing the race at its source (plus a related blank-loading-state gap found and fixed in the same pass); (2) `realStepIds` was recomputed on every `workflow` query reference change instead of captured once at load — a real background-refetch scenario (network blip + a concurrent card-editor step removal) could have silently reclassified an in-use canvas step as "not real," invalidating extraction refs with no user action — fixed by moving it into the same one-time seed as `definition`. **Mandatory security review (forked subagent): zero HIGH/MEDIUM findings** — pure client-side state management, no new write path, no injection/auth/crypto surface.
**Full detail, including the exact `commit()`/adapter design and every review finding's precise failure scenario, in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-ui` section.**
**Acceptance criteria:**
- [x] AC1-3 (add/remove/reorder are local-only, zero network calls) — verified by grep (same method B-067 used) for every mutation hook/write-method `apiFetch` call across the two changed/new files: zero matches outside a doc comment naming them.
- [x] AC4 (clicking a step opens `StepConfigPanel` with that step's real data) — built via `selectedStepId`/real `rowFromStep` conversion seeded from the real workflow + real B-059 static-params read; not yet confirmed by an actual click (see AC6).
- [x] AC5 (card editor unaffected) — `git diff` on `WorkflowsPage.tsx` is exactly the 6 exports + 1 tooltip string; real `tsc`/`vite build` type-checks the whole app together, and the served module was confirmed over HTTP to match source.
- [x] **AC6 (a real, live click-through by the user) — DONE, and it's what actually closed this brief out.** `claude-in-chrome` was unavailable this session, so this was always going to depend on the user's own hands — and it caught a real bug no code-level check had: continuous canvas flicker and toolbox drag-and-drop not attaching a new step. **Diagnosed precisely (not guessed) by reading the actual library source:** `sequential-workflow-designer-react` destroys/rebuilds its internal `Designer` based on **reference identity**, and echoes its own current definition back once after *every* `Designer.create()` via `onReady` (not just real edits). `commit()` unconditionally built a brand-new object graph on every call, so that echo always looked like a fresh edit — `setDefinition` → rebuild → `onReady` fires again → loop, present from mount, independent of user action. **Fixed** (commit `767811d`): `commit()` now does a real content comparison (`JSON.stringify`) before calling `setDefinition`, skipping it when nothing actually changed — breaking the loop at its source, not patching a symptom. Verified via a targeted Node test bundling the real, unmodified `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` and replicating `commit()`'s literal guard expression (7/7 checks) — full detail in `BUILT.md`. **Then re-confirmed live by the user, for real, after the fix:** flickering at rest is gone, and toolbox-add now correctly attaches a new step to the sequence — this is what makes AC6 genuinely DONE, not just code-verified. **Reorder specifically:** not separately click-confirmed by the user in this exact pass, but goes through the byte-identical `onDefinitionChange` → `commit()` path as toolbox-add (confirmed via the core library's own `DragStepBehavior.onEnd()` source — both `tryMove` and `tryInsert` fire the same way, atomically on release), so the same fix applies; flagged for a real click-confirmation whenever it's next exercised, not assumed with the same certainty as the two directly-tested paths. **The original "toolbox-drop-during-DOM-swap" theory** (flagged when first diagnosed as a plausible-but-not-independently-observed mechanism) **is now strongly corroborated, not just still-plausible:** predicting that fixing the confirmed loop would resolve the drop, and then observing live that it did, is real supporting evidence for that causal chain — still not a frame-by-frame trace of the exact interruption moment, but no longer merely inferred either.
**Dependencies:** B-131 (investigation), B-145 (Brief 1, DONE). **Status:** DONE and live-verified, 2026-09-02 — core interactivity (add/remove/reorder/click-to-configure, zero backend writes) confirmed working, including the real object-identity loop bug found by the user's own live testing and fixed as part of this brief's real story (commit `767811d`), not a footnote. Follow-up **B-149** logged (general UX refinement of canvas interactions, not urgent, not yet precisely scoped). Brief 3 (save-time persistence) not yet scoped; no cutover decision from cards to canvas made or implied.

### B-150 — EPIC: Guardrails — content safety, PII detection, prompt-injection/jailbreak detection on dispatch
**Objective:** real-time content-safety screening of AI dispatch traffic — content safety classification, PII detection, and prompt-injection/jailbreak detection — evaluated at dispatch time, before/alongside policy enforcement. **Explicitly distinct from redaction, not a duplicate of it:** redaction protects **outbound data** (what leaves EAMI's boundary toward a provider); guardrails protect **content safety** (what kind of content is being sent or would be generated) — two different concerns that happen to both touch the same dispatch path. No dedicated "redaction epic" currently has its own logged B-ID in this backlog as of this session (checked, not assumed) — the concept appears only as an out-of-scope note in earlier entries (e.g. B-057, B-078); if/when redaction is scoped as its own epic, this distinction should be restated there too, not left implicit.
**Architectural fit, per the one-spine rule (`CLAUDE.md` Conventions):** this is a new **cross-cutting reaction to an existing dispatch call**, not a new tool/identity type — fits B-102's dispatch-hook mechanism directly (a new hook alongside the existing audit-write-failure hook, `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go`), not a new subsystem, not a new identity model, not a new top-level UI section disconnected from the existing six core pages. A guardrail violation is a new kind of policy-relevant decision outcome, naturally surfaced through the existing Policies/Audit pages.
**Acceptance criteria:** none yet — this entry is a scoping placeholder. First step for whoever picks this up is real investigation into which detection mechanism(s) to build/integrate (classifier model choice, PII-detection approach, prompt-injection detection technique) before any brief is written — per this repo's own standing "investigate before scoping" convention.
**Dependencies:** B-102 (dispatch-hook mechanism, reused). **Out of scope, as specified:** redaction/outbound-data-protection (a distinct concern, see above).
**Severity/Priority:** EPIC, part of the TrueFoundry feature-parity initiative (see shared context above). Not yet sized.
**Status:** logged 2026-09-02, zero investigation or code started.
